# The Runtime

The **Runtime** runs a **Page**'s scripts in a process of its own, on the **Engine Release**'s V8, while the Page's **Session** stays in the caller's process with its network, cookies and cache ([ADR-0009](adr/0009-a-process-for-each-page.md)). Terms in **bold** are defined in [CONTEXT.md](../CONTEXT.md). [building.md](building.md#build-the-runtime) says how the Page program is built. Until the release that ships the Runtime, the Page API is private, and a wheel that a release makes holds no Page program. [From Python](#from-python) says how the private API works.

## A Page and its process

Each Page is a process of the Page program, `mitto_page`, or `mitto_page.exe` on Windows. `mitto_page_open()` in the C API starts it from the directory that the library was loaded from, which is where a wheel puts both. Without a program there, as in a wheel that a release made, it fails with `ERR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`.

The library makes the channel first, then starts the program with its end of the channel and one argument, `--session=CHANNEL,PARENT`. `CHANNEL` names that end and `PARENT` is the ID of the process that holds the library. The program's standard input is the null device, and it shares standard output and error with the process that holds the library, so that what V8 or a sanitizer reports on a crash is not lost. It inherits nothing else:

| Host | The channel | What else the Page process inherits | The Page ends when the process that holds the library ends |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| macOS | One end of a socket pair, at descriptor 3 | Nothing: `posix_spawn()` closes every descriptor that it is not told to pass on | Its end of the channel reads the end of the stream. It also watches its parent with a `kqueue`, for when another process holds the library's end, such as one that the parent forked |
| Linux | One end of a socket pair, at descriptor 3 | Nothing: `//base` closes every other descriptor before it starts the program, and sets `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS` | The end of the stream, and the kernel's `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`, which kills it when the thread that started it ends. So the library starts each Page from a thread that never ends |
| Windows | The client end of a named pipe, whose handle value the argument holds | Nothing: only that handle is in `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` | The pipe breaks. It also runs in a job object of its own, with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, which ends it when the last handle to the job closes, as when the process that holds the library ends |

On Windows the job also has `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION`, so that a crash ends the process at once rather than waiting for Windows Error Reporting, and the program starts hidden, so that a caller with no console gets no window. The named pipe has a random name under `\\.\pipe\LOCAL\`, one instance, and remote clients refused. Its end in the library is not inherited by any program, and the library opens the client end itself before it starts the Page, as Mojo's `PlatformChannel` does.

On Linux, a Page process holds about 18 MB of memory before a page loads. Besides its main thread and its I/O thread it runs V8's worker threads, one fewer than the machine has cores.

## What a Page process holds and reads

A Page process holds its end of the channel and its standard streams, and opens no file or socket of its own, so that Chromium's sandbox can hold it before the Runtime ships (roadmap, M10):

- ICU's whole data file and V8's startup snapshot are inside the program ([building.md](building.md#icus-data-and-v8s-snapshot)).
- What it would read of the machine and of its browser comes from the Session, in the start message below: everything of the Session's [Machine](persona.md#the-runtime) that a page reads, such as its time zone, processors, memory, display and its refresh rate, work area, window and network, and what the browser tells a renderer of itself, the user agent and the values of the user agent client hints of its Persona, and the languages of its `Accept-Language`. So ICU never reads `TZ`, `/etc/localtime` or the zone files. The `start` option of `mitto_page_open()` gives them, which Python fills from the Session's Machine, Persona and settings, and the library adds what `navigator.connection` gives. The Page sets the time zone and the locale as Blink sets the time zone that its browser reads, and its `navigator`, `screen` and window give the rest ([The window](#the-window)). The start also gives the runtime-enabled features and V8 flags of the Persona's Chrome Release ([persona.md](persona.md#what-a-page-of-each-persona-turns-on-and-off)), which a renderer of Chrome gets on its command line.
- V8's random numbers come from the system's random source through `//base`, which reads no file, as in Chrome's renderers.

What it holds is checked on Linux, where a test reads `/proc/<pid>/fd` of a Page that has run a script. It finds standard input on `/dev/null`, standard output and error as the test program's, the socket at descriptor 3, and the epoll and eventfd of the program's I/O thread, which name no file.

Before its `main()`, the dynamic loader of Linux opens the shared libraries of glibc, as it does for any program. After it, `strace` shows the Page reading `/sys/devices/system/cpu/online`, for how many threads V8 starts, and `/proc/self/maps`, which glibc reads to find the main thread's stack, and opening nothing else. Chrome's renderers read the same, and the sandbox of M10 has to allow them, as Chrome's does.

## Inside the Page program

The program runs V8 on its main thread, and reads the channel on a thread of its own. A Session's Page starts V8 once its start message has come, since V8 takes its flags before it starts and each isolate's runtime-enabled features are fixed when it is made: it sets the flags of every Page of the Engine Release, as Chrome's renderer sets them ([building.md](building.md#v8s-flags)), then the Persona's V8 flags from the start, starts V8 and makes the isolate with the Persona's features and the heap's limits that its Machine's memory gives. Given a script, the program starts V8 before it runs it, with the Engine Release's flags and the limits of 8 GiB, the memory of the most common Machine. The I/O thread checks each message, hands scripts to the main thread in the order they came, and sends their results back. Because it reads while a script runs, the process ends as soon as the channel closes, even in the middle of a script that never ends.

As each of Chrome's processes does, the program sets `//base`'s features when it starts, each to its default, since a Page gets none of the browser's. So `base::RandBytes()`, which `crypto.getRandomValues()` fills a view with, takes BoringSSL's random bytes, as Chrome's renderer does.

Each script runs in the context of the window of the Page's top-level frame, so each sees what the ones before it left there, until a navigation gives the frame a new one. Each frame of a Page holds its window's context and what the window runs in it, its timers, idle callbacks, animation frames, fetches, tasks and workers, as Blink's `LocalFrame` and `LocalWindowProxy` hold them ([`page_frame.h`](../native/page/page_frame.h)), and each task runs in the context it belongs to: a window's tasks in its own, and what the Session sends of a request in the context that made it. Its result is what `JSON.stringify()` writes for it, nothing for a value that it writes nothing for, such as `undefined` or a function, and what was thrown, as `Uncaught TypeError: ...`, for a script that throws or a result that cannot be written as JSON, such as a BigInt. When V8 runs out of memory, the program ends with exit code 3, which the library reports as running out of memory.

When the result is a promise, the result of the call is what the promise settles with, as when DevTools evaluates a script and waits for its promise: what it was fulfilled with, or `Uncaught (in promise) ...` and why it was rejected. The microtasks that the script queued have run by then, so a promise that they settle has settled. One that is still pending gets a handler, the call stays in progress, and the scripts after it run meanwhile. So a later script, a timer or a response can settle it, and results come in any order. Only a promise is waited for, and not another object with a `then` method.

The console is V8's own, as in Chrome, where V8 installs `console` and Blink hears of each call through V8's inspector. So the program starts an inspector, as Blink does for each isolate, and the inspector hands it each message, with its arguments written as V8 writes them for Chrome's log, separated by spaces, and the level of the method that wrote it. The inspector also gives `console` what it gives in Chrome, `console.createTask()` and, for a window's context, `console.memory` ([below](#what-chrome-adds-beyond-the-idl-files)). An error that nothing caught, in a script of the document, a timer, a microtask, an event listener or a mutation observer, or that `reportError()` reports, fires `error` at the window first, as [Errors at the window](#errors-at-the-window) says, and then goes to the Session as a console says it, `Uncaught TypeError: ...`, unless a listener canceled it. The program hears of each through V8's message listener, as Blink does, or reports it itself where it calls a page's function, with V8's message of it, which says where it was thrown. A promise rejected with no handler is reported as Blink reports it: the promises that a task leaves rejected are reported from a task of their own, which comes after the tasks queued before it, unless a handler has come by then, also the one that a call adds to the promise it waits for. That task fires `unhandledrejection` at the window, and the Session gets `Uncaught (in promise) ...` unless a listener canceled it. A script's console messages and the errors that it leaves uncaught reach the Session before its result, because results wait for those reports.

### The event loop

The main thread is the Page's event loop, a `base::sequence_manager::SequenceManager` on the real clock, with no virtual time, and with Blink's eleven task priorities ([`page_scheduler.h`](../native/page/page_scheduler.h)). Every task is one of its tasks: a script or a cookie answer from the Session, each step of a document's loading, each part of a response, each timer, and V8's own tasks for the main thread, such as its garbage collector's, which the program's `v8::Platform` posts to a queue of V8's, as gin's does for Chrome's renderers ([`page_platform.h`](../native/page/page_platform.h)). A task of the DOM has one of Blink's task types ([`event_loop.h`](../native/dom/event_loop.h)) and goes to the queue that Blink's frame scheduler makes for that type, at the priority that it gives it on a page that is visible: Blink's continuation of script loading at a very high priority, networking control at a high one, low-priority script execution at a low one, and every other task source of the HTML Standard, such as networking, DOM manipulation, timers, idle callbacks that time out, the Cookie Store's answers and Web Locks, at the normal priority. The scripts that the Session evaluates go to the inspector's queue, as DevTools' do, each part of a response to the loading queue, what belongs to no frame, such as the start and a document's loading, to a default queue, and the compositor has a queue at the normal priority where [a frame's](#animation-frames) rendering steps begin, which goes very high while rendering starves. Beside them, `scheduler.postTask()` and `scheduler.yield()` have a queue for each of their priorities, idle callbacks one that runs only when nothing else is ready, and the idle periods a control queue before all the others, ordered as Blink orders them: a task of a higher priority runs first, and tasks of one priority run in the order they were ready, whichever queue holds them. A timer of no delay is an immediate task, as Blink's `TimerBase` posts it, so it keeps its place among the tasks posted beside it. A parser that goes on once the script that it waited for is ready, and the next deferred script, run at the very high priority, as [Loading a document](#loading-a-document) says, and an async script of another site while a page loads at the low one.

Microtasks run as Blink runs them, with V8's `kScoped` policy: once the outermost script that the Runtime runs returns, which is the HTML Standard's clean up after running a script, and in a microtask checkpoint after each task, as Blink's scheduler runs one after each task. `queueMicrotask()` queues a function on V8's microtask queue, with promise jobs. A task that settles promises from C++, such as the end of a response, runs their reactions after it, not between them.

### Timers

`setTimeout()`, `setInterval()`, `clearTimeout()` and `clearInterval()` follow the HTML Standard's timer initialisation steps as Chrome runs them, in [`native/dom/dom_timer.cc`](../native/dom/dom_timer.cc), a port of Blink's `DOMTimer`:

- A timeout less than 0 is 0, and one that does not fit a `long` wraps, as Web IDL converts it.
- A timer set from a timer whose nesting level is 5 or more waits at least 4 milliseconds, so the seventh of a chain of `setTimeout(f, 0)` is the first that does. An interval grows to 4 milliseconds once it has repeated that often. Chrome counts the nesting level up before it compares it, which comes to the same.
- There is no clamp to 1 millisecond: `setTimeout(f, 1)` runs after a `setTimeout(g, 0)` set after it, as in Chrome, and `setInterval()` has none either, since `SetIntervalWithoutClamp` is on for stable.
- A timer shorter than 32 milliseconds runs on time, and a longer one when the thread wakes up anyway soon after, as Chrome posts them.
- A string runs as a classic script. An empty string sets no timer and gives 0, as in Chrome.
- IDs start at 1 and wrap at the largest `long`, skipping those in use. A timer's arguments go to its function, with the window as `this`.
- The timers stop with their window's context, when the Page loads a document.

### Idle callbacks

`requestIdleCallback()` and `cancelIdleCallback()` run as Blink's `ScriptedIdleTaskController` runs them, in idle periods that [`idle_helper.h`](../native/page/idle_helper.h) ports from Blink's `IdleHelper`. While a Page begins no [frames](#animation-frames), its periods are the long idle periods that Chrome has once a page expects none: each lasts until the next delayed task is due, and at most 50 milliseconds, and one that would be shorter than a millisecond waits a millisecond. A frame ends the idle period, and a short one lasts from the frame's end until the next vsync, as Blink's `WillBeginFrame()`, `DidCommitFrameToCompositor()` and `BeginMainFrameNotExpectedUntil()` have it, and long ones start again once frames stop, with `BeginFrameNotExpectedSoon()`. Idle tasks wait behind a fence that lets through only those posted before the period started, so the callbacks of a period share its end as their deadline, and one requested inside a period waits for the next, which starts when this one ends. Periods pause when no callback waits, and start again with the next request. `timeRemaining()` is coarsened as `performance.now()` is, and 0 past the deadline. A callback with a timeout runs as a task of its own once the timeout has passed, if it has not run by then, with `didTimeout` true. IDs start at 1 and wrap at the largest `long`. What a callback throws is reported as an uncaught error. Chrome's recording of these orders and deadlines is of a page that has loaded: while a page loads, Chrome draws frames and its idle periods end with them, and a Page begins frames only for its animation frame callbacks.

### Animation frames

`requestAnimationFrame()`, `webkitRequestAnimationFrame()`, `cancelAnimationFrame()` and `webkitCancelAnimationFrame()` run as Blink's `ScriptedAnimationController` and `FrameRequestCallbackCollection` run them, in [`animation_frame_controller.h`](../native/dom/animation_frame_controller.h), in the frames that [`begin_frame_source.h`](../native/page/begin_frame_source.h) begins as Chrome on Windows begins them for a visible page that asks for one. The Page lays nothing out, so a frame runs only the animation frame callbacks of the HTML Standard's rendering steps, and before them the events that the DOM queues for the next frame, as Blink's `Document::EnqueueAnimationFrameEvent()` queues a dialog's `close`, each with its microtasks.

- **When.** Frames begin at the vsyncs of the display of the Page's [Machine](persona.md#the-runtime), a period apart that Chrome works out from the refresh rate in whole microseconds, so 16,667 at 60 Hz and 6,944 at 144 Hz, at a phase of the Page's own. A request while frames do not come gets a missed frame at once, which has the time of the last vsync, as Viz sends one to a compositor that starts to observe it, so its time can be up to a period before the request. While frames come, the next begins with the first vsync whose BeginFrame reaches the compositor after the last frame ended, which is about as long after the vsync as a frame takes to start, so a display faster than that still gets a frame at each vsync. Frames stop with the next BeginFrame after a frame that asked for no other. A frame starts 1.2 milliseconds after its vsync, and a missed one 0.15 milliseconds after the request, as Chrome 157.0.8103.51 did on macOS, headless.
- **Where.** Each frame is a task of the compositor's queue, at the normal priority, and at a very high one once a task ends 100 milliseconds or more after the last frame, until the next frame, as Blink keeps rendering from starving. A frame that waits behind a busy task runs late with the time of its vsync.
- **What a callback gets.** The frame's time since the start of the navigation that loaded the document, coarsened to 100 microseconds, or 5 in a cross-origin isolated window, with a secret of its own, as Blink's `PageAnimator` gives it, so whole tenths of a millisecond, such as `162.4`, and never less than the frame before. Every callback of a frame gets the same time. A callback of `webkitRequestAnimationFrame()` gets that time on the wall clock, in milliseconds since the Unix epoch, as precise as the clock is.
- **How.** Each callback that waits when a frame begins runs in it, in the order they were asked for, with undefined as `this`, its microtasks after it, and what it throws reported as an uncaught error. A callback asked for in a frame runs in the next. One that an earlier callback of its frame cancels does not run. A `scheduler.yield()` in a callback inherits what the task that asked for it had, as `FrameRequestCallback`'s task attribution carries it.
- **IDs** start at 1 for each document, shared by both forms, as in Chrome, and a request gives 0 once the window's context has gone.

[`tests/test_page_animation_frames.py`](../tests/test_page_animation_frames.py) holds a Page to what Chrome 157 gave for the same scripts, and to the period of its Machine's refresh rate.

### The scheduler

`scheduler.postTask()` posts its callback, after its `delay`, to the queue of its priority: its `priority` option, else that of its `TaskSignal`, else `user-visible`, which shares the normal priority with the HTML Standard's task sources. Its promise settles as the callback returns or throws, and rejects with the signal's reason if the signal aborts before the callback has returned. `scheduler.yield()` continues in a queue that comes before the tasks of its priority, which it inherits, with the signal, from the task it runs in, as Blink's task attribution carries it: a `postTask()` callback has its own, an idle callback the `background` priority, and V8 hands that state on to the promise reactions they leave, through the isolate's continuation-preserved embedder data ([`scheduler_task_context.h`](../native/dom/scheduler_task_context.h)). A timer or an event carries none, so a `yield()` there continues at `user-visible`, as in Chrome. A `TaskSignal` keeps the priority that it was made with, since `setPriority()` is a Known Gap.

### Web Locks and the Cookie Store

`navigator.locks` keeps Web Locks within the Page: [`lock_service.h`](../native/dom/lock_service.h) stands where Chrome's browser keeps a storage key's lock group, with one group for each storage key across the Page's documents and workers, Chrome's order of grants, shared and exclusive modes, `ifAvailable`, `steal` and signals, and `query()` gives the held locks and the waiting requests by name, each with the document's or worker's client ID. What a context asks reaches the service from a task, and the grant comes back from another on the context's own thread, as a Mojo message each way would, so a grant lands after a timer set beside its request, as in Chrome. The service holds its state under a lock, which every thread's contexts reach, and a context that goes, such as a worker that ends, lets its locks and requests go at once, with those it let go of just before, whose release was still on the way. A document of an opaque origin has none, and gets Chrome's `SecurityError`. Chrome shares a storage key's locks across the tabs of a profile; a Page shares them across its own documents and workers, the only clients it has.

`window.cookieStore` reads and writes the Session's cookies for the document, as Blink's `CookieStore` asks `RestrictedCookieManager`: the Page checks names, values, domains, with the public suffix list, and paths with Blink's messages before it asks, the Session makes and stores the cookie as `RestrictedCookieManager` does, always secure and never HttpOnly, and each promise settles from a task once the Session has answered ([The messages](#the-messages), 19 to 24). While a page listens for `change`, the Session tells the store of each change to the document's cookies that its script may see, and the store fires a `CookieChangeEvent`, whose `changed` and `deleted` are frozen arrays of `CookieListItem`s, each with the members Blink gives, in the order of their names. A document with an opaque origin, such as about:blank, gets a `SecurityError`, and so does the command line.

### Clocks

The clocks are coarsened as Chrome's are, against timing attacks, each with a secret of the process that decides where the clamped value steps to the next:

- `performance.now()` and `performance.timeOrigin` to 100 microseconds, as Blink's `TimeClamper` does for a context that is not cross-origin isolated, and to 5 microseconds for one that is ([Cross-origin isolation](#cross-origin-isolation)). So does every time that Blink coarsens with the context's capability: `Event.timeStamp`, an idle deadline's `timeRemaining()`, the times of the Performance Timeline and `performance.timing`, and those that an animation frame callback gets. `now()` counts from the start of the navigation that loaded the document, which the Session sends, and from when the context was made for about:blank. A Page's navigation starts when the caller opens the Page, before its process starts, as Chrome starts a tab's navigation before its renderer runs, so its fetch starts a few milliseconds later, as Chrome's does. Every time of [the Performance Timeline](#the-performance-timeline) is coarsened with the same clamper. So `performance.now()` gives multiples of 0.1, up to the rounding of a double, such as `52.80000000074506`.
- `Date.now()` and `new Date()` read the clock that the program's `v8::Platform` gives V8, coarsened to 5 microseconds as gin's `TimeClamper` does, which shows only within 5 microseconds of a millisecond's boundary.

Given a script on its command line instead of `--session=`, the program has no Session to start it, so its `navigator` and `screen` give empty strings and zeros, `devicePixelRatio` 1, and its frames come at 60 Hz. It runs the script, then its event loop until no task is ready and no timer, idle callback, animation frame callback or event, or scheduler task waits, that of an `AbortSignal.timeout()` among them, and prints the result, which [building.md](building.md#build-the-runtime) describes: what a promise that the script returns was fulfilled with, or why it was rejected, or that it never settled. There, `fetch()` fails, for want of a Session. Either way, the context has the interfaces that the Runtime implements, as the Page's runtime-enabled features expose them ([the bindings](#the-bindings)).

### What Chrome adds beyond the IDL files

Chrome's renderer puts objects on a page that no Web IDL file declares, and Blink gives some values from its own state. A Page has them as Chrome 157 shows them on a top-level page, which [`tests/test_page_renderer.py`](../tests/test_page_renderer.py) holds it to:

- **`window.chrome`**, a plain object on the global object, from [`native/page/chrome_object.cc`](../native/page/chrome_object.cc). Chrome adds it once the global object has what Window's template gives it and before what the context decides, so it comes right after V8's builtins, `Temporal` and `WebAssembly`, and before members such as `crashReport` and `cookieStore`, and so does the Page's.
  - `loadTimes()` and `csi()`, from `chrome/renderer/loadtimes_bindings.cc`, are functions with no name and a `prototype`. Each call gives a new object, whose properties ignore what a page assigns to them.
    - `loadTimes()` gives the navigation's times in seconds, read from `performance.timing`, as Chrome's reads Blink's `WebPerformance`: `requestTime` and `startLoadTime` its `navigationStart`, `commitLoadTime` its `responseStart`, the first byte of the response's headers, `finishDocumentLoadTime` and `finishLoadTime` the ends of `DOMContentLoaded` and `load`, each 0 until it has come.
    - `firstPaintTime` stays 0, because the Page paints nothing, where Chrome's comes once a page has painted. That is a Known Gap.
    - `navigationType` is `"Other"`, as for a typed URL, `"BackForward"` for a document of a traversal of the session history, or `"Reload"` for one of a reload. `wasFetchedViaSpdy`, `wasNpnNegotiated`, `npnNegotiatedProtocol` and `connectionInfo` are the response's connection, which the Session sends.
    - `csi()` gives `startE` and `onloadT`, the start and the end of `DOMContentLoaded` in milliseconds, `pageT`, the time since the start, and `tran`, 15, 6 for a document of a traversal of the session history, or 16 for one of a reload.
  - `app` is what Chrome's extensions system gives every web page that no installed app covers:
    - `isInstalled`, `false`, and `getIsInstalled()`, also `false`;
    - `getDetails()`, `null`, and `runningState()`, `"cannot_run"`;
    - `installState()`, which calls a callback with `"not_installed"` from a later task;
    - the enumerations `InstallState` and `RunningState`.

    Each method throws the extensions system's `TypeError`, such as `Error in invocation of app.getDetails(): `, for an argument its signature does not take. Members that only an installed extension's pages or the Web Store get, such as `chrome.runtime`, are not there.
- **`performance.memory` and `console.memory`** give Blink's `MemoryInfo`, a new object each time, with the sizes of V8's heap, from [`native/dom/memory_info.cc`](../native/dom/memory_info.cc). `MemoryInfo` has no interface object, so a page reaches its prototype only through one.
  - `performance.memory` is precise while the document is at an HTTP or HTTPS URL, as Chrome's site isolation locks a desktop renderer to the site of its document, and read at most once in 50 milliseconds.
  - `console.memory` is always bucketized: each size rounded up to one of 100 sizes from about 10 MB to about 4 GB, read at most once in 20 minutes.
  - Both read one cache, as in Blink, so a reading of one precision stands for the other until the other's time is up.
  - `jsHeapSizeLimit` follows the Machine's memory ([building.md](building.md#what-chromes-renderer-sets-up-on-its-isolate)). The used and total sizes follow what the page has done, and the Normal Form drops them.

What else Chrome's renderer sets up for V8, such as `SharedArrayBuffer`, `WebAssembly.compileStreaming()` and `Atomics.wait()`, is in [building.md](building.md#what-chromes-renderer-sets-up-on-its-isolate).

## The channel

The channel is the native core's own, on `//base`, and not Chromium's Mojo ([ADR-0009](adr/0009-a-process-for-each-page.md)). In the library, the network thread reads and writes it, on its message pump: with a watch on the socket on macOS and Linux, and with overlapped reads and writes on the pipe, which complete on the thread's completion port, on Windows. The Page program does the same on its I/O thread. Neither ever blocks on it: what the system cannot take yet waits in memory.

Each message travels as a frame: the size of its payload and its type, each a 32-bit little-endian number, and then the payload. Each side refuses a frame whose payload is larger than 16 MiB, so that the other cannot make it hold more for one message. So a script can be at most 16 MiB less 8 bytes, and a result whose JSON is larger fails with a message that says so.

Both programs come from one tree and ship together, so each speaks exactly one version of the messages, sends it in its hello and refuses any other. The version is a fingerprint of the messages, which the build works out: the first 32 bits of the SHA-256 of the files that define their bytes, which [`native/BUILD.gn`](../native/BUILD.gn) lists in `_page_message_sources`, and of the files that each program builds on its own but that must agree byte for byte with the other's, which it lists in `_page_message_counterparts`: the two ways of writing a storage key, the library's, which a broadcast to a Page carries, and the Page's, which the Page matches it against. Each file goes in with its name and its text with LF line endings, in the order of their names ([`native/page_protocol_version.py`](../native/page_protocol_version.py)). So every Host works out the same number from the same tree, any change to those files changes it, and no file holds it. A refusal of a hello gives both versions in hexadecimal.

[`native/page_messages.h`](../native/page_messages.h) holds the frames and the messages, which both programs build. [`native/page_channel.h`](../native/page_channel.h) holds the channel, with one file for macOS and Linux and one for Windows.

## The messages

| Type | Name | Way | Payload |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Hello | Each way, first and once | The version of the messages that the sender speaks: a fingerprint of the messages, a 32-bit number ([The channel](#the-channel)) |
| 2 | Refused | Each way | Why the sender refuses what it got, in UTF-8. The sender closes the channel after it |
| 3 | Start | To the Page, once, after the hello | A JSON object with what the Page would otherwise read of the machine and the browser, each key of the `start` option of `mitto_page_open()`: `time_zone`, `locale`, `processors`, `touch_points`, `work_area_x`, `work_area_y`, `work_area_width`, `work_area_height`, `window_state`, `window_x`, `window_y`, `window_width`, `window_height`, `contrast_theme`, `inverted_colors`, `memory`, `screen_width`, `screen_height`, `scale`, `refresh_rate`, `viewport_width`, `viewport_height`, `color_scheme`, `reduced_motion`, `reduced_transparency`, `effective_type` and `navigator`, an object of `user_agent`, `platform`, `languages` and `user_agent_data`, which `mitto.h` describes. Beside them, `connection`, which the library adds: the `rtt` in milliseconds and the `downlink` in Mbps that `navigator.connection` gives about:blank's document. And `features`, what the Page turns on and off beside the Engine Release's runtime-enabled features and V8's defaults: `enable_blink_features` and `disable_blink_features`, lists of names of runtime-enabled features, `v8_flags`, a list of V8's flags, each as gin writes one, such as `--no-foo`, and `deprecated_unload`, what the variations seed says of unload's deprecation: `enabled`, `allow_list`, null or a list of hosts, and `by_bucket`, null or a `percent` and a `bucket` that may be null ([persona.md](persona.md#what-a-page-of-each-persona-turns-on-and-off)); and `media_devices`, whether the Machine has an `audio_input`, a `video_input` and an `audio_output` |
| 4 | Evaluate | To the Page | The number of the call, a 64-bit number, and the script in UTF-8 |
| 5 | Evaluated | To the Session | The number of the call, a byte that is 0 for a result and 1 for a failure, and then the result as JSON, or why it failed, in UTF-8. Results come in any order, since a call whose result is a pending promise waits for it |
| 6 | Console | To the Session | A byte for the level, 1 to 5 for debug, log, info, warning and error, and then the message, in UTF-8 |
| 7 | Uncaught error | To the Session | What a console says of an error that no script caught, such as `Uncaught (in promise) Error: no`, in UTF-8 |
| 8 | Request | To the Session | The ID of the document that asks, a 64-bit number, as each message of a document starts with ([below](#the-documents-of-a-page)), the number of the request, a 64-bit number, larger than that of each request before it, a byte for its Request Kind, 1 for `script`, 2 for `fetch`, 3 for `navigation`, a navigation that the document starts ([Navigations a page starts](#navigations-a-page-starts)), 4 for `image`, 5 for `style`, 6 for `font`, 7 for `module`, 8 for `xhr`, 9 for `worker`, 10 for `beacon`, 11 for `iframe`, 12 for `favicon`, 13 for `module_json` and 14 for `prefetch`, a byte of flags, 2 for a navigation that the user activated, which sends `Sec-Fetch-User`, 4 for one that reloads, which validates what the cache holds, 8 for a request that outlives its document, a fetch's or a beacon's, 16 for a synchronous XHR, 32 for a request with no referrer, 64 for a fetch whose body is a stream, which messages of type 160 and 161 bring, and 128 for `fetchLater()`'s, a keepalive fetch of a whole body, which the Session holds until a message of type 220, its document goes or the Page ends ([fetch()](#fetch)), of a window's document to a potentially trustworthy URL only, up to three times 640 KiB of their URLs without the fragment, methods, referrers, header lines and bodies at once, what Blink's quotas let a frame tree queue, with room for a string body, which counts there by its UTF-16 length and comes as UTF-8, past which one is dropped without a word, as if it went, before anything of it is copied, since the Page may have let it go before an answer came, then six bytes that leave the Kind's own with 0: its mode, 1 to 4 for `navigate`, `same-origin`, `no-cors` and `cors`, its credentials mode, 1 to 3 for `omit`, `same-origin` and `include`, its referrer policy, 1 to 8 for the policies in alphabetical order, from `no-referrer` to `unsafe-url`, its priority, one more than a `net::RequestPriority`, from 2 for `IDLE` to 6 for `HIGHEST`, and whether it is incremental, 1 for no and 2 for yes, a byte for a fetch's cache mode, 0 for `default`, 1 for `no-store`, 2 for `reload`, 3 for `no-cache`, 4 for `force-cache` and 5 for `only-if-cached`, which the library gives //net's load flags as Blink's `GetLoadFlagsForWebUrlRequest()` does, and a byte for its redirect mode, 0 for `follow`, 1 for `error` and 2 for `manual`, where a redirect that is not followed is the response, both 0 for any other kind, then the target of a frame's navigation, a 64-bit number, 0 for any other request, then its URL in UTF-8, its method, the header lines that the page's script set, each `name: value` and a line feed, and its referrer, empty for the document's URL, each of these four after its size as a 32-bit number, and then a byte that is 1 if a body follows, which is the rest. The library refuses a value that the Kind never takes, such as a navigation's flags on another Kind, a method, header lines or a body on an element's request, a mode that the Kind never has, a `worker` in mode `cors`, which is what a module worker's top-level graph imports, from anything but a worker, a mode `no-cors` with a method that is not CORS-safelisted, a referrer that is neither a URL of the document's origin nor the final URL of a response that came whole to an earlier request of the context, such as that of a module of another origin, which names it for what it imports, a target on anything but a frame's navigation, and a body that streams on anything but a fetch whose method has a body, with no body of bytes |
| 9 | Credit | To the Session | The number of a request, and how many more bytes of its body the Page has taken, a 32-bit number |
| 10 | Get cookies | To the Session | The ID of the document, and the number of the question, a 64-bit number |
| 11 | Set cookie | To the Session | The ID of the document, and what a script set its `cookie` to, in UTF-8 |
| 12 | Loaded | To the Session | The ID of a document whose window's `load` event has been dispatched |
| 13 | Response started | To the Page | The number of the request, 0 for the navigation, its status, a 32-bit number, a byte that is 1 if it followed a redirect, a byte that is 1 if its tainting is cors, because a hop went to another origin than the document's, a byte that is 1 if it came over HTTP/2 or QUIC and one that is 1 if ALPN chose its protocol, when the navigation started, as a 64-bit count of microseconds of `base::TimeTicks`, which every process of the machine shares, and 0 for the Page's own requests, what the document's `navigator.connection` gives, its `rtt` as a 32-bit number and its `downlink` as a 64-bit IEEE double, each 0 for the Page's own requests. Then what [the Performance Timeline](#the-performance-timeline) needs, as Chrome's network service hands it to Blink: ten times of its last hop from //net's `LoadTimingInfo`, each a 64-bit count of microseconds of `base::TimeTicks` or 0 for a step that did not happen, its start, the start and end of its DNS lookup and of its connection, the start of TLS, of sending, of the first and last byte of the headers and of the final response's headers, then six bytes, each 0 or 1: whether it reused a connection, passed the TAO check on every hop, was validated by the server from the cache, followed a redirect to another origin, restarted for Critical-CH, which only a navigation does, and has opaque tainting, as a script has once a hop went to another origin than the document's, never with cors. Then how many bytes had come with its headers, a 64-bit number, none from the cache, how many redirects it followed, a 32-bit number of at most 20, and three times as before: when its first hop started, when its last redirect's headers ended, and when the navigation restarted for Critical-CH, if no redirect came after. Then the padding of an opaque response, or of a redirect that a `fetch()` of redirect mode `manual` does not follow ([The Cache API](#the-cache-api)), a 64-bit number below 14,777,344, 0 for any other. Then its URL, its reason phrase, the name of its connection's protocol as `net::HttpConnectionInfoToString()` gives it, such as `h2`, and the protocol that ALPN chose, `unknown` for none, and, for a navigation of the Page, the `Referer` that its last hop sent, as //net kept it through each redirect, empty for none and for every other request, each after its size as a 32-bit number, and then its header lines, each `name: value` and a line feed, but for `Set-Cookie` and `Set-Cookie2` |
| 14 | Response body | To the Page | The number of the request, and the next bytes of its body |
| 15 | Response ended | To the Page | The number of the request, and a net error, a 32-bit number: 0 when all of the body has come, and otherwise why the request failed, with or without a response: `ERR_ABORTED` with none for a navigation whose response is no document, such as a 204 or a download, and the navigation that opened the Page ends this way too when it has no document. Then what it took, each a 64-bit number: its body's bytes as they came over the wire, before any content coding was undone, all the bytes it received, headers included, and when it ended, as a count of microseconds of `base::TimeTicks`, 0 for a request that the library refused before it started, as Blink fails such a fetch before it loads |
| 16 | Cookies | To the Page | The number of the question that it answers, and the document's cookies, as a `Cookie` header's line holds them |
| 17 | Cancel | To the Session | The number of a request that the Page no longer wants. Its end follows, with `ERR_ABORTED`, unless it has ended already |
| 18 | Known gap | To the Session | The name of a member whose behaviour the Runtime lacks, such as `Highlight.prototype.priority` or `new Highlight`, in UTF-8, the first time the document reaches it ([What a Known Gap does](#what-a-known-gap-does)) |
| 19 | Cookie store get | To the Session | The ID of the document, and a question of its `CookieStore`: its number, a 64-bit number, a byte that is 1 for all of the document's cookies and 0 for those of a name, and the name in UTF-8, empty for all |
| 20 | Cookie store set | To the Session | The ID of the document, and a cookie for its `CookieStore` to store: the number of the question, when the cookie expires, a 64-bit count of microseconds since the Windows epoch as `base::Time` holds it, 0 for a session cookie, a byte for its `SameSite`, 1 to 3 for strict, lax and none, a byte that is 1 if it is partitioned, its name, value and domain, empty for a host cookie, each after its size as a 32-bit number, and then its path, all in UTF-8 |
| 21 | Cookie store listen | To the Session | The ID of the document, and a byte, 1 for its `CookieStore` to hear of each change to its cookies, and 0 to stop |
| 22 | Cookie store cookies | To the Page | The number of the question that it answers, how many cookies follow, a 32-bit number, and each cookie: a byte of flags, 1 if it is secure, 2 if it is partitioned and 4 if it expires, a byte for its same-site mode, 0 for one that is unspecified and 1 to 3 for strict, lax and none, when it expires, a 64-bit count of microseconds since the Unix epoch, and its name, value, domain, with a leading dot for a domain cookie, and path, each after its size as a 32-bit number |
| 23 | Cookie store stored | To the Page | The number of the question that it answers, and a byte that is 1 if the cookie was stored |
| 24 | Cookie store change | To the Page | The ID of the document whose `CookieStore` listens, a 64-bit number, a byte that is 1 for a cookie that went and 0 for one that was set, and the cookie, as a cookie of type 22 is. Each document listens on its own, and a document that goes stops listening |
| 25 | Broadcast | To the Session | The ID of the document, and a message of one of its `BroadcastChannel`s, for the Session's other Pages: the channel's name in UTF-8, after its size as a 32-bit number, a byte that is 1 if the message is locked to the Page's agent cluster, as one that holds a WebAssembly module is, and then the message's bytes as the Page serialized them, which the library does not read ([Structured clone and messaging](#structured-clone-and-messaging)) |
| 26 | Broadcast received | To the Page | A message of a `BroadcastChannel` of another Page of the Session, for one of this Page's contexts of its storage key: that key, serialized as Blink serializes it, after its size as a 32-bit number, then the message as a message of type 25 holds it after its document's ID. The Page hands it only to the channels of that key. One too large for a frame with its key stays out |
| 27 | Storage bind | To the Session | The ID of a document, and a storage area of its storage key, as Blink binds a `StorageArea` and adds its observer when a window first reaches its `Storage` ([localStorage and sessionStorage](#localstorage-and-sessionstorage)): the area's number, a 64-bit number larger than that of each area before it, a byte, 1 for `localStorage` and 2 for `sessionStorage`, and the number of its first observer, a 64-bit number larger than that of each observer before it. The library works out the area's storage key from the document it loaded |
| 28 | Storage get all | To the Session | The number of an area, and of a new observer that takes the place of its last one. Type 33 answers it, and the Page waits for the answer, as Blink's `GetAll()` is a synchronous call |
| 29 | Storage put | To the Session | A change to an area: its number, the source of the change, the key after its size as a 32-bit number, and the value, which is the rest. The source is the random token of the writer's `Storage` object, two 64-bit numbers, and its document's URL in UTF-8, after its size, as Blink's `StorageAreaSource` holds them. A key and a value are the bytes that Blink sends: for `localStorage` a byte that is 1 for Latin-1 and 0 for UTF-16, and the characters, and for `sessionStorage` a key in UTF-8 and a value in UTF-16 |
| 30 | Storage delete | To the Session | The number of an area, the source, and the key, which is the rest |
| 31 | Storage delete all | To the Session | The number of an area, the number of a new observer or 0, and the source. Blink's `clear()` takes a new observer when the window has not read the area, so that the first event the observer hears of is this one |
| 32 | Storage unbind | To the Session | The number of an area that the Page no longer uses |
| 33 | Storage entries | To the Page | What an area holds, which type 28 asked for: its number, a byte that is 1 in the last frame of the answer, and then each key and value after its size, in the order of their bytes. An area of 10 MiB of short keys does not fit in one frame, so the answer may take several |
| 34 | Storage key changed | To the Page | For an observer of an area, a change to it, the writer's own included: the observer's number, the source, the key and the new value, each after its size, and a byte that is 1 if the old value follows, which is the rest |
| 35 | Storage key change failed | To the Page | For an observer, a change that the Session refused for the quota: the observer's number, the source, and the key, which is the rest |
| 36 | Storage key deleted | To the Page | For an observer, a key that went: the observer's number, the source, the key after its size, and a byte that is 1 if the old value follows |
| 37 | Storage all deleted | To the Page | For an observer, a `clear()`: the observer's number, a byte that is 1 if the area held anything, and the source |
| 38 | Storage part | To the Page | The next part of the payload of a storage event of types 34 to 37 that does not fit in one frame, such as a change from one value of 9 MB to another: the Page joins the parts and the payload of the event that follows them, at most 40 MiB in all. Chrome's pipes carry such a message whole |
| 40 | Cache API | To the Session | The ID of the document, and what its `CacheStorage` or one of its `Cache`s asks, and `navigator.storage.estimate()`: a byte for the operation, and its fields, which [`native/cache_storage_messages.h`](../native/cache_storage_messages.h) lists ([The Cache API](#the-cache-api)). A question has a 64-bit number larger than that of each before it; a call of a `CacheStorage` names its bucket after it, the number that a message of type 181 gave a `StorageBucket`, or 0 for the default bucket ([Storage buckets](#storage-buckets)); a cache's name travels in UTF-16, as a page gave it; a request as its URL, method and header lines; a response as its URL list, status, reason phrase, type, header lines, the names that CORS exposes, its time, its padding, and the size and number of its body. The parts of a put's body, and the reads of a stored body and the credit for it, are operations of their own |
| 41 | Cache API answer | To the Page | What the Session answers a question of type 40, with Chrome's `CacheStorageError` and what the question asks for, or asks of a put's body, at most so many bytes of it, or the next bytes of a stored body and its end |
| 42 | IndexedDB | To the Session | The ID of the document or worker, and what its `IDBFactory`, or one of its `IDBDatabase`s, `IDBTransaction`s or `IDBCursor`s, asks, as Blink asks the browser over `blink.mojom`'s interfaces: a byte for the operation, and its fields, which [`native/indexed_db_messages.h`](../native/indexed_db_messages.h) lists ([IndexedDB](#indexeddb)). A call that is answered has a 64-bit number larger than that of each before it, and a call of an `IDBFactory` names its bucket after it, as a call of a `CacheStorage` does; where Mojo hands an interface over, the Page names it by a number of its own: a connection and its upgrade's transaction in an open, a transaction in its creation, a cursor in its opening. Keys travel as a type byte and their data, names in UTF-16, values as `SerializedScriptValue` writes them |
| 43 | IndexedDB callback | To the Page | What the Session answers a call of type 42, by its number, with Chrome's `IDBException` code and message when it fails: an open hears blocked and upgradeneeded before its end, and `getAll()` its records in chunks of at most 1000; and what it tells a connection: versionchange, a forced close, and the end of each of its transactions |
| 70 | Document committed | To the Session | A document that the Page has made: its ID, a 64-bit number larger than that of each document and worker before it, a byte for what it is made of, 0 for a frame's initial about:blank, 1 for the document of a navigation's response, 2 for an about:blank that a navigation commits, 3 for a frame's srcdoc document, 4 for Chrome's error page, which a frame commits once its navigation fails without a response, 5 for a frame's document of a data: URL, which the Page answers itself, and 6 for a document of a blob: URL, which the Page answers itself from its Blob, a byte of flags, 1 for a frame's document whose origin a sandbox makes opaque, as it does that of each document under it, its parent, a 64-bit number, 0 for the top-level document and otherwise the document whose frame element holds its frame, then for 1 the number of the navigation, for 2 the document that started its navigation and the frame's document that it takes the place of, for 3 and 4 the frame's document that it takes the place of, a 64-bit number, and for 5 and 6 the document that started its navigation and the document that it takes the place of, each a 64-bit number, then its URL, the rest of the payload. The library gives an about:blank its initiator's origin, a srcdoc document its parent's origin and its parent's URL as the source of its requests' `Referer`, and an error page and a data: URL's document an opaque origin, whose requests go as that origin's, with `Origin: null` and no `Referer`, a data: URL's document with the embedder policy that its initiator had as its navigation started (type 72), and a blob: URL's document the URL's origin, which has to be its initiator's. A top-level document takes the place of the one before it and of everything under it, and a frame's document of a navigation, an about:blank, a srcdoc, an error page, a data: URL's or a blob: URL's document the place of the frame's document that it names. No Page sends 6 yet, and the library refuses it until it can tie it to a navigation of its own and a Blob, for [#283](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/283)'s blob: documents |
| 71 | Same-document navigation | To the Session | The ID of the document, and the URL in UTF-8 that a navigation within it gave it, such as one to a fragment, which is of the document's origin, or, for the top-level document, whose traversals go to entries at it, the about:blank that type 73 gave it or one before it of its origin, which keeps the origin |
| 72 | About:blank navigation | To the Session | The ID of a frame's document, the ID of the document that has started a navigation of the frame to about:blank or to a `data:` URL, which commits without the network from a later task, as a document of type 70 of source 2 or 5, and a byte for which: 0 for about:blank and 1 for a `data:` URL, whose commit has to be of that source. 2, with 0 as the initiator, says that the one that waited ended with no document, as a download, an error page or a navigation that a newer one or `document.open()` canceled, and the library forgets it. The about:blank takes the origin, embedder policy and cookie URL that its initiator has now, and the `data:` URL's document its embedder policy, as the browser's `NavigationRequest` holds them from its start, though the initiator goes before it commits |
| 73 | Document opened | To the Session | The ID of a document, and the ID of the document of another window, of its origin, that has opened it with `document.open()`. The library gives it that document's URL, without its fragment, unless that is about:srcdoc, and its cookie URL, from what it knows of that document, as Chrome's browser takes the URL with `DidOpenDocumentInputStream()` |
| 100 | Worker started | To the Session | A dedicated worker that a context has started: its ID, a 64-bit number larger than that of each document and worker before it, the ID of the context that started it, a 64-bit number, a byte for where its script comes from, 1 for the response of a request of Kind `worker` that its creator made, 2 for a blob: URL of its creator's origin and 3 for a data: URL, a byte of flags, 1 for a module worker, the number of the request for 1, and 0 for the others, a 64-bit number, and then for 2 the blob: URL in UTF-8, which is the rest |
| 101 | Context gone | To the Session | The ID of a context that goes while its parent stays, a frame's document that its element let go or a worker that ended, which the Page names no more. The contexts under it go with it |
| 140 | Network hint | To the Session | The ID of the document, a byte for what it hints, 0 for a DNS prefetch and 1 for a preconnect, a byte that is 1 if a preconnect's connection may carry credentials, always 0 for a DNS prefetch, and then the origin in UTF-8, an HTTP or HTTPS URL with the path `/` and nothing after it, as Blink's `WebPrescientNetworking` hands the browser a scheme, host and port ([Links and the document's resources](#links-and-the-documents-resources)). The library refuses a hint of a worker and one of any other URL |
| 160 | Request body part | To the Session | The number of a request whose body is a stream, and the next bytes of it, at most 64 KiB, and no more than the credit that messages of type 162 gave ([fetch()](#fetch)) |
| 161 | Request body ended | To the Session | The number of a request whose body is a stream, and a byte that is 0 when all of the body has gone and 1 when its stream errored, which fails the request with `ERR_FAILED`, as Blink's `BytesUploader` fails it |
| 162 | Request body credit | To the Page | The number of a request whose body is a stream, and how many more bytes of it the library takes, a 32-bit number: 64 KiB when `//net` first reads the body, where Chrome's network service asks the renderer to start reading the stream, and then as much as the library has handed to `//net` |
| 180 | Storage buckets | To the Session | The ID of the document or worker, and what its `StorageBucketManager` or one of its `StorageBucket`s asks ([Storage buckets](#storage-buckets)): a byte for the operation, and its fields, which [`native/storage_bucket_messages.h`](../native/storage_bucket_messages.h) lists. A question has a 64-bit number larger than that of each before it. An open names its bucket in UTF-8, after its size as a 32-bit number, then a byte of flags for the policies that its options gave, `persisted` and its value, `quota`, `durability` and whether it is strict, and `expires`, and then the quota, a 64-bit number, and the expiration, a 64-bit count of microseconds since the Windows epoch as `base::Time` holds it, each if given; a delete names its bucket the same way; a `StorageBucket`'s calls name the bucket by the number that type 181 gave it, `setExpires()` with its time; and a release says that the Page no longer uses a bucket, as dropping a `BucketHost` remote does |
| 181 | Storage buckets answer | To the Page | What the Session answers a question of type 180, by its number: an open's error, 0 for none, 1 to 3 for Chrome's `BucketError`, unknown, quota exceeded and an invalid expiration, and the bucket's number, larger than that of each bucket before it, for none; or whether the call could, and what it asks for: the names of `keys()`, the byte of `persist()` and `persisted()`, the usage and quota of `estimate()`, the durability, 0 for relaxed and 1 for strict, or the expiration, if any |
| 220 | Send deferred request | To the Session | The number of a request with flag 128, a 64-bit number, which the Session sends now and finishes with no client, as Chrome's browser sends a `FetchLaterLoader`'s once Blink's `DeferredLoader` asks it to. Nothing of it comes back, and a Cancel of type 17 that comes first drops it ([fetch()](#fetch)) |
| 280 | Traversal request | To the Session | The ID of the top-level document that a traversal of the session history leaves, and a navigation that repeats the navigation of a document that the entry holds ([Session history](#session-history)): its number, as a request's, the ID of that document, and the ID of the document that was at the entry when the back/forward cache took it, which the library may answer with type 283 and which is not always that document, or 0, each a 64-bit number, a byte of flags, 1 for an entry that a navigation within that document made, a byte of the referrer policy, as a request's, 0 for that of the navigation's own request and otherwise the document's policy when a navigation within it made the entry, and the URL in UTF-8, which is the rest: that document's URL for an entry of its navigation, or one that a navigation within it gave it. The library makes it from what it kept of that navigation, with the Referer that it sent last, never from what the Page names, and a traversal to a URL that it did not see the document take, or no longer keeps, goes nowhere, with no request, and takes what the cache holds without validating it. Its responses come on 13 to 15, and its document on 70, as a navigation's |
| 281 | Records released | To the Session | The IDs of documents whose navigations no entry of the session history holds any more, each a 64-bit number, one at least, which the library forgets |
| 282 | Document stored | To the Session | The ID of the top-level document that the back/forward cache keeps, a 64-bit number, just before the document of type 70 that takes its place ([Session history](#session-history)). The library keeps its context apart, with its requests, which go on, and hears nothing of it while it is stored. It has no frames or workers under it, and the cache keeps at most six besides one that a traversal may restore |
| 283 | Restore | To the Page | The number of a traversal request that names a stored document, a 64-bit number, which the library answers by restoring the stored document in place of a response, unless its response said `no-store` and a cookie of its URL has changed since it committed: then the request goes as any traversal's. Nothing more comes of the request |
| 284 | Document restored | To the Session | The ID of the stored document that type 283 restored, a 64-bit number, which takes the place of the top-level document and everything under it, as a document of type 70 does, but for one that a type 282 just before kept. The library tells the caller of a navigation that the cache restored |
| 285 | Document evicted | To the Session | The ID of a stored document that goes, a 64-bit number, and its requests with it, which the Page cancelled before this |
| 300 | Isolation | To the Page | Whether the document that a navigation's response makes is cross-origin isolated, and its embedder policy, just before the response starts, as Chrome's browser commits a document with its agent cluster and its policy container: the number of the navigation, a 64-bit number, 0 for the navigation that opened the Page, and a byte, 0 for not isolated and 1 for isolated: a top-level document by `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy`, and a frame's where its parent's is and it is of its parent's origin, and a byte for its embedder policy, 0 for none, 1 for `credentialless` and 2 for `require-corp` ([Cross-origin isolation](#cross-origin-isolation)). The Page refuses one for a request that is not a navigation whose response has yet to start, and a second one for the same navigation |

The library sends its hello, the start and any script at once, without waiting for the Page's hello, because the Page reads them in order.

### The documents of a Page

Each message that a document sends names the document by an ID that the Page gives it, from 1 up, never again for another, as [#261](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/261) decided for a Page's documents, frames and workers. The Page tells the library of each document it makes, with a message of type 70, before anything that the document sends: about:blank first, as 1, then the document of each navigation's response, and of each frame, under the document that holds the frame, and of each dedicated worker, with a message of type 100, under the context that started it. Documents and workers share the IDs. The library keeps the URL that it loaded each at, the final URL of the navigation's response, a frame's initial about:blank, which has its parent's origin, or a worker's script's final URL, and works out what a message of the context asks for from that and from the frame tree, never from what the Page says: the Initiator and `Referer` of its requests, the top-level frame that partitions them and the site for cookies, which a frame of another site than the top-level one, or under one, has none of, the cookies that a document's `document.cookie` and `CookieStore` read and write, the origin of its broadcasts, and its embedder policy, from the response that it loaded each from, or its parent's or creator's for a frame's initial about:blank and a worker of a `blob:` or `data:` URL ([Cross-origin isolation](#cross-origin-isolation)). A worker's requests start from its script's URL, in its creator's frame, with no client hints, as #265 recorded Chrome's, and a worker sends no cookie message, load, same-document navigation or navigation. A top-level document takes the place of the one before it and of everything under it, which the library forgets, a frame's navigation that of the frame's document that it names, and the channel's order puts every message of the old document before it, so a request that the old document asked for keeps the old document as its Initiator while the new one loads. A frame's document that its element lets go, or a worker that ends, goes with a message of type 101. What a context that the library has forgotten still sends, such as a worker whose creator's document another took the place of while it was busy, or a worker under one that ended, the Page drops, its message of type 101 among it, so that nothing of a forgotten context comes after what made the library forget it, as Chrome's browser drops what the pipes of a context that went still carry. Each context that goes lets its keepalive requests go on without it. The Page program makes no frame yet, which the library takes for [#285](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/285), and makes dedicated workers ([Dedicated workers](#dedicated-workers)). A frame's initial about:blank and a worker of a blob: URL speak for their origin in what they ask of cookies, broadcasts and the Cache API. A context's Cache API puts that wait for a body fail when it goes. A worker of a data: URL has an opaque origin, which its requests come from, as below. In Chrome, the browser gives each document a pipe of its own to the network service, whose requests it trusts only as that document's, which is what the ID stands for on a Page's one channel.

A body travels only as far as the Page has given credit for it. Each request starts with 64 KiB of credit, as much as the library holds of a request's body for a caller, and the library reads more of the body from `//net` only once the Page has given credit back for what it took. So a Page that takes its bodies slowly, as a parser that waits for a script does, slows the server down, as a slow reader of a request does. The library answers questions for cookies in the order they come, and the channel keeps the order of everything, so a read of `document.cookie` after a write sees it, and so does a request that the Page asks for after it.

Each side refuses, with a Refused message that says why:

- a hello of another version, and any message before the hello;
- a type that it does not know, or one that only goes the other way;
- a payload that is not what its type says, such as text that is not UTF-8 or a console message of an unknown level;
- a frame larger than the channel carries;
- for the library, a result of a call that is not in progress, a message of a context that the Page has not told of, or that has gone, a document or worker whose ID is not larger than the one before, a top-level initial about:blank after another document, a document whose parent is not one of the Page's documents, a document of a navigation whose response the library has not sent or that has a document already, or of a frame's navigation under another parent than its frame's, an about:blank, a srcdoc, an error page, a data: URL's or a blob: URL's document that does not take the place of a document of its frame, a srcdoc, an error page or a data: URL's document of the top-level frame, a blob: document, an about:blank navigation whose initiator is not one of the Page's documents, one of type 72 of no kind, or that ended and names an initiator, an about:blank whose navigation did not start with type 72 of kind 0, a data: URL's document whose navigation did not start with type 72 of kind 1 from the same initiator, or whose URL is not a data: one, or that another document started, an about:blank or a data: URL's document that its parent's embedder policy blocks, a document that no document of its origin opened, a frame's navigation that does not name a frame's document, a worker whose creator is not one of the Page's contexts, whose script is not the response of a request of Kind `worker` of that creator, or whose blob: URL is of another origin, a message that only a document sends from a worker, or a navigation that a worker starts, the top-level document going while the Page stays, a request whose number is not larger than the one before, more than 2700 requests at once, as Chrome's network service allows a renderer process, credit for a request that the Page has not asked for, or for more of a body than the library sent, or a cancel of one that it has not asked for, a part or the end of the body of a request that the Page has not asked for, whose body does not stream or has ended, or more of it than the library gave credit for, or a cookie of the `CookieStore` for a domain that the document's host does not match, which the Page checks first, or a broadcast from a Page with no document of an HTTP or HTTPS origin, whose channels no other Page shares, or a same-document navigation to a URL of another origin than the document's, or for a document of an opaque origin to another URL but for its query and fragment, unless it goes from one about:blank or about:srcdoc URL to another, or a traversal that the top-level document does not leave, or to a document that the library kept nothing of, or a question of the Cache API from a document that is not of an HTTP or HTTPS origin in a secure context, or whose number is not larger than the one before, a call of a cache that the Page has not opened, a put that Blink would not send, such as one of a `POST` or with `Vary: *`, a part of a put's body that the library did not ask for, or a read of a stored body that it did not give; or a call of IndexedDB from a document whose origin may not reach it, or whose number is not larger than the one before, a connection, transaction or cursor number that the Page uses already, a call of a connection or cursor that it does not have, or a call that Chrome's browser takes for a bad message from a renderer, such as an open of version 0, a put in a readonly transaction, or a call of an object store or index that the database does not have, which also closes the storage key's databases, as Chrome does, or a storage area or observer whose number is not larger than the one before, more than 4,096 storage areas at once, a storage area of a document with no storage key, which the Page checks first, or a question for or a change to an area that is not bound, or a change of a key or value that Blink does not write, such as a key of `localStorage` with no byte for its format, or a call of the Storage Buckets API from a context that is not a secure one of an origin that is not opaque, or whose number is not larger than the one before, an open or a delete of a bucket whose name is not valid, an open with a quota of 0 or larger than 2^53 - 1, or a call or release of a bucket, or of its IndexedDB or caches, that the Session did not give the context. Also a request whose method is not a token or is forbidden, such as `TRACE`, whose header lines are not valid or include a forbidden request header, such as `Cookie` or one that starts with `Sec-`, that has a body with `GET` or `HEAD`, that asks for a script or a navigation with any of these, or that is not a navigation and has a navigation's flags: Chrome's network service refuses such a request from a renderer too, and a Page that sends one is not the Runtime's;
- for the Page, a second start, a script before the start, a start that lacks a key or has another, or a value that a Machine, a Persona or Chrome's network estimate cannot have, a time zone or locale that ICU does not know, a name in its features that is not a runtime-enabled feature, a response whose `rtt` or `downlink` Chrome cannot give, or whose timing //net cannot give, such as a negative time or more than 20 redirects, a response or body of a request that is not in progress, more of a body than it has given credit for, credit for the body of a request that is not in progress or whose body does not stream, or for more of it than the Page sent and the 64 KiB that it may have sent ahead, or cookies or an answer for a question, of `document.cookie` or of the `CookieStore`, that no script waits for, or a broadcast or a storage event before the start, an answer of the Cache API or the entries of a storage area that no script waits for, parts of a storage event larger than any, or before a message that is not one, a request for a put's body that no put holds, more of a stored body than it has given credit for, or an answer to an IndexedDB call or a Storage Buckets question that no script waits for.

The library reads what a Page sends as hostile input: a Page runs a site's scripts. It checks each frame field by field before it acts on it, and the fuzz target `page_messages_fuzzer` feeds any bytes to that code ([building.md](building.md#sanitizer-builds-and-the-fuzz-target)). When the library refuses, it also kills the Page process, rather than trusting it to end.

A refusal ends the Page, and its calls fail with `MITTO_PAGE_ERROR_REFUSED` and a message that says which side refused what, such as:

```text
the library refused a message from the Page: the Page speaks version 0x1f04c2a7 of the messages, and the library speaks 0x9be3d150
the Page refused a message from the library: the time zone "Mars/Olympus_Mons" is unknown
```

## The C API

[`mitto.h`](../native/include/mitto/mitto.h) has the Page's calls, which return at once, and its events, which wait in a native queue as a request's do ([ADR-0003](adr/0003-c-abi-and-ctypes.md)):

- `mitto_page_open()` opens a Page on a Session, with versioned JSON options, whose `start` is what the Page starts with. An unknown key is refused. With `url`, the Page navigates there once it opens, as [Loading a document](#loading-a-document) says, with what each Request Kind sends in `requests`.
- `mitto_page_evaluate()` sends a script and stores the number of its call. Each call gets one `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_EVALUATED` event, with the result as JSON or an error.
- `mitto_page_next_event()` takes the next event, blocking for as long as the caller says, and `mitto_page_event_free()` releases it. Beside `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_EVALUATED` and `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_ENDED`, a Page has a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_CONSOLE` event for each console message, with its level, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_UNCAUGHT_ERROR` event for each error that no script caught, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_KNOWN_GAP` event with the name of each Known Gap, the first time each document reaches it, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_NAVIGATION_STARTED` event, with its URL, when its document starts a navigation to another document, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_NAVIGATED` event for each navigation, the one that opened the Page and each that started, in order, once the Page has made the document of its response, after every event of the old document, with the document's URL and status, or when it has ended with no document, with `ERR_ABORTED` and the status, or another took its place first, with `ERR_ABORTED` and status 0, or has failed, with the net error, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_URL_CHANGED` event, with the new URL, when a navigation within the document changes its URL, and a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_LOADED` event when the window's `load` event has been dispatched.
- `mitto_page_end_waits()` ends each wait of `mitto_page_next_event()` on another thread, and makes each later one return at once if no event waits, so that the thread that closes a Page can stop another that waits, as `mitto_session_end_waits()` does for a Session.
- `mitto_page_process_id()` gives the ID of the Page's process.
- `mitto_page_close()` closes the Page.

At most 1000 console messages and uncaught errors of a Page wait untaken, and at most 32 MiB of their text, so that a Page that writes without end cannot fill the memory of the process that holds the library. V8's inspector keeps as many console messages for DevTools. The library drops the ones past that, and the next one that has room comes after a console warning that says how many it dropped. Results and the end of the Page are never dropped. A message's text goes up to 16 MiB less a byte, what a Page message carries, cut at a UTF-8 boundary, the Runtime's own warnings that hold what a page gave included, such as a value that an enumeration lacks.

A Page's events list its handle in the Session's list of what is ready, beside its requests' events. So the file descriptor that an asyncio caller watches on macOS and Linux, and `mitto_session_wait_for_event()` on Windows, wake for both. `mitto_session_next_ready()` names the handle and says whether it is a request's or a Page's.

## How a Page ends

Each way of ending reaps the process, so none is left behind:

- `mitto_page_close()` closes the channel. The Page process reads the end of the stream and ends at once. The call waits for that, for up to 2 seconds before it kills the process, and returns once the process has gone.
- `mitto_session_destroy()` does the same for each Page of the Session. Each Page gets a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_ENDED` event with `MITTO_PAGE_ERROR_SESSION_DESTROYED`, and its handle stays valid until it is closed.
- When the process that holds the library ends without either, the Page process ends as the table above says.
- When the Page process crashes, runs out of memory or is killed, the library reads the end of the stream on the network thread, and a worker thread reaps the process to learn how it ended. Each call in progress then fails with `MITTO_PAGE_ERROR_PROCESS_ENDED`, a `MITTO_PAGE_EVENT_ENDED` event follows, and each later call fails the same way. The message says how the process ended, such as `the Page process was ended by signal 9` or `the Page process ran out of memory`. The Session's other Pages and requests go on.

On macOS and Linux the library reaps its Page processes with `waitpid()` on the process's ID. A caller that waits for any child, or ignores `SIGCHLD`, can take a Page's exit status first. The library then says that the process ended and that something else in the process took its exit status.

## Dedicated workers

A page's `new Worker()` starts a dedicated worker as Chrome 157 does, on a thread of its own in the Page process, with an isolate of its own, an event loop of its own and the context of a `DedicatedWorkerGlobalScope` ([`native/page/dedicated_worker_thread.h`](../native/page/dedicated_worker_thread.h), [`native/dom/worker.h`](../native/dom/worker.h)). A worker may start workers of its own.

- **Its URL.** `new Worker()` resolves the script's URL against the creator's base URL, and throws a `SyntaxError` for one that does not parse and a `SecurityError` for one of another origin, with Chrome's messages, before anything is fetched. A `data:` URL is of no origin and passes, and a `blob:` URL's origin is that of the context that made it.
- **Its script** comes from the blob that a `blob:` URL names when the `Worker` is made, as the browser's `BlobURLLoader` reads it, so that revoking the URL then does not stop it, and from a `data:` URL in the Page, with no request either way. Any other comes through the Session as a request of the Kind `worker` of the creating context, of mode `same-origin` and the credentials of the options, as Chrome's browser fetches it with PlzDedicatedWorker. Its entry in the creator's Performance Timeline is of the initiator type `other`, as Chrome 157's `WorkerMainScriptLoader` adds it: named by the URL asked for, from when the response started, with none of the request's own steps nor its redirects, and only for an ok response that came whole. Known Gap: Chrome's entry starts once the worker's thread has started, so for a small script its duration is often a few milliseconds below 0, where the Page's is about 0. The worker's thread starts once the response has started, as Chrome starts it, and the Page tells the Session of the worker (message 100) before anything the worker sends. A response that fails before it starts fires a plain `error` event at the `Worker`. One that is not 2xx, or whose MIME type is not one to run, also does, once the worker has started: a worker that a window starts is held to Blink's lax check, which runs `text/html` or `text/plain`, and one that a worker starts to the strict one, which runs only a JavaScript MIME type. The worker's URL is then its script's final URL, its origin is its creator's, and it is a secure context if its creator is. A `data:` URL's script never reaches the Session: the Page decodes it as `//net`'s `DataURL` does in Chrome's renderer, the worker starts at once, from a task of its own, and its origin is opaque.
- **Its thread** has the scheduler of a Blink worker ([`native/page/worker_scheduler.h`](../native/page/worker_scheduler.h)): Blink's four queues of a worker, all at the normal priority, so tasks run in the order they were ready, with a microtask checkpoint and the report of promises rejected with no handler after each task. Its isolate is set up as Blink's `V8Initializer::InitializeWorker()` sets one up: `Atomics.wait()` works, WebAssembly has no limit of what it compiles at once, and V8's stack limit is 500 KB below where the thread's stack starts. V8's tasks for the isolate run on the worker's thread.
- **Its isolation.** A worker is cross-origin isolated if its creator is, but for one of a `data:` URL, and one whose script's embedder policy does not fit its creator's fails to load ([Cross-origin isolation](#cross-origin-isolation)).
