| `styleMedia.type`, `crashReport`, `customElements`, `trustedTypes.defaultPolicy`, `documentPictureInPicture.window` | `screen`, an object whose methods are Known Gaps, a registry with no custom elements that `document.customElementRegistry` gives too, and `null` twice |
| `document.images`, `embeds`, `plugins`, `links`, `forms`, `scripts`, `anchors`, `applets`, `all`, `children`, `childNodes`, and a node's `children` and `childNodes` | Live collections of the document's tree, each the same object on every read, `plugins` being `embeds` and `applets` always empty, as in Chrome. `document.all` is undetectable, as in Chrome, and its named properties, `namedItem()`, `item()` and its call find elements by ID and by name, as [Collections](#collections) says |
| `document.scrollingElement`, `activeElement` | The root element in standards mode and the body in quirks mode, and the body, as no element has focus, which is null before the parser has made one, as in Chrome |
| `document.lastModified` | The time that the response's `Last-Modified` gives, or else the time of the read, in the Page's time zone, as Chrome writes it: `MM/DD/YYYY hh:mm:ss` |
| `document.visibilityState`, `hidden`, `fullscreenEnabled`, `pictureInPictureEnabled`, `designMode`, `fonts.status` | `visible`, `false`, `true`, `true`, `off` and `loaded`, a tab in front that loads no web font, with their WebKit names alike. A document of no window, as `new Document()` makes, is hidden and allows neither |
| `performance.eventCounts` | Each type of event that Chrome counts, none of each, in the order Chrome's map of them gives them |
| `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures` | The 13 WGSL language features that Chrome 157 gives every page, in its order |
| `Notification.maxActions`, `MediaSource.canConstructInDedicatedWorker`, `PerformanceObserver.supportedEntryTypes`, `PressureObserver.knownSources`, `PushManager.supportedContentEncodings` | `2`, `true`, the entry types of a window that the Page's features allow, `["cpu"]` and `["aes128gcm", "aesgcm"]`, each array the same on every read |

`mitto::NewPageContext()` makes such a context, and the Page program makes one for about:blank and one for each document that its Session loads. The window's timers, animation frame callbacks, `queueMicrotask()`, `performance`, `fetch()` and `matchMedia()` have their behaviour, as [the event loop](#the-event-loop), [Animation frames](#animation-frames), [fetch()](#fetch) and [Media queries](#media-queries) say.

### Loading a document

Opening a Page with a URL navigates it, in [`native/page/document_loader.cc`](../native/page/document_loader.cc) on the Page's side and [`native/page_host.cc`](../native/page_host.cc) on the Session's, with the document's parser in [`native/dom/document_parser.cc`](../native/dom/document_parser.cc), as Blink's `HTMLDocumentParser` and `HTMLParserScriptRunner` have it. Everything that touches the network and cookies goes through the Session, as [ADR-0009](adr/0009-a-process-for-each-page.md) says:

- **What each Request Kind sends** is the Session's. Python gives the native Page what its Persona says a request of each kind sends, its header lines, priority and fetch parameters, when the Page opens. The library starts each of the Page's requests from that, with the same `RequestJob` and rules as a request of the C API's. So a Page's navigation sends what `session.get(url)` sends, and its script what `session.get(src, kind="script", initiator=url)` sends. A Page's requests run no timers, as Chrome's have none.
- **The Initiator** of a Page's request is its document, as the library loaded it: the URL that its navigation ended at, after redirects. The Page names the document by its ID ([The documents of a Page](#the-documents-of-a-page)), the Kind, the URL and, for `fetch()`, what the script gives, but never an Initiator, so a Page cannot claim another site's Initiator to get that site's cookies, as Chrome's network service does not take a renderer's word for it. A Page at about:blank has no Initiator to give, so its requests fail with `ERR_ACCESS_DENIED`. A worker of a data: URL asks as its opaque origin, as Chrome's does: `Origin: null`, `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, no `Referer`, no cookie but where it asks to include them and a cookie lets another site have it, and partitioned under its creator's top-level frame. A URL that is not HTTP or HTTPS fails with `ERR_UNKNOWN_URL_SCHEME`, and the Page goes on.
- **The navigation** is a request of Kind `navigation` with no Initiator. The library follows its redirects, as it does a request's, and sends the Page the final response, whose URL is the document's. A navigation that fails before a response leaves the Page at about:blank, and so does a response that Chrome makes no document of, a 204, a 205 or a download, as [Navigations a page starts](#navigations-a-page-starts) says of each navigation.
- **The document** gets a new context, as Blink makes a new window for a tab's first navigation, whose about:blank has an opaque origin. Calls that wait for a promise of the old context fail, as DevTools says: `Execution context was destroyed.` The body is decoded as it arrives, in pieces of up to 64 KiB, and fed to the parser, with the encoding that Chrome works out for it, as [Text encodings](#text-encodings) says. The response's MIME type decides the kind of document, as Blink's `DocumentInit::ComputeDocumentType()` does, with a feed's type, RSS or Atom, taken as `text/plain`, as Chrome's network service gives it, and `contentType` is that type ([`document_kinds.h`](../native/dom/document_kinds.h)). A text type, JavaScript's or another of `text/` but HTML, XML and XSL, makes a text document: a meta of its color scheme and a `pre` with Blink's inline style, which holds the text as plaintext's, with its first line feed kept, as Blink's `TextDocumentParser` makes it. JSON's type makes its text a `pre` beside the formatter's container, one text node for each piece of the body that arrives, as Blink's `JSONDocumentParser` appends them, in UTF-8. An empty text or JSON body makes an empty HTML document. An image makes the tree of Blink's `ImageDocument` for a top-level frame, with its inline styles, on its first piece of the body, whose img takes the response itself as its image and fetches nothing, and at its end the title of the file's name and the size that the image's header gives, and the img's style of a loaded image; an empty image makes a document with no children. The image is not decoded, so what of its style follows its size or whether it moves is a Known Gap. Audio or video makes the video element of Blink's `MediaDocument` at the end of the body, which plays nothing. Each of these stays in no-quirks mode. An XML, XHTML or SVG response makes an `XMLDocument` of its MIME type, which [the XML parsers](#the-xml-parsers) read. A PDF, which Chrome's viewer shows, is a Known Gap, which the parser reads as HTML.
- **Scripts** run as the HTML Standard prepares them and Blink's `ScriptLoader` and `HTMLParserScriptRunner` schedule them ([`script_loader.h`](../native/dom/script_loader.h), [`pending_script.h`](../native/dom/pending_script.h), [`document_script_runner.h`](../native/dom/document_script_runner.h)), in the orders that Chrome gave for the pages of [`tests/test_page_scripts.py`](../tests/test_page_scripts.py). The parser takes the body from tasks of the networking task source, and yields once it has parsed for 10 milliseconds since the body began, and for 500 after six such yields, as Chrome's does, though it looks at the clock only after each script and each piece of the body. An inline classic script runs as soon as the parser reaches its end tag. One with `src` is fetched through the Session as Kind `script`, with the URL resolved against the document's base URL, and the parser waits for it while other tasks run, such as scripts that the caller evaluates. What arrives of the document meanwhile waits, and the Session gets no credit for it until the parser takes it. Once the script is ready, it runs from a task of a very high priority, before the timers that wait, and the parser goes on from a later task, after the timers that the script set. One with `defer` runs after parsing, in document order, each from a task of its own at that priority. One with `async` runs from a task of the networking task source as soon as it is ready, before or after `DOMContentLoaded`, and one of another site within 3 seconds of the document's start runs at a low priority, after the tasks that wait or a second later at the latest, on a Machine of 4 GiB or more, as Chrome runs such a script while a page loads. A fetched script decodes as Chrome decodes it: with the encoding that its response's `charset` names, or else its element's `charset` attribute, taken as it is, with no white space trimmed, or else, if the attribute names no encoding, its document's encoding, as the HTML Standard has it and Chrome's preload scanner fetches a script that the parser reaches, but for a byte order mark, which wins. The query of a script's `src` is in the document's encoding, as [Text encodings](#text-encodings) says.
- **Scripts that the DOM inserts** run too. An inline one runs at once, when it becomes connected, when something is inserted into it while it is connected, or when it gets a `src` then; one with `src` runs as soon as it is ready, unless a script set its `async` to false, which makes it run in order with the others like it. Each async and in-order script holds `load` back until it has run. A script runs once: one that has run, a copy of it and one that `innerHTML` makes never run, and neither does one that has gone to another document before it runs. The first of the scripts that one call inserts runs once all of them are connected.
- **SVG's script element** runs as the HTML Standard's does, through the same `ScriptLoader`, as Blink's `SVGScriptElement` has it ([`script_element_base.h`](../native/dom/script_element_base.h)): its `href`, or else its `xlink:href`, is its source, and it has `type`, `async` and `integrity`, but no `defer`, `charset`, `nomodule`, `crossorigin` or `language`, and it never blocks rendering. One that the DOM inserts runs in order with the others like it once an `async` attribute has come and gone, as an HTML script does once a script sets its `async` to false. `document.currentScript` is the SVG script while it runs.
- **What runs**: a script whose type is a JavaScript MIME type, empty, or missing with a `language` that makes one, without `nomodule`, and with `event` and `for` only for `for="window"` and `event="onload"`. A data block does not run and fires no event. Speculation rules and web bundles are Known Gaps. A fetched script runs if its response has an ok status, its body matches `integrity` as Blink checks it, and its MIME type is one that Chrome runs as a script, and then `load` fires at its element. Otherwise, or when it cannot be fetched, `error` fires there, and from a task of the DOM manipulation task source for a `src` that is empty or no URL. As in Chrome, a response with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` whose type is no JavaScript type, and an image, audio, video or CSV one, does not run, and neither does one whose digest does not match or that came from another origin without CORS, with Chrome's console errors that say why. The console says the errors of Subresource Integrity once for each script, where Chrome says one again for each script that reuses a response from its memory cache, and checks a signed response as if it were not signed: a Known Gap. What a script leaves uncaught reaches the Session as an uncaught error, with its errors muted if it came from another origin without CORS.
- **Module scripts** run as Blink's `Modulator`, `ModuleMap` and `ModuleTreeLinker` run them ([`modulator.h`](../native/dom/modulator.h), [`module_tree_linker.h`](../native/dom/module_tree_linker.h)): a `<script type="module">`, inline or with `src`, is deferred, in document order with the deferred classic scripts, unless it is `async`, and one that the DOM inserts runs as soon as its graph is ready, or in order once a script set its `async` to false. Its graph comes before it runs: each module that it imports, at the URL that its specifier resolves to against the importing module's URL, through the document's import maps, and each URL and module type once, in the window's module map, which keeps what it fetched and fetches again what failed. Each module is fetched through the Session as Kind `module`, as a script resource of the document's, which a module of the same URL may take while the document loads ([below](#links-and-the-documents-resources)), but one at a `data:` URL, which the Page answers itself with no request, as Chrome does, in mode `cors`, with credentials only for `crossorigin="use-credentials"` and else for the same origin, the importing module's URL as its referrer, at the Kind's own priority, which only the `fetchpriority` of the top-level module moves, and runs only with a JavaScript MIME type, a JSON one for `with {type: "json"}`, and any for `with {type: "text"}`, with Chrome's console error for a MIME type that does not fit. A JSON module goes as Kind `module_json`, with the `Accept` and `Sec-Fetch-Dest` of its destination, as in Chrome. A text module goes as Kind `module` with a JavaScript module's, until Chrome 155, which has no text modules, leaves the Supported Window and the Session gets a Kind for it, a module imported by an inline module beneath a `base` element of another origin sends the document's referrer, and a CSS module, which needs M9's CSSOM, fails as a fetch does: Known Gaps. A module with no current script runs with its document's destructive writes ignored, a failed fetch anywhere in its graph fires `error` at its element, an error of parsing or linking is reported at the window, as an evaluation's is once its promise rejects, and `load` follows at the element of a module with `src`, as in Chrome. Top-level `await` holds back what imports the module, but neither the next deferred script nor the load event.
- **`import()`, `import.meta` and import maps**: `import()` in any script, a timer's string or code that `eval()` makes, in a window or a dedicated worker, resolves against the base URL of the script that calls it, or the document's or worker's base URL for one with none, and is fetched with the destination `script`, as V8 hands Blink the script's host-defined options, with that script's credentials mode, nonce and referrer policy, and settles with the module's namespace once its evaluation has, or with Chrome's `TypeError`, such as `Failed to fetch dynamically imported module: URL` or `Failed to resolve module specifier 'x'`. `import.meta.url` is a module's base URL, and `import.meta.resolve()` resolves as an import does. A `<script type="importmap">` is parsed with Blink's JSON parser and merged into the document's map at once, as many as come, with Chrome's console warnings for each rule it drops, one that does not parse is reported at the window, and one with `src` fires `error`. Its `integrity` gives a module without one its digest.
- **`document.currentScript`** is the element whose classic script runs, while the microtasks that it queued run too, and null elsewhere, such as in a timer, a listener or a script's `load` event.
- **`crossorigin`, `referrerpolicy` and `fetchpriority`** change a classic script's request, as Blink's `ScriptLoader` reads them: with `crossorigin`, it goes in mode `cors`, with credentials only for `use-credentials`, in any case, and its response has to pass the CORS check, so a script of another origin that fails it does not run; `referrerpolicy` sets its referrer policy; and its priority is Blink's: `LOWEST` for an async, deferred or script-inserted script, which `fetchpriority="high"` or blocking rendering raises to the Kind's own, and the Kind's own for any other, which `fetchpriority="low"` lowers to `LOWEST`.
- **Rendering waits for the scripts that block it**, as Blink's `RenderBlockingResourceManager` waits for them: a document's first frame, with its animation frame callbacks and `pagereveal`, comes once the parser inserts the body, or has parsed a document with none, and once each classic script fetched before the body that is potentially render-blocking has run, or its fetch has gone. That is one with `blocking="render"`, and one that the parser inserted with neither `async` nor `defer`. A module script blocks only with `blocking="render"`. A script that leaves its document's tree, as when another document adopts it, or whose `blocking` no longer says `render`, blocks no more.
- **`<base>`**: the document's base URL is the `href` of its first `base` element that has one, in tree order, resolved against the document's URL, as Blink's `Document::ProcessBaseElement()` has it, and a script's `src`, a reflected `[URL]` attribute and `baseURI` resolve against it, through `Document::CompleteUrl()`, with the query in the document's encoding, while `fetch()` and `Request` parse against it with the query in UTF-8, as Blink's `KURL(base_url, input)` does. An `href` that is no URL leaves the document with none, so `baseURI` is about:blank and only absolute URLs resolve, and one of `data:` or `javascript:`, or in a document without a window, gives the document's URL, the first with Chrome's console error. A base element's own `href` resolves against the document's URL.
- **`document.cookie`** reads and writes the Session's cookies for the document's URL, as Chrome's `RestrictedCookieManager` does for a top-level page's script: a read gives no `HttpOnly` cookie, a cookie that a script sets is stored as one set by script, with `source_type` `"script"`, and a script cannot set an `HttpOnly` one. A read waits for the Session's answer, as Blink's does. A document keeps the URL it was made at as its cookie URL, as Blink's `Document::CookieURL()`, so a navigation within it changes the path of no cookie that it sets. A document that the Session did not load over HTTP or HTTPS, such as about:blank, has an opaque origin, so its `cookie` throws a `SecurityError`, as in a new tab of Chrome. The Page never sees an `HttpOnly` cookie's value: the response headers that it gets have no `Set-Cookie`.
- **`document.write()` and `writeln()`** insert markup at the parser's insertion point, which is just after the end tag of a script that the parser runs while the script runs, and parse it at once, as Blink's `HTMLDocumentParser::insert()` does: an inline script in it runs before the write returns, and a script with `src` in it blocks the parser once the scripts around it are done, so that what follows it, written or not, waits for it. What a script writes in pieces joins up: a tag, an attribute or a character reference may end in a later write, and an element that one leaves open takes the network's markup that follows. `writeln()` adds a line feed, each argument becomes a string and they join, and a `TrustedHTML` is its string, as no policy checks it. The errors of an inline script that a script wrote count from its start, and those of the document's scripts from where they are in its markup, as written markup does not count in positions, as in Blink. Writes nested deeper than 21 do nothing, and those within them neither until they have unwound, as Blink's `kCMaxWriteRecursionDepth` has it. With no insertion point, a write opens the document first: a script with `src` that runs async or deferred cannot, so its write does nothing and the console says Chrome's warning, `Failed to execute 'write' on 'Document': It isn't possible to write into a document from an asynchronously-loaded external script unless it is explicitly opened.`, while a write from a timer, a listener or the caller's script opens it, while it loads too. A script of another site with `src` and neither `async` nor `defer` that a write inserts gets Chrome's warning of its intervention ([`document_write_intervention.h`](../native/dom/document_write_intervention.h)), `A parser-blocking, cross site (i.e. different eTLD+1) script, ... is invoked via document.write. ...`, once as it is fetched and once more if the write leaves the parser waiting, as Blink's preload scanner of what was written finds it again. Chrome blocks such a script only on a 2G cellular connection, which a desktop never reports, so the Page fetches and runs it as Chrome on Windows does, whatever `navigator.connection.effectiveType` says.
- **`document.open()`** does nothing from a script that the parser runs, and otherwise opens the document anew, as Blink's `Document::open()`: every listener and event handler of its nodes and of its window goes, its children go, and it is loading again, in no-quirks mode until a parser finds no doctype, with a script-created parser that parses what is written at once. The parser that loaded it stops for good: a script that it waited for never runs, and what comes of the body goes unread. The document keeps its URL and its history entry, as it has its window's own. One that a document of another window opens takes that document's URL, such as a frame's about:blank, but for about:srcdoc, and keeps its origin, so the Navigation API keeps its entries, the ones that it makes at that URL among them, as Chrome reads the window's security origin and keeps the origin that committed each entry, and a traversal goes back to the URL that the document had and forward again. `close()` ends the input of a script-created parser, and does nothing for any other, and the document then goes through the end again: `interactive`, `DOMContentLoaded`, `complete` and the window's `load`, for the listeners added since, each before `close()` returns, and the Session hears of each `load`. A document without a window, such as one of `createHTMLDocument()`, is written and opened the same way, but runs no script. A navigation that the page started and that waits for its response stops, with the document's loading, as Blink's `FrameLoader::StopAllLoaders()` has it, so the page stays, opened; one started while the document still loads has already stopped its parser, so `open()` and `write()` then do nothing, as in Chrome. In a `beforeunload`, `pagehide`, `visibilitychange` or `unload` listener of a navigation, `open()` and a write with no insertion point do nothing, as the HTML Standard's ignore-opens-during-unload counter has it. A document that is not an HTML document throws Chrome's `InvalidStateError` for each of the four. The unload of frames and the counter that custom elements raise come with those, in later tickets.
- **Readiness**: `document.readyState` is `loading` while the parser runs, and `interactive` once it has taken all of the body, with `readystatechange`. Then the deferred scripts run, and `DOMContentLoaded` fires at the document in the task of the last, as Blink dispatches it, then a microtask checkpoint, and once no async or in-order script holds it back, `complete`, with `readystatechange`, and `load` at the window with the document as its target, in that task too, or in a task of the DOM manipulation task source once the last script that held it back has run, as Blink's `Document::CheckCompleted()` has it. When the whole body has come while the parser waits for a script, the end begins from a later task than the one that parses the rest, after the timers that the rest set, as Blink's `HTMLDocumentParser::EndIfDelayed()` has it. An image holds `load` back too, from the microtask that starts its load to the task that fires its own `load` or `error`, which checks the document's at once, as Blink's `ImageLoader` and loader hold it, unless it is lazy ([Images](#images)). Each of these events goes through the tree as [Events](#events) says: `DOMContentLoaded` bubbles from the document to the window, and the window's capturing listeners hear `readystatechange` and a script's `load` and `error` too.

### Navigations a page starts

A document navigates its Page's frame as Chrome 157 does, in [`native/dom/frame_loader.cc`](../native/dom/frame_loader.cc), a port of Blink's `LocalFrame::Navigate()` and `FrameLoader::StartNavigation()`, and [`native/page/page_frame.cc`](../native/page/page_frame.cc), which holds the loader of the frame's document and that of the navigation that waits for its response:

- **What starts one.** `location`'s `href` setter, `assign()` and `replace()` resolve their URL against the base URL of the document whose script the task entered, which is not the location's own for a parent that sets its frame's, and the navigation is the caller's, as Blink's `Location` takes the incumbent window, and throw Blink's `SyntaxError`, such as `'http://[/' is not a valid URL.`, with what Blink's URL parser made of it, for one that does not resolve. Its other setters change a part of the document's URL as Blink's `KURL` does, `protocol` with Blink's `'…' is an invalid protocol.`, and a `hash` that is the fragment the URL has navigates nowhere. `reload()` navigates to the document's URL again, and nothing for a `javascript:` one. A link navigates when a click that nothing canceled or handled activates it, as `click()` does, to its `href` resolved against its document's base URL: its `target`, or that of the first `base` element that has one, names the frame for `_self`, `_top`, `_parent` and none, and any other name, `_blank` among them, a new window, which Chrome's pop-up blocker opens only on a click that the user made, so nothing navigates. A Page has no user activation, so no navigation sends `Sec-Fetch-User`.
- **Refresh.** A `Refresh` header of the document's response, when the document commits, and a `meta` element whose `http-equiv` is `refresh`, once it is in the document and again whenever its `content` or `http-equiv` changes there, ask for a refresh, as Blink's `HttpRefreshScheduler` has it ([`native/dom/http_refresh_scheduler.cc`](../native/dom/http_refresh_scheduler.cc)): a delay of digits and full stops, floored to whole seconds, then, after white space or one comma or semicolon, a URL that may follow `url=` and stand in quotes, resolved against the document's base URL, and the document's own URL for none. Content with no delay asks for nothing, and so does a delay longer than 2,147,483 seconds. Of several, the one of the shortest delay wins, and the newest of equal ones. The delay starts once the document's `load` event has finished, and the navigation starts from a task of the loading task source: a reload for the document's own URL, which validates the cache, one within the document for its URL with another fragment, a replace for a delay of at most a second, and a new entry otherwise. A navigation that stops the document's loading drops its refresh. A refresh to a `javascript:` URL is refused with Chrome's console error, such as `Refused to refresh https://example.com/ to a javascript: URL`.
- **`window.open()`.** A target of `_self`, `_top` or `_parent` navigates the Page's frame, unless the URL is empty, and gives the window back, also with `noopener`; with `noreferrer` it still sends the document's `Referer`, where Chrome sends none, a Known Gap until a navigation can say its referrer policy ([#282](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/282)). Any other target, an empty one and `_blank` among them, asks for a new window, which Chrome's pop-up blocker refuses without the user's activation, so `open()` gives `null`, with nothing in the console, as in Chrome. A URL that does not resolve throws Blink's `SyntaxError`, such as `Unable to open a window with invalid URL 'http://[/'.`, with the line feed that Blink ends it with.
- **Which entry.** As Blink decides it for the session history and the Navigation API: a navigation to the document's own URL, one of the initial about:blank, `replace()`, and one that a script starts before the document's `load` event, but for a link, replace the current entry, and `reload()` reloads. The session history and the Navigation API take the entry so, as [Session history](#session-history) says. More than 200 navigations of a frame in 10 seconds, which `location`, the history API and the Navigation API share, are dropped, with Chrome's warning in the console for the first, and each frame counts its own, as Blink's `NavigationRateLimiter` does.
- **Within the document.** A navigation to a URL that differs from the document's only in its fragment commits at once, with no request: the document takes the URL, the Session hears of it, the element that the new fragment indicates becomes the document's target, which `:target` matches, `popstate` fires at the window with a null state, and then `hashchange`, from a task of the DOM manipulation task source, if the fragment changed, with `oldURL` and `newURL`.
- **`javascript:` and `data:` URLs.** A `javascript:` URL's text after the scheme, its escapes decoded as UTF-8, runs as a classic script from a task of the networking task source, whose errors, such as a `SyntaxError`, reach the window's `error` event as they are, and navigates nowhere. A string that it gives would take the document's place in Chrome: a Known Gap. A `data:` URL whose type Chrome shows is refused with Chrome's console error, such as `Not allowed to navigate top frame to data URL: data:,x`, and one of another type, which would be a download, starts nothing.
- **Before the request.** Nothing navigates or traverses a frame while a new document commits in it, as Blink's `FrameNavigationDisabler` has it, so a navigation that one of its frames' unload listeners starts goes nowhere. In the call that starts it, as in Chrome, `beforeunload` fires at the window, a `BeforeUnloadEvent` with the document as its target, if the document has a body, and then at each document of its frames, and of theirs, in tree order, as Blink's `FrameLoader::ShouldClose()` has it. A listener that cancels it, or gives it a `returnValue`, or an `onbeforeunload` handler that returns something but null or undefined, would ask the user to stay, which Chrome does only on a page that the user has activated: the console says ``Blocked attempt to show a 'beforeunload' confirmation panel for a frame that never had a user gesture since its load. https://www.chromestatus.com/feature/5082396709879808``, and the navigation goes on. The window's document opens no document while any of them runs its listeners. Once none has kept the navigation from going on, each of the documents will unload, one with no body yet too, as Blink's `BeforeUnloadDoneWillUnload()` has it, so its `load` never fires, even where the navigation then ends with no document. Then a document that still loads stops: its parser stops where it is, the scripts that it would run do not, the document is `complete`, with `readystatechange`, and its `load` event never fires. No navigation starts while `beforeunload`, `pagehide` or `unload` is dispatched, nor one of the frame whose frames' `beforeunload` is.
- **The request** goes to the Session as a request of Kind `navigation` whose Initiator is the document, from the Persona's lines for a navigation, so it sends what `session.get(url, kind="navigation", initiator=document_url)` sends, but `Sec-Fetch-User`, with the document's URL as `Referer` under Chrome's default referrer policy. A reload validates what the cache holds, so //net sends `Cache-Control: max-age=0` when it goes to the network, and its `Referer` is the document's own URL, as for a client redirect. A navigation the same as the one that waits, to its URL from the same document, origin and referrer with the same history handling, started less than 3 seconds after it while no script wrote `document.cookie`, is dropped, as Chrome's `Navigator::Navigate()` drops it; otherwise a new one takes the place of the one that waits, which ends with `ERR_ABORTED`. The document goes on meanwhile, with its timers and fetches.
- **The response.** A 204, a 205 and a download, a response that `Content-Disposition` says is an attachment or whose MIME type Chrome does not show as `blink::IsSupportedMimeType()` says ([`native/mime_types.cc`](../native/mime_types.cc)), end the navigation with `ERR_ABORTED`, and the document stays, though what happened when it started does not come undone: `beforeunload` fired, and the parser of a document that loaded stopped. As Chrome's network service does, the library holds back a response that `net::ShouldSniffMimeType()` would sniff, such as one of `text/plain` or of no type, unless its first `X-Content-Type-Options` value is `nosniff`, until `net::SniffMimeType()` has decided from the start of its body, its first 1024 bytes have come, or it has ended. A type sniffed that Chrome does not show makes a download of it, so `text/plain`, or no type, with a binary byte such as a NUL among those bytes keeps the document. Any other type sniffed, such as `image/png` for an image's magic number, leaves a document, of the type the response gives. The library reads a download's body to its end and drops it, as Chrome's download manager reads it. A navigation that fails before a response keeps the document too, where Chrome commits its error page: a Known Gap. Any other response takes the document's place: the old document stops loading, then fires `pagehide` at the window, a `PageTransitionEvent` with the document as its target, that bubbles and can be canceled and whose `persisted` is false, as Blink makes it, then, while the page is visible, `visibilitychange` and `webkitvisibilitychange` at the document, whose `visibilityState` is then `hidden`, and `unload` at the window, then a new context's document takes its place and loads as [Loading a document](#loading-a-document) says, with the `Referer` that its request sent last as its `referrer`, and fires `pageshow`, the same kind of event, right after its `load` event. Each new document gets `pagereveal`, a `PageRevealEvent`, at the window at its first rendering opportunity, before that frame's `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks, as Blink's `PageAnimator` fires it. That opportunity waits until the parser inserts the document's body, or parsing ends, as Blink holds back a new document's lifecycle updates until it has something to render, so a frame that a script in the head asks for waits too; its `viewTransition` and its constructor are Known Gaps, as a Page has no view transitions. Leaving the initial about:blank fires the same events there, but before it stops loading, as Blink's frame that has committed no navigation does. An `unload` listener is refused where the Persona's seed deprecates `unload`, as `document.featurePolicy` says, with Chrome's console error `Permissions policy violation: unload is not allowed in this document.` for each, as Chrome refuses one when the permissions policy disallows it.
- **The Navigation API**, as Blink's `NavigationApi` has it ([`native/dom/navigation.cc`](../native/dom/navigation.cc), [`native/dom/navigate_event.cc`](../native/dom/navigate_event.cc)). `navigation.navigate()` and `reload()` navigate as `location` does, with a `NavigateEvent` whose `info` and `destination.getState()` are those they were given, and give `committed` and `finished` promises, both rejected at once with Chrome's error for a URL that does not resolve, a `javascript:` URL, a state that cannot be cloned, a `push` from the initial about:blank, a detached window and a call while `beforeunload`, `pagehide` or `unload` is dispatched. Each navigation that a document starts, but for a `javascript:` URL, fires `navigate` at `navigation` first, before `beforeunload`, as a `NavigateEvent` whose `userInitiated` is false, as a Page has no user activation, and whose `sourceElement` is the link that started it. Canceling it stops the navigation, which fires `navigateerror` with an `AbortError` and rejects the promises of a `navigate()`. `intercept()`, for a URL that differs from the document's only in its path, query and fragment, as the history API allows, makes the navigation one within the document: it commits at once, with no request, then the handlers run, and the navigation ends with `navigatesuccess` once all their promises have fulfilled, or with `navigateerror` at the first that is rejected. A navigation that starts before one has ended aborts it, with its signal. Each commit within the document makes or replaces the current entry and fires `currententrychange`, and `navigation.transition` stands for an intercepted navigation until it ends. A new document keeps the entries of its origin from the document before it, as Chrome's browser gives a renderer the same-origin entries around the current one, with an `activation` of `push`, `replace` or `reload` whose `from` is the old entry: the new entry itself for a reload, and a detached one for a replace. A document of another origin starts with its own entry alone. Chrome 157 gives the entry of the document that a `navigate()` or `reload()` loads none of the state they were given, and neither does the Page. Traversing is as [Session history](#session-history) says. Known Gaps: `NavigationCurrentEntryChangeEvent`'s `navigationType`, a nullable enumeration that the bindings do not write yet, which gives `null`, and its constructor; `NavigateEvent`'s constructor, whose dictionary holds a `FormData`; and `NavigationPrecommitController.redirect()`.
- **Known Gaps**: a page at about:blank, whose origin is opaque, cannot navigate, since its request has no Initiator to give, and a navigation to about:blank or another URL that is not HTTP or HTTPS keeps the document, where Chrome makes a new one; a link's `ping` sends nothing; `referrerpolicy` and `rel=noreferrer` change no `Referer`, and neither does `window.open()`'s `noreferrer`; a link with `download` to a URL that the document can read, which Chrome downloads, starts nothing; so does a navigation to a URL longer than 2 MiB, `url::kMaxURLChars`, which Mojo does not carry, so that Chrome's browser makes it one to about:blank#blocked; and SVG's `a`, which Blink's `SVGAElement` follows on a click, follows nothing. A page's own `MouseEvent` whose button is not the left one still follows a link.
- **Navigation timing.** A reload's document has `performance.navigation.type` 1 and its navigation entry's `type` is `reload`, and a document of the origin of the one before it gets that document's unload times, `unloadEventStart` and `unloadEventEnd`, as Blink's `UnloadEventTimingInfo` carries them.

### Session history

A Page keeps its tab's session history as Chrome's browser keeps it in its `NavigationController`, in [`native/page/session_history.cc`](../native/page/session_history.cc): a list of entries, at most 50, each a tree of frame entries, one for each frame, with the top-level frame's at the root, and the entry that the frames show now. The Page holds every frame of a tab, so the list is the Page's, beside its frames, and `history.length` is its own count, at once, as the renderer's copy of it is in Chrome, but for a `pushState()` from a listener while a traversal within the document fires its events, where Chrome's copy has taken the traversal's place in the list already and counts from there, not from the entry that its browser adds the new one after. A frame entry holds what Blink's `HistoryItem` holds ([`native/dom/history_item.h`](../native/dom/history_item.h)): its URL, the sequence numbers that tell its document and the entry itself apart, the state that `pushState()` or `replaceState()` gave it, the Navigation API's key, ID and state, and its `scrollRestoration`, with the ID of the document whose navigation made it. The root matches the top-level frame by its place, as Chrome's does, and a frame's entry will match its frame by Blink's unique name; until frames have entries of their own, each entry has a root alone: a [frame](#frames)'s `history.length` is the tab's count, its `back()`, `forward()` and `go()` move the tab's history, and its `pushState()` and `replaceState()` change its own document's URL and state, with no entry, a Known Gap of the session history of frames.

- **Entries.** The tab starts with an initial entry at about:blank, which its first navigation replaces. A new document adds an entry after the current one, in place of the entries forward of it, or replaces the current one, as [Navigations a page starts](#navigations-a-page-starts) says which, and a reload replaces it with one of the same key, ID and Navigation API state, as Blink's `SetHistoryItemStateForCommit()` makes them, but for a reload that a redirect takes to another origin, whose entry takes nothing of the old one's, as in Chrome. Past 50, the oldest goes, as each entry before one that a Page's script added is one that Chrome's history intervention skips, a Page having no user activation, and the Navigation API's entries of the documents drop it from a later task, as Chrome's browser tells them.
- **`pushState()` and `replaceState()`** add an entry within the document, or replace the current one, in Blink's order ([`native/dom/history.cc`](../native/dom/history.cc)): the state is serialized as Blink serializes it for storage, with no limit to its size, and a `DataCloneError` for what cannot be cloned; the window must be fully active, or `SecurityError`; the URL, the document's own for none or `""`, and otherwise resolved against the document's base URL, must be one that the history API lets the document take, by its origin, so that a frame's about:blank of its parent's origin keeps its query where a top-level about:blank, whose origin is opaque, may change it, or Blink's `SecurityError`, such as `A history state object with URL 'http://www.example.com/' cannot be created in a document with origin 'http://127.0.0.1:8000' and URL 'http://127.0.0.1:8000/'.`; the rate limit of navigations drops the call; the `navigate` event, of type `push` or `replace`, which may cancel it or intercept it; then the document takes the URL and the entry, the Session hears of the URL, and `currententrychange` fires, with no `popstate` and no `hashchange`. `title` goes nowhere. A new entry takes the current one's `scrollRestoration` and, from a navigation to a fragment that nothing intercepts, its Navigation API state; an intercepted navigation's entry takes none, and the classic state that `pushState()` or `replaceState()` gave, or none, as Blink makes an entry of the history API; `replaceState()` keeps the entry's own sequence number. A `pushState()` or `replaceState()` from an unload listener, as a new document commits, makes no entry and no URL that the Session hears of, as Chrome's browser ignores the document that it replaces. The Session takes the new URL as Chrome's browser does: one that the history API allows, about:blank, or for an origin that is not opaque any URL of it, as `document.open()` gives a document the URL of the one that opens it.
- **`state`** gives the same object each time while the entry keeps the same state, and a new one once it changes, as Blink caches it, and a traversal's `popstate` has that object as its `state`. A traversal gives the entry's state anew, as Blink reads it from the browser for each, not the object that a script changed before it left. **`scrollRestoration`** is the current entry's, which keeps it, and a value that is none of `auto` and `manual` does nothing but warn, as the bindings do for an enumeration. Nothing scrolls without layout.
- **`back()`, `forward()` and `go()`** do nothing for an offset past the entries, and otherwise go from a later task of the DOM manipulation task source, as Chrome's browser answers: the target is worked out when that task runs, from where the history is then, so each `go()` goes from where the one before it went. `go()` with no offset, or 0, reloads the document, as `location.reload()` does. Within the document, a traversal fires `navigate`, of type `traverse`, which a page may cancel, as Chrome lets a script cancel its own traversal of the top-level frame, and then, as Blink's `CommitSameDocumentNavigation()` has it: `hashchange` is queued if the fragment changes, the document takes the entry, its URL and state, the Session hears of the URL, `currententrychange` fires, the promises of `navigation` settle and `navigatesuccess` fires, `popstate` fires with the entry's state, and the fragment indicates the document's target. A traversal within the document leaves a navigation to another document that waits for its response to go on, which commits after it, in place of the entries forward of where the traversal went, as in Chrome. A traversal to an entry of another document goes through the Session, as below.
- **To another document.** Unless the back/forward cache restores the entry's document, as below: the document fires `beforeunload`, which it cannot cancel without the user's activation, and keeps loading until the response commits; then the Session repeats the navigation that the entry holds, with message 280, and the document fires `navigate`, of type `traverse`, without the request waiting for it, as Chrome's browser tells the renderer, which it can neither cancel nor intercept, for an entry of its origin only, by the document's origin, with no destination entry, index -1, for one that its entries leave out, past one of another origin. The Session repeats the navigation as Chrome's browser repeats a `FrameNavigationEntry`'s: the Initiator, the Referer and the user's activation of the navigation that made the entry, whichever document goes back, and for an entry that a navigation within its document made, that document's origin as the Initiator, and its Referer with the document's referrer policy when it made the entry, so `Sec-Fetch-Site` and `Sec-Fetch-User` are the entry's own. The library keeps what each document's navigation went as for that, with the Referer that its last request sent, to which the first request's policy applies again, as Chrome's `FrameNavigationEntry` keeps it, and the last 200 URLs that navigations within the document gave it, from the commit of the document until the Page releases it, which it does not while a traversal that needs it runs or waits. A traversal to anything else goes nowhere, with no request: a Known Gap for an entry whose URL the library let go of, or for one at the about:blank that `document.open()` gave the document, where Chrome makes a new document. A response that a redirect brings, also one that ends at the entry's URL, and a restart for a critical client hint,, leaves nothing of the entry: no state, and a new key, ID and document, as Chrome's browser resets the entry's page state on a redirect, and its URL becomes the entry's. If a listener's new entry pruned the entry meanwhile, the document takes the current entry's place, as in Chrome. The request takes what the Session's cache holds without validating it, as `LOAD_SKIP_CACHE_VALIDATION` does, so a cached page makes no request, and only one that the cache does not hold, such as one of `no-store`, goes to the server. Its response takes the document's place as any navigation's, with the entry's own state, key, ID and `scrollRestoration` before any script runs, no `popstate`, an `activation` of `traverse`, and the navigation type `back_forward` in navigation timing. The other entries of that document are within the new one, as in Chrome. While such a traversal runs its events or waits, the history ignores another traversal, also one that the page asked for before it started, and the page's own navigations, as Chrome's browser does without the user's activation, and the traversal takes the place of a page's navigation that waits.
- **The back/forward cache.** The top-level frame keeps a document that a navigation leaves, as Chrome's `BackForwardCacheImpl` keeps a page, if Chrome would keep it ([`native/page/page_frame.cc`](../native/page/page_frame.cc)): one of a 200 response at an HTTP or HTTPS URL, past `DOMContentLoaded`, whose parser no script stopped while it parsed, with no `unload` listener, which the seed's DeprecateUnload refuses where it covers the host, whose scripts asked neither WebOTP nor the password store, which has no WebAuthn request waiting once it has frozen, and for a response of `no-store`, whose scripts' requests, `fetch()`, `XMLHttpRequest` and the favicon that Chrome's renderer fetches, had no response of `no-store`; and the navigation neither replaces the entry, nor reloads, nor goes to the same URL but for its fragment. The document fires `pagehide`, with `persisted` true, and `visibilitychange`, in place of its unload events, keeps its context, with its own global proxy, while the next document gets a new one, as Chrome's browsing instance swap gives the next page a new frame, what a `pagehide` listener of it does with `pushState()`, `replaceState()` and a fragment stays in the history, as Chrome's does, though a page that is not kept loses it, and it fires `freeze`, in which no request starts but a keepalive one, as the console says, and freezes: its `scheduler` tasks, `MessagePort` messages, Web Locks callbacks, timers, `AbortSignal.timeout()`s and geolocation timeouts, idle callbacks, animation frames, tasks and microtasks wait until it comes back, the microtasks as it thaws, `scheduler` tasks by their priority then, and what was delayed after what its `resume` and `pageshow` listeners post, as Chrome's queues have it, and so do the answers of the `CookieStore`, `navigator.credentials` and every other promise that a task settles, its `unhandledrejection` and `resourcetimingbufferfull` events, and what the Session sends of its requests, which go on, each request's in a task of its own then. A message of a `BroadcastChannel` for it evicts it, as in Chrome, as does anything else that would run in its window directly, and its keepalive requests outlive the eviction. A traversal to the entry it was stored at restores it, with no request, unless its response said `no-store` and a cookie of its URL has changed since it committed, as at Chrome's default level, restore-unless-cookie-change: the current document leaves as at any commit, the kept one thaws, fires `resume`, `visibilitychange`, `pageshow`, with `persisted` true and the traversal's start as its time, and `pagereveal` in its first frame, its Navigation API's entries are the session history's again, with an `activation` of `traverse`, its `window.name` is its own again, and its navigation timing stays that of its load. The caller hears of such a navigation as restored. A traversal to another of its entries, or that takes a document into the browsing instance of its page, lets it go, and a new entry that prunes the one it was stored at leaves its restored document after the entry a listener made, as in Chrome. A navigation, a traversal or a change of the history that a document asks for once Chrome's browser has it in the cache, from a `freeze` listener, or from any of its last listeners as a restore takes its place, which the browser has committed by then, goes nowhere and lets the document go, as `IsInactiveAndDisallowActivation()` evicts a page. It goes with no event after 600 seconds, 180 for a response of `no-store`, past six kept documents, the oldest first, once something would run in it, once a request of it, a keepalive one too, has been open for 60 seconds while it is kept, and once more than 1,024,000 bytes of bodies wait, of all the kept documents, for the one whose body came last. The Performance Timeline has a `visibility-state` entry for each time that it hides and shows. A restored document's Cache API, IndexedDB and Storage Buckets answer it again. Known Gaps for later: a document with frames or dedicated workers, or one that used the Cache API, IndexedDB, Storage Buckets or a `CookieStore` change listener, which the Session keeps only for the current document, unloads instead, as does one that waits for `navigator.storage.estimate()`; a navigation to about:blank or a `data:` URL, which commits without the network, unloads the old document; a navigation to another document, or a traversal, that a `pagehide` listener of a document that the cache takes starts goes nowhere, where Chrome's goes on; the library counts cookie changes from the commit, where Chrome counts them from the request's start; every cookie change counts, as 157's own default has it, where the seed puts each installation in a group of `BackForwardCacheCCNSIgnoreUnchangedCookies`, whose launched groups count no change that leaves the cookie as it was; a `FinalizationRegistry`'s callback may run while its document is kept, where Chrome evicts it; a browsing instance that `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` alone swaps, after a document that the cache would not keep, counts as that document's; and the cache evicts no document for a redirect of its request, nor for Web Locks or IndexedDB that another context waits for.
- **The Navigation API** builds a new document's entries from the session history: those of the top-level frame of its origin right before and after its own, each entry once, and the entry it came from, if it is of its origin, as Chrome's browser gives a renderer `NavigationApiHistoryEntryArrays`. An entry made under a referrer policy of `no-referrer` or `origin` has no URL in the other documents' arrays, as Chrome's `protect_url_in_navigation_api` keeps it from them: a new document's entry and the entries it makes take the response's policy, and a meta element's policy changes only the entry that the document is at. The document then changes them itself for each commit within it. `traverseTo()`, `back()` and `forward()` go to an entry as `go()` does, the nearest of its key before the entry that the history counts from, the one a traversal goes to while one waits or runs its `navigate` event, then after it, within the origin, as Chrome's controller finds it, with promises that settle as a navigation's do, Chrome's `InvalidStateError` for a key that no entry has (`Invalid key`) and at either end (`Cannot go back`, `Cannot go forward`), the current entry's promises fulfilled at once, and the same promises for a second call to an entry that waits. `updateCurrentEntry()` sets the state of the entry that was current when it was called, which the session history keeps if that entry is still the current one once the state is serialized, and fires `currententrychange`, as Blink's `SetAndSaveState()` does. An entry that goes, forward of a new one, replaced or dropped past 50, gets its `dispose` event.

### Frames

An `iframe` or `frame` element holds a frame of the Page, as Chrome 157 has it, in the Page's process ([ADR-0009](adr/0009-a-process-for-each-page.md)): Blink's `HTMLFrameOwnerElement` and `HTMLFrameElementBase` in [`native/dom/html_frame_owner_element.cc`](../native/dom/html_frame_owner_element.cc), and the frame itself in [`native/page/page_frame.cc`](../native/page/page_frame.cc), which holds its window's context as the top-level frame holds its own ([Inside the Page program](#inside-the-page-program)).

- **Its first document.** Once the element is in a document that has a frame, it makes a child of that frame, named by its `name` attribute, whose first window has an initial about:blank of the parent document's origin, in a context of its own with the bindings of a top-level window, as Blink's `LocalFrameClient::CreateFrame()` makes one. Its base URL is the parent document's at that time, the HTML Standard's about base URL, until a `base` element of its own says otherwise. The Session hears of the document under its parent's, so it works out its origin, cookies and storage from the parent's, as Chrome's browser does ([The documents of a Page](#the-documents-of-a-page)). The element then navigates the frame to its `src`, or to about:blank for none.
- **Its navigations.** An element's `src`, `srcdoc` and their changes navigate its frame through Blink's `FrameLoader` ([Navigations a page starts](#navigations-a-page-starts)) from the parent's window, as `HTMLFrameOwnerElement::LoadOrRedirectSubframe()` does: a URL that differs from the document's only in its fragment commits within the document, with no load event, and a `javascript:` URL runs in the frame when the document that starts it is of the frame's origin. `srcdoc` wins over `src`, and taking it away with no `src` goes to about:blank. A srcdoc, about:srcdoc, and an about:blank go through Chrome's browser, so they commit from a later task, the newest in place of one that waits, whatever that newest one's URL, and a `document.open()` of the frame's document cancels one that waits, as `FrameLoader::StopAllLoaders()` cancels the browser's navigation: a srcdoc of the parent's origin, embedder policy and referrer policy, and of the cookie URL that the parent has as it commits, with the parent's URL as its requests' `Referer` and the parent's base URL as its own, in no-quirks mode whatever its doctype, from the markup that an `iframe`'s `srcdoc` has then, where a `frame`'s srcdoc document is empty, as in Chrome; an about:blank of the origin, embedder policy, referrer policy and cookie URL of the document that started it, which a `location` setter's caller is, with that document's base URL as its own and no `Referer` for its requests, whatever their referrer policy, but for a `referrer` that `fetch()` names. Each one's `document.referrer` is what the starting document's URL gives a navigation to about:srcdoc or about:blank under the element's `referrerpolicy`, or else that document's referrer policy, as Blink's `SecurityPolicy::GenerateReferrer()` gives it, so its origin by default, and none from an HTTPS document. What an about:blank takes of the document that started it is what that document had when the navigation started, as Chrome's browser takes it then, so it commits though that document goes first. A listener of the old document's unload events that removes the element has the frame go, and the navigation commits nothing, and while the frame's document commits, no script of the old document or of its frames starts a navigation of it, as Blink's `FrameNavigationDisabler` has it. A new window's time origin is the navigation's start, before the old document's unload events. Each of these documents, and the about:blank that commits at once, has the navigation's entry in its Performance Timeline, as Chrome's has: its URL, with no fetch, delivered from the cache, with its srcdoc's bytes as its body, and its end when it committed. A frame keeps its window for the first document of its initial about:blank's origin, as Blink's `DocumentLoader` reuses it, and gets a new one in a new context for any other, while its WindowProxy stays: `contentWindow` and every reference to it give the new window, as Blink's `LocalWindowProxy` reinitializes on the same global proxy. A reused window keeps its `performance.timeOrigin`, while the new document's `performance.timing` starts again and its navigation entry is the new one, as in Chrome. A frame that has committed its about:blank at once gets a new window for its next document, as in Chrome. The parent's `load` waits for a frame's navigation that waits to commit.
- **Through the Session.** A frame's navigation to an HTTP or HTTPS URL is a request of kind iframe (11) that names the frame's document that the new one takes the place of, and the document that started it, its initiator, as Chrome's browser makes a `NavigationRequest` of it: a `frame` element's too, until the Session has Baselines of destination `frame`. Its response makes a document as a top-level navigation's does, with its sniffing, and none of a 204, a 205 or a download, where the frame keeps its document and neither the frame's element nor the parent fires `load` for it, as in Chrome. One that fails without a response commits Chrome's error page, chrome-error://chromewebdata/, of an opaque origin, whose `load` the parent waits for. A frame's first document of its initial about:blank's origin keeps the about:blank's window, as Blink's does, but the Session forgets the about:blank: the fetches it still waits for fail, keepalive ones too, whose requests go on, as Chrome's do, and its dedicated workers are terminated, a `Cache` or storage bucket that it opened rejects each call with an `InvalidStateError`, and its IndexedDB connections hear nothing more, where Chrome keeps them all working with the window. A navigation to the URL that two of the frame's ancestors' documents have, but for the fragment, ends before its request, or at the redirect that takes it there, and the frame keeps its document, as Chrome's `NavigationRequest::IsSelfReferentialURL()` has it, so a document that embeds its own URL is in itself once and no more. The `navigate` event fires at the frame's document only where the document that starts the navigation can reach it, as Blink's `FrameLoader::StartNavigation()` has it, so a frame's document of another origin hears nothing of its parent's. Only the top-level frame's navigations reach the caller's navigation events, and only its COOP and COEP make the Page's agent cluster. A `Location` that a script navigates takes the URL against the document of the entered window and names the incumbent window's document as the initiator, as Blink's `Location::SetLocation()` does, so a parent that navigates its frame sends its own URL as the `Referer`. A frame's document has no favicon.
- **`data:` URLs.** A frame's navigation to a `data:` URL, from its element or a `Location`, is one that Chrome's browser answers itself, as `DataURLLoaderFactory` does, and so does the Page: from a later task, through the same `DocumentLoader` as a response, with no request. Its document has an opaque origin and is no secure context, its referrer is what the initiator's outgoing referrer gives a navigation to its URL under the navigation's referrer policy, as for a srcdoc, it takes the initiator's referrer policy, whether a header gave the initiator a Content-Security-Policy, which hides its elements' nonces, and, in the library, its embedder policy, as Chrome gives a local URL's document the initiator's policy container, and `navigator.connection` reads the network quality of no host, `document.cookie` throws "Cookies are disabled inside 'data:' URLs.", and its requests and navigations go through the Session as an opaque origin's, as Chrome's do. A `data:` URL that does not parse commits the error page, and one whose MIME type Chrome would download, `multipart/related` and `message/rfc822` among them, as `download_utils::MustDownload()` has it, commits nothing and fires no `load`, while the frame loads until its task. Only the top-level frame refuses a `data:` URL that a page navigates it to.
- **Sandboxes.** An `iframe`'s `sandbox` attribute is parsed as Chrome's `ParseWebSandboxPolicy()` parses it ([`native/dom/sandbox_flags.h`](../native/dom/sandbox_flags.h)), and what it withholds holds for the documents that the frame navigates to from then on, as it was when each navigation started, with what the parent's document has withheld from it, the frame's initial about:blank too. Without `allow-same-origin` a document has an opaque origin, made for the one that it would have: the Page gives it no cookies, storage or workers of its URL's origin, with Chrome's errors, which name the sandbox, and its window reaches no other document, not even that of its own frames, and the library, which hears of it with message 70 and holds each document under it to the same, sends its requests as an opaque origin's, with no cookies, `Origin: null` where Chrome sends one, and no `Referer` but a module's. `isSecureContext` is what its URL would have. For messages between frames it is in a process for sandboxed documents of its origin, as Chrome's `kIsolateSandboxedIframes` has it, but for an about:blank ([`native/dom/remote_window.h`](../native/dom/remote_window.h)). A `javascript:` URL does nothing in a frame whose element withholds its origin now, whatever its document has. Without `allow-scripts` no script element, event handler attribute, timer or `javascript:` URL of it runs, each with Chrome's console message, its markup is parsed with scripting disabled, so a `noscript` holds elements, the media feature `scripting` is `none` there, a `meta` refresh does nothing, and what a script of another document does there runs, as Chrome's `CanExecuteScripts()` has it. An unknown token, and a sandbox that its document can take away, say so in the console as in Chrome. Known Gaps: the other flags, the `sandbox` directive of a response's `Content-Security-Policy`, a `blob:` worker of an opaque origin, and a callback or an `import()` of a script that another document runs in one without scripts with its window's `eval()`, which Chrome does not run.
- **about:blank at once.** The first navigation of a frame's initial about:blank to about:blank commits before it returns, as Chrome's `RenderFrameImpl::SynchronouslyCommitAboutBlankForBug778318()` does: a new about:blank document of the same origin takes the place of the initial one in the same window, with the parent document's URL as its referrer and the initial document's base URL, loads with no body, and fires `DOMContentLoaded`, the window's `load` and the element's `load` during the insertion that made the frame. Chrome keeps the initial document's token for it, so the Session knows it by the same ID.
- **The tree.** `frames`, `length`, `parent`, `top` and `frameElement` give the window's place in the tree, as Blink's `DOMWindow` gives it, `window[i]` the window of the frame's `i`th child, in the order they were made, as a read-only, enumerable own property of the window, and `window[name]` a child frame of that name, before the document's named elements, unless the child's document is of another origin and the name is not its element's `name` attribute. `name` is the browsing context's name, which a script sets and which an element's later `name` attribute leaves alone, as in Chrome. An element's `contentWindow` gives its frame's window, and `contentDocument` its document to a caller of the document's origin and `null` to another, as `[CheckSecurity=ReturnValue]` checks it. `open()`, `write()` and `close()` of a frame's document from another window of its origin write into it, and throw Chrome's `SecurityError` from one of another origin.
- **Realms.** Each frame's window has its own interfaces and builtins, so `frame.contentDocument instanceof Document` is false, as in Chrome, and what a frame's function throws in another frame's `requestAnimationFrame()`, timer or mutation observer fires the error event of its own window, as Blink's `InvokeAndReportException()` reports it.
- **Load.** A document's load event waits for the frames under it that still load, as Blink's `AllDescendantsAreComplete()` has it, and an element's `load` fires once its frame's window's `load` has, before `pageshow`. A frame's initial about:blank holds nothing back, as Blink's `FrameLoader::Init()` suppresses its finish, unless a script opens it with `document.open()`. A frame that held its parent's load event back no longer does once it goes, and the parent checks again from a task. Each frame that loads or stops has its parent check again, and so on up, whether or not the parent has loaded, as Blink's `FrameLoader::DidFinishNavigation()` has it, so a frame that a loaded frame gains holds back an ancestor that still loads until it ends.
- **Storage.** Each frame's Cache API, IndexedDB and Storage Buckets ask the Session as the frame's own document, as each of Blink's frames has its own remotes, so the Session closes what a frame opened when the frame goes. What a removed frame's scripts made of them stays callable, and the Session hears nothing more of it.
- **Going.** A frame whose element leaves its document goes: its document gets `pagehide` and `visibilitychange`, then `unload` as a top-level document does, its frames go with it, and its window loses its frame, so that `closed` is true, `parent`, `top` and `frameElement` are `null` and its timers set nothing, and the Session forgets its document. A listener of those events that removes the frame's element again, or an ancestor's, has the frame go then, with its own frames, and nothing more fires there, as Blink's frame does. So does a timer, an animation frame or idle callback or a fetch's handler of the frame that removes it: it returns, and no other timer, callback or response of the frame runs, not even one that waits for the same animation frame. A frame's documents go with their parent's document when the parent navigates. `moveBefore()` keeps a frame, whether it moves the element or an ancestor of it.
- **Known Gaps**, for later pull requests of [#285](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/285): a frame's navigation to a `blob:` URL, which leaves it at its document, the entry of a frame's navigation in its parent's Performance Timeline, what a sandbox withholds beside a document's origin and its scripts, such as modal dialogs, `document.domain` and navigations of other frames, `allow`, `loading`, `X-Frame-Options` and `frame-ancestors`, the session history of frames, a frame's initial about:blank, which has no navigation entry in its Performance Timeline where Chrome's has one with no name, and whether `unload` fires in a frame that goes, which Chrome decides by the seed's deprecation of `unload` and the frame's permissions policy, and which no recording of Chrome with a Persona's seed has shown yet. A frame of another origin reaches no other frame's objects, and none reaches its own, until [#286](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/286). An HTML document in an `object` or `embed` element is a Known Gap for now: the element holds no frame, where Chrome makes one for it and loads the document there.

### Cross-origin isolation

A document is cross-origin isolated as Chrome isolates it, in [`native/cross_origin_policies.h`](../native/cross_origin_policies.h) on the Session's side, a port of what Chrome's network service parses and what its browser makes of it:

- **What the Session parses.** `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy` of a top-level navigation's response, with their report-only forms, as structured headers, as the network service's parsers read them: a value that is no token, two lines of one header, or a token that Chrome does not know gives `unsafe-none`. The embedder policy counts only at a potentially trustworthy URL, such as one of HTTPS, `localhost` or `127.0.0.1`, as the HTML Standard has it for a secure context. So does the opener policy, and a response that has one elsewhere, a redirect's or the last, puts Chrome's console error on the document that the navigation makes, `The Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy header has been ignored, because the URL's origin was untrustworthy. ...`, once for each such response.
- **What isolates a document.** `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` with an embedder policy of `require-corp` or `credentialless`, which Chrome's browser calls `same-origin-plus-COEP`. The Session tells the Page so before the response starts (message 300), and the Page makes the new window's context in an agent that is isolated, as Blink takes the agent cluster from the browser: per document, so a Page whose isolated document navigates to one that is not isolated keeps its process, where Chrome would swap processes, and the new document is not isolated.
- **What it gives.** Clocks of 5 microseconds rather than 100 ([Clocks](#clocks)), `performance.measureUserAgentSpecificMemory()` on a window, whose behaviour is a Known Gap, as Blink installs a `[CrossOriginIsolatedOrRuntimeEnabled]` member for each context, `crossOriginIsolated` is true, `SharedArrayBuffer` is on the global object, which V8 adds once the context has everything else, as Blink installs it with the context's conditional features, and a `SharedArrayBuffer` goes in a message to the window, a dedicated worker or a port, sharing its memory with the copy ([Structured clone and messaging](#structured-clone-and-messaging)). `Atomics.waitAsync()` waits on such memory, in windows and workers, and `Atomics.wait()` stays refused on the main thread, as in Chrome. A buffer serialized for storage, as IndexedDB serializes it, throws `A SharedArrayBuffer can not be serialized for storage.` whether the context is isolated or not.
- **Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy.** Each response and redirect of a Page's request of mode no-cors, such as a script's, an image's or a `fetch()` that asks for it, is held to its `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` as Chrome's network service holds it ([`native/cross_origin_resource_policy.h`](../native/cross_origin_resource_policy.h)), whatever the embedder policy: `same-origin` lets only the origin of the document or worker that asks have it, and `same-site` only its site, as Chrome 157 compares registrable domains, so that two hosts with none, such as `localhost` and `intranet`, count as one site, while a host that is an IP address has none beyond its origin, nor an HTTP origin of an HTTPS response. Under an embedder policy of `require-corp`, a response with no policy, or one that is not valid, is held to `same-origin`, and under `credentialless` too when the request sent credentials. One that fails ends the request with `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_RESPONSE` before anything of it reaches the Page, so a `fetch()` rejects with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` and an element fires `error`. A request of mode cors or a navigation is not held to it. The HTTP client's own requests are not either, as they are not held to CORS.
- **Credentialless.** A document or worker whose embedder policy is `credentialless` sends and keeps no credentials on a request of mode no-cors to another origin, from the hop that goes there on, and takes nothing of it from the HTTP cache, as Chrome's `WebPoliciesAllowCredentials()` has it.
- **Dedicated workers.** A worker takes its creator's isolation, but one of a `data:` URL, which never is, as Blink's `DedicatedWorkerGlobalScope` has it. Such a worker is still of its creator's agent cluster, so a `SharedArrayBuffer` that its creator posts it, directly or through a port, shares its memory, as in Chrome. Its embedder policy is its script response's, or its creator's for a `blob:` or `data:` URL, and a worker of a creator whose policy is `require-corp` or `credentialless` whose script's policy is neither fails to load, as Chrome's `DedicatedWorkerHost::CheckCOEP()` fails it, which fires a plain `error` event at the `Worker`.
- **Frames.** A frame's navigation is held to its parent's embedder policy, as Chrome's browser holds it in `NavigationRequest`: under a parent whose policy is `require-corp` or `credentialless`, a response whose own policy is neither, or a response or redirect of another origin than the parent's whose `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` does not let the parent have it (`cross-origin`, or `same-site` for one of the parent's site), which mode navigate needs under either policy, ends the navigation with `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_RESPONSE`, and the frame commits Chrome's error page. A `blob:` or `data:` URL is not held to `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`. A frame's document takes its response's embedder policy where the top-level document's URL is potentially trustworthy, and none elsewhere, an initial about:blank and a srcdoc take their parent's, and an about:blank and a `data:` URL's document their initiator's, the document that started the navigation. One of those two whose initiator's policy is not `require-corp` or `credentialless` is blocked under a parent whose policy is, as a response is, though a `data:` URL that would be a download stays one. It is cross-origin isolated where its parent's document is and it is of its parent's origin, the default allowlist `'self'` of the `cross-origin-isolated` feature, so a same-origin frame of an isolated page, a nested one and a srcdoc have `crossOriginIsolated` and `SharedArrayBuffer`, and a frame of another origin, a same-origin frame under one, a `data:` URL's document, Chrome's error page, an iframe whose `sandbox`, as its navigation started, withholds `allow-same-origin`, or a frame whose response's `Content-Security-Policy` sandboxes it so does not, as Chrome gives the last four an opaque origin. The Session decides it for a frame's navigation and tells the Page so before the response starts (message 300), and the Page works it out the same way for what a frame makes without the network.

[`tests/test_page_isolation.py`](../tests/test_page_isolation.py) holds a Page to what Chrome 157 gave for the same pages: which headers isolate a page and which do not, the console error, workers, shared memory and `Atomics.waitAsync()`. [`tests/test_page_resource_policy.py`](../tests/test_page_resource_policy.py) holds it to Chrome's outcomes for `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` and credentialless requests.

Known Gaps, which [#377](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/377) closes: the Cache API does not hold an opaque response that it matches to its `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` for a context with an embedder policy, as Chrome's browser does, and under `credentialless` the check also reads whether the response's request sent credentials, which the cache does not store, and carrying that would change message 13 and what older storage directories hold; a frame's `allow` attribute and a response's `Permissions-Policy` for the `cross-origin-isolated` feature, a top-level document's included, are not read until [#285](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/285) brings the container policy, so a frame has the feature's default allowlist `'self'` and a top-level document always has the feature; a frame's `credentialless` attribute is not read; `Document-Isolation-Policy`, which isolates a document by itself in Chrome 157, is not read. A violation's report goes to no endpoint, as the roadmap leaves out Reporting, and to no `ReportingObserver`, which the Runtime does not have. The opener that `same-origin` cuts is [#378](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/378)'s, with the windows that a page opens.

### fetch()

A page's `fetch()` goes through the Session as a request of Kind `fetch`, with the document as its Initiator, on the same path as the document's scripts, in [`native/dom/fetch_loader.cc`](../native/dom/fetch_loader.cc) and [`native/page/page_fetcher.cc`](../native/page/page_fetcher.cc) on the Page's side:

- **What it sends.** `Request`'s constructor checks the URL, the method and the header lines as Blink's does, with Chrome's messages, normalizes the method and drops each forbidden request header, such as `Cookie`, as its `request` guard does. A string body adds `Content-Type: text/plain;charset=UTF-8`, an `ArrayBuffer` or a view goes as its bytes, a `Blob` as its bytes, with its type, if it has one, as the `Content-Type` ([The File API](#the-file-api)), a `URLSearchParams` as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded;charset=UTF-8`, and a `FormData` as `multipart/form-data` with a boundary of `----WebKitFormBoundary` and 16 random characters, its names and file names quoted with their quotes and line breaks percent-encoded, its strings' line breaks as CRLF and a file without a type as `application/octet-stream`, as Blink's `FormDataEncoder` writes it ([`form_data.h`](../native/dom/form_data.h)). A `FormData` body with a file in it reads as a blob, after a task, and one without as its bytes at once, as Blink's `FormDataBytesConsumer` reads them. `fetch()` takes the body of the `Request` it makes, as Blink's `PassRequestData()` does, so a `Request` that `fetch()` was given, or that another was made from, has a used body.
