A stream as a body needs `duplex: 'half'`, never goes with `keepalive` in the same `RequestInit`, though a `Request` that holds one may be fetched with it, as in Chrome, and makes a `Request` of mode `cors` or `same-origin` only, with Chrome's messages, which then goes as Blink's mode `cors-with-forced-preflight` does: to another origin only after a CORS preflight, whatever its method and lines, even from mode `same-origin`. `fetch()` sends such a body as the Session reads it, a chunked upload, through a reader of its own that locks the stream at once, as Blink's `BytesUploader` does ([`bytes_uploader.h`](../native/dom/bytes_uploader.h)): over HTTP/2 and HTTP/3, as Chrome does, and over HTTP/1.1 it fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` before anything goes, with the stream never read. The Page reads the stream from when `//net` first reads the body, where Chrome's network service asks the renderer to start, and keeps at most 64 KiB ahead of what the library has handed to `//net`, as messages of types 160 to 162 carry it. A chunk that is not a `Uint8Array`, a stream that errors, a redirect other than a 303, a challenge for credentials, a server's or a proxy's, and, for a keepalive fetch, its document going before the library has all of the stream, fail the request, as in Chrome, and an abort cancels the stream with the signal's reason, as Blink's `FetchManager::Loader::Abort()` does. The library starts the request from the Persona's `fetch` lines, or from those of a fetch with a body, whose `Content-Type` stands where the Persona puts it, and the script's lines go among the Persona's in the order that Blink's `HTTPHeaderMap` gives them, with the names as the script wrote them ([parity.md](parity.md#the-order-of-a-subresources-lines)), as a caller's do on the kind `fetch`. So `fetch(url)` from a page sends what `session.get(url, kind="fetch", initiator=page_url)` sends, and the Session's cookies go with a same-origin one.
- **What comes back.** `fetch()` settles once the response starts, with a `Response` whose status, reason phrase, URL, `redirected` and headers are the response's, never with a `Set-Cookie`, or rejects with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` when the request fails first. Its `body` is a byte stream over what the Session hands over, as Blink's `BodyStreamBuffer` is over the data pipe of the network service ([`body_stream_buffer.h`](../native/dom/body_stream_buffer.h)), an empty one for a HEAD request or a null body status, as in Chrome, and null for an opaque response; a read of it gives the bytes that have come, a BYOB read as many as fit. `text()`, `json()`, `arrayBuffer()`, `bytes()`, `blob()`, `formData()` and `textStream()` lock and disturb it, as Blink's `Body` does ([`body_mixin.h`](../native/dom/body_mixin.h)), with Chrome's errors for a body that is locked or used: all but the last wait for the whole body, `blob()` giving a `Blob` of the body's `Content-Type` in lowercase, as Blink's `ExtractMIMEType()` gives it, decode it as UTF-8 with a byte order mark dropped, parse JSON with V8's `JSON.parse()`, so its `SyntaxError`s are Chrome's, and settle from a later networking task, as Blink's `ResolveLater()` does. A body that fails after the response started errors its stream with Chrome's `TypeError: network error`, and rejects them with `TypeError: Failed to fetch`. A `Response` or a `Request` that a script makes has a body of its bytes, or of the stream it was given, whose chunks must be `Uint8Array`s.
- **Credit.** The body arrives in pieces of up to 64 KiB, and the Page gives the Session credit for each as it is read from the body's consumer, as reading Chrome's data pipe makes room in it ([`body_loader_bytes_consumer.h`](../native/dom/body_loader_bytes_consumer.h)). Blink's `BufferingBytesConsumer` reads a fetch's body 50 ms after it starts, or at its first read, and holds up to 55 MiB of it ([`buffering_bytes_consumer.h`](../native/dom/buffering_bytes_consumer.h)), so a body that no script reads goes on coming, as in Chrome, unless the response has `Cache-Control: no-store`, whose body Blink does not buffer: then it holds the server back, as a full Mojo data pipe does in Chrome. A body that the garbage collector takes, or that a script cancels, cancels the rest of its request.
- **Another origin.** A cross-origin `fetch()` has no credentials, as a page's does by default. Once any hop of a request in mode `cors` goes to another origin than the document's, a fetch's or a script's with `crossorigin`, its response tainting is cors, and each response from then on, redirects included, has to pass the CORS check in the library before the redirect is followed or any of the response goes to the Page, as Chrome's network service does: `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` must be `*` or the document's origin, or `null` once a redirect from one other origin to another has tainted the request's origin, and for a request with credentials it must name the origin, with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`. A response that fails fails the fetch. The library tells the Page the tainting, so the `Response`'s type is then `cors`, also after a redirect back to the document's origin, and its scripts read only the CORS-safelisted header names and those that `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` names, where `*` names every one for a request without credentials and only itself for one with them. A request that needs a CORS preflight, such as one with a header line that is not safelisted, goes after the library's preflight, if that allows it, and otherwise fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` and sends nothing more to the other origin ([persona.md](persona.md#a-cors-preflight)). One that a redirect takes to another origin waits there for a preflight, if it needs one, as in Chrome.
- **`RequestInit`'s members** that change the request go to the Session with it, as Blink's `Request` takes them: `credentials`, `"omit"`, `"same-origin"` or `"include"`, which says whether the Session's cookies go, as `credentials=` does for a caller; `mode`, where `"no-cors"` takes only a CORS-safelisted method, or throws Chrome's `TypeError`, and only the no-CORS-safelisted header lines, dropping the rest as the `request-no-cors` guard does, the `Content-Type` of its body too, such as a Blob's of type `application/json`, needs no preflight, and settles with an opaque `Response` from another origin: type `opaque`, status 0, no URL, no header lines and a null body, whose bytes stay untaken, as in a data pipe that nothing drains; `referrer`, where the empty string sends none, a URL of the document's origin is sent as the referrer, and any other, or `about:client`, leaves the document's URL, and a URL that does not parse throws Chrome's `TypeError`; `referrerPolicy`, which the library applies to the referrer as Chrome's network service does; `priority`, where `"low"` sends the request at `LOWEST` and `"high"` at the Kind's own, as Blink's `ResourceFetcher` moves a fetch's priority; and `keepalive`.
- **`cache`, `redirect` and `integrity`.** The cache mode goes to the Session, which gives the request //net's load flags as Blink does, so `no-store` and `reload` send `Pragma: no-cache` and `Cache-Control: no-cache` and go past the cache, `no-cache` sends `Cache-Control: max-age=0`, `force-cache` takes what the cache holds however old, and `only-if-cached`, which needs mode `same-origin`, with Chrome's `TypeError`, fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` when the cache holds nothing. A window's fetch of mode `default` with a conditional line, `If-Match`, `If-Modified-Since`, `If-None-Match`, `If-Range` or `If-Unmodified-Since`, validates the cache as `no-cache` does, as Blink's `FrameFetchContext` has it, so it sends `Cache-Control: max-age=0`, or the script's own `Cache-Control` in its place, and its preflight sends `Cache-Control: max-age=0`; a worker's does not. A redirect mode of `error` or `manual` stops the Session at the first redirect: `error` fails the fetch, and `manual` gives an opaque-redirect filtered response, of type `opaqueredirect`, status 0, no header lines and a null body but the URL that redirected, with the redirect behind it, as Blink's `WillFollowRedirect()` has them. A fetch with integrity metadata waits for the whole body, from a later networking task, checks it with the Page's Subresource Integrity, and then settles with a `Response` of that body, or fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` and Blink's console errors, as Blink's `IntegrityVerifier` does.
- **`keepalive`.** A keepalive fetch outlives its document: when the document goes, the Page lets the request go without cancelling it, and the Session, once the next document commits, finishes it with no client, still holding it to the CORS check, and reads and drops its response, as Chrome's `KeepAliveURLLoader` finishes one in the browser after its renderer has gone. A worker's ends with the worker, as every request of it does, once its thread has ended and the Session has sent the request, so that what a worker asks for just before it closes is still sent, as in Chrome, whose end of a worker's loaders reaches its network service only after the worker's teardown and a hop between processes. Such a request follows no redirect and gives the Page nothing more, still counts among the Page's requests and keepalive requests, and ends with the Page; one whose body streams, or that waits for its preflight, ends at once, and one that the Session has not sent within a second ends then, as does one over HTTP/3 that has had no response, since a QUIC stream does not say when it has written what it took. Chrome's end comes once the teardown reaches the network service, whatever the request's state, so over a slow first connection, such as a cold TLS handshake, Chrome may end the request before it writes it, where the Session sends it. A Page that closes lets its keepalive requests go the same way. The bodies of a document's keepalive requests in flight may hold 64 KiB in all, as Blink's `ResourceFetcher` allows, and one more fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` before it goes. The Session allows 2,048 keepalive requests in flight, 256 of a Page and 512 KiB of their URLs and header lines, as Chrome's network service allows them a top-level frame, and one more fails with `ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES`.
- **`sendBeacon()`** sends a keepalive POST of the Request Kind `beacon`, with the Session's cookies, from the window's document, as Blink's `PingLoader` does: a string as `text/plain;charset=UTF-8`, an `ArrayBuffer` or a view as its bytes with no `Content-Type`, a `Blob` as its bytes with its type if that parses, a `URLSearchParams` and a `FormData` as `fetch()` sends them, and nothing as an empty body with no `Content-Type`, as Blink's `BeaconData` writes each. It goes in mode `no-cors`, but for a `Blob` of a type that is not CORS-safelisted, such as `application/json`, which goes in mode `cors`, after a preflight at another origin; a safelisted type goes in mode `no-cors` whatever its length, as Blink's `BeaconBlob` checks it, where a script's line of more than 128 bytes would need a preflight. It counts among the document's keepalive bodies, and returns `false` for one past their 64 KiB, and for a detached window, before it reads its data, so a stream gives `false` there too, and `true` for the rest, whatever the response, which nothing reads. A URL that does not parse, one that is not HTTP or HTTPS and a `ReadableStream` throw Chrome's `TypeError`s ([`navigator.cc`](../native/dom/navigator.cc)). So `navigator.sendBeacon(url, 'text')` sends what `session.post(url, kind="beacon", initiator=page_url, data="text")` sends. Chrome checks its URL against the document's `connect-src`, which a Page does not, as for `fetch()`.
- **`fetchLater()`** makes a keepalive fetch that the Session holds, as Chrome's browser holds a `FetchLaterLoader`'s: it sends the request once `activateAfter` has passed, from a task of the window's event loop, when its document goes, as when another takes its place or its frame goes, when the back/forward cache takes its document, as Chrome 157's `send_on_enter_bfcache` has it, once the next page has been asked for, or when its Page ends, and drops it when its signal aborts or its Session closes. Its `FetchLaterResult` says `activated` once it has gone, which for a document that goes is at once, before any script runs again, as `FetchLaterManager::ContextDestroyed()` processes each, so an abort after that changes nothing, and nothing of its response reaches the page, which has no entry of it in the Performance Timeline. A window that the Page has let go, such as a detached frame's whose `fetchLater()` a script kept, throws `TypeError: The global scope is shutting down.` before the `Request` takes its input, as Blink's `ScopedFetcher` does. It checks what Blink's `FetchLaterManager` checks, in its order and with its errors: an aborted signal, a negative `activateAfter`, a window that is not fully active, a URL that is not HTTP or HTTPS, or not potentially trustworthy, and a body of a stream. Its quota is that of its deferred-fetch control document, as Blink's `FetchLaterUtil` works it out: the top-level document's for a frame of its origin, or of a frame of its origin, and otherwise the frame's own. A top-level document has 512 KiB, less what each frame's element under it reserves, and a frame of another origin than its parent's has the 8 KiB that its element reserved, as the element of a frame of another origin does under a top-level control document, at most 16 of them at once, or nothing, so one under a frame of another site has none. The element reserves it as its parent navigates the frame, for the URL's origin, an opaque one under a sandbox that withholds the origin, and lets it go once the frame has a document of its parent's origin. A Page takes no `Permissions-Policy` header and no `allow` attribute, so `deferred-fetch` is in a document of the top-level document's origin through each of its frames, and `deferred-fetch-minimal` in every one, as their default allowlists have them. Of that quota, a request may take at most 64 KiB for each origin, less what the requests that wait take, those of every window of the same control document, each counted as Blink counts it: its URL without the fragment, its referrer as the `Request` holds it, `about:client` by default, its header lines and its body, a string's by its length in UTF-16 code units, as Blink's `ExtractBody()` records it, though it goes as UTF-8; past it, it throws a `QuotaExceededError` ([`fetch_later.h`](../native/dom/fetch_later.h)). A cross-origin request of mode `same-origin`, or of mode `no-cors` that would not follow a redirect, never goes, as Blink's main fetch ends it. A `fetchLater()` that another window's script borrows runs in its receiver's realm, as Blink's bindings give an operation its receiver's `ScriptState`, so it resolves, queues and goes as that window's, and so does a borrowed `sendBeacon()` as its navigator's window's. One that would take the keepalive requests in flight past the Session's limits when it goes never goes, as Chrome's network service fails it as the browser starts it. So `fetchLater(url)` sends what `session.get(url, kind="fetch", initiator=page_url)` sends, later. The page's messages carry it as a request with flag 128, and a message of type 220 sends it.

A `HEAD` request's response has a null body, as one with a null body status does: each read gives nothing, and the body is never used.

`fetch()` follows the request's signal, which follows `RequestInit`'s `signal`, or the input `Request`'s, as Blink's does: one that has aborted rejects at once with its reason, and one that aborts later rejects the promise with its reason if no response has started, and cancels the request through the Session, as Blink's `FetchManager::Loader::Abort()` does. The `Response`'s body follows the signal too, as Blink's `BodyStreamBuffer` does: a read that waits for the body rejects with the reason, and so does each later read while `bodyUsed` stays false, unless a read had all of the body first. A null body that no script read rejects the same way, as Chrome's empty body of a `HEAD` request or a null body status does.

Known Gaps of `fetch()`: `RequestInit`'s `targetAddressSpace`, `adAuctionHeaders`, `privateToken` and `retryOptions`, which throw when given a value other than their default, `Request`'s `targetAddressSpace`, which reads as its default, a preload that a fetch of another cache mode or redirect mode, or with integrity, takes in Chrome but not in a Page, a request too large for one message, with a body of nearly 16 MiB, which fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch`, and URLs that are not HTTP, HTTPS, `data:` or `blob:`, which fail as in Chrome.

`Request` has Blink's members: `destination`, empty for a `Request` that a script makes, `referrer`, `about:client`, the empty string or the URL, `referrerPolicy`, `mode`, `credentials`, `keepalive`, `isHistoryNavigation` and `isReloadNavigation`. `clone()` of a `Request` or a `Response` copies it with a tee of its body, as Blink's does, and a cloned `Request` has a signal that follows the original's. Its body tees as Blink's `BytesConsumerTee()` splits one: a stream's own tee for a body made from a `ReadableStream`, a blob for each branch for a body that nothing has read, a string's or a buffer's among them, so a branch's reads settle from later tasks, as in Chrome, and otherwise each piece once, kept until both branches have read it ([`bytes_consumer_tee.h`](../native/dom/bytes_consumer_tee.h)). `Response.error()` gives a network error, of type `error`, and `Response.redirect()` a response with `Location`, with Chrome's errors for a URL that does not parse and a status that is not a redirect's. A body belongs to the context that it was made in: once that goes, as a frame's does with the frame, a read under way settles nothing and leaves a page's own stream as it is, and a new one rejects with `InvalidStateError`, as in Chrome. An opaque `Response` keeps the response it filters, its status, URL and header lines, as Blink's `FetchResponseData` keeps its internal response, and the Cache API stores those, as Chrome's does.

`formData()` reads a body as Blink's `Body::formData()` does ([`multipart_parser.h`](../native/dom/multipart_parser.h)). A `multipart/form-data` body with a `boundary` goes through Blink's `MultipartParser`, piece by piece as it comes: each part needs a `Content-Disposition` of type `form-data`, in that case, with a `name`, and a part with a `filename` gives a `File` of its bytes, of its `Content-Type` as it is written, or `text/plain`, and of no known time, while any other part gives its bytes as UTF-8, and a part that is not UTF-8 fails. A part's header fields are parsed as `//net` parses them, and the first value of each name stands, as Blink's `HTTPHeaderMap::Add()` keeps it. An `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body is parsed as `URLSearchParams` parses a query, with a byte order mark kept as a character. Any other body is read and then fails with `TypeError: Failed to fetch`, and a null body, or a `multipart/form-data` type without a boundary, rejects with `TypeError: Invalid MIME type`, without reading it, but an urlencoded null body gives an empty `FormData`.

### Images

An `img` loads its source through the Session as a request of Kind `image`, with the document as its Initiator, as Blink's `ImageLoader` and `ResourceFetcher` run it, in [`native/dom/image_loader.cc`](../native/dom/image_loader.cc) and [`native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc`](../native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc):

- **Which source.** A change of `src`, `srcset` or `sizes`, and an `img` going into or out of a `picture`, picks the source anew, as Blink's `HTMLImageElement::SelectSourceURL()` does: the first `source` of its `picture` before it with a `srcset`, a `type` that a decoder of Chrome's takes, and a `media` that the Machine matches, and otherwise its own `srcset` and `src`. A candidate's width descriptor is divided by the source size that `sizes` gives, its first media condition that matches and its length, through the media queries and CSS math of [`native/css`](../native/css), 100vw for none, as Blink's `SizesAttributeParser` has it. The candidate is the first whose density reaches the Machine's scale, or the densest, as Chrome 157's `SrcsetSelectionMatchesImageSet` picks it, unless a denser one is loaded already or a data: URL. A candidate that does not parse goes, with Chrome's console warnings, such as `Failed parsing 'srcset' attribute value since its 'w' descriptor is invalid.` and `Dropped srcset candidate "a.png"`. `auto` in `sizes` takes the width of a layout box that a Page never has, so it gives the fallback that follows it, or 100vw.
- **Lazy images.** As the owner chose ([#284](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/284)), without layout to tell how far an image is from the viewport: a `loading="lazy"` image in the document waits until the next rendering opportunity, where Blink's observer of lazy images hears of what came near the viewport, and then loads at its normal priority, never from the preload scanner. One out of the document never loads, as in Chrome, until it goes into one. One that the document has loaded already, or that stops being lazy, loads at once.
- **When it loads.** As the HTML Standard's "update the image data": from a microtask, or at once for a URL that the document has loaded already, which is the list of available images, as an `img` does whether or not it is in a document, and never for one in a document without a window. A source that is empty or no URL fires `error`, and none at all nothing. A change of `crossorigin` or `referrerpolicy` loads again. `new Image()` loads like any other.
- **The request.** `crossorigin` sends it in mode `cors`, with credentials only for `use-credentials`, and a response that fails the CORS check breaks the image; `referrerpolicy`, with the legacy keywords that Blink takes for an image, sets its referrer policy, and `fetchpriority` its priority, from Blink's `kLow` for an image, as `ResourceFetcher::ComputeLoadPriority()` works it out. The document's resources serve each request of a URL that Blink would not fetch again: every one while the document loads, and afterwards an image that loaded, as Blink's `DetermineRevalidationPolicy()` has it. A data: URL loads in the Page, with no request, as Blink loads one in the renderer.
- **What comes back.** An image ignores its response's status, as Blink's `ImageResource` does, and is broken when no decoder of Chrome's reads its first bytes: JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP, ICO and CUR, BMP, AVIF and JPEG XL, by their signatures, and SVG by the MIME type `image/svg+xml`, with an `svg` root element in SVG's namespace. The Page decodes no pixels. It reads the size from the header, as each decoder does before it decodes ([`native/dom/image_header.cc`](../native/dom/image_header.cc)), with the EXIF orientation of JPEG, PNG and WebP, an AVIF's rotation and a JPEG XL's orientation, which swap width and height, and EXIF's resolution where it matches its pixel dimensions, as Blink corrects the density. An SVG image's natural size comes from its root's `width` and `height` in user units, none for a percentage or a negative or missing one, and its ratio from those or its `viewBox`, with Blink's `preserveAspectRatio="none"` rule; one without both falls back to the default object size, 300 by 150, as CSS's default sizing algorithm has it.
- **What a page reads.** `load` or `error` fires at the `img` from a task of the DOM manipulation task source once the load ends. `complete` is true once it has ended, or when there is nothing to load. `currentSrc` is the URL that was requested once the image is available or broken, and `""` before, so an image from the document's resources has it at once. A source whose URL does not resolve, such as `//[`, is the text that Blink's `KURL` keeps of it, which its fetcher blocks as an access check, so `currentSrc` gives the source, `complete` is true, and setting it again fails again. `naturalWidth` and `naturalHeight` are the natural size divided by the density of the candidate that `srcset` chose. `decode()` resolves from a task once the image has loaded, at once for SVG, and rejects with Chrome's `EncodingError`, `The source image cannot be decoded.`, for a broken image, a document that has gone, or a source that changed before it settled, as Blink's `ImageLoader::Decode()` does.
- **Holding `load` back.** The fetch holds the window's `load` back until it ends, as Blink's loader does, and the `img` from its first data until its own `load` or `error`, which checks the document's at once. A lazy image waits for none of it until it comes near the viewport, and its fetch holds nothing back then either.
- **SVG's `image`.** Its `href`, or else `xlink:href`, loads through the same loader as an `img`'s source, as Blink's `SVGImageLoader` does ([`native/dom/svg_image_element.cc`](../native/dom/svg_image_element.cc)): as soon as it is set, by the DOM or through `href.baseVal`, in a document or out of one, again when `crossorigin` changes its state, with the `crossorigin`, `referrerpolicy` and `fetchpriority` of its request as for an `img`, and never lazily. It holds the window's `load` back, and `decode()` settles, as an `img`'s does. It fires `error` once its load fails, and SVG's `load` once it has loaded and nothing of its loader waits, as Blink's `SendSVGLoadEventToSelfAndAncestorChainIfPossible()` does: at the image, if something listens there or captures `load` at an ancestor, and then at each `svg` inside another that holds it, until the document's `load` has finished.
- **Known Gaps**: an image that starts loading while the document unloads, which Blink sends as a keepalive request, sends nothing; the memory cache that Blink's renderer shares between documents; images that a viewport's change would pick again, as a Machine never changes; `x` and `y`, which need layout (roadmap, M9); the `img`'s alternative text, which a rendering shows; SVG's `load` at an outermost `svg`, which Blink fires with the window's, and at an `svg` inside another once its children are parsed, if it holds nothing that loads; and what the other SVG elements that name a resource load, such as `use` and `feImage`.

### Links and the document's resources

A `link` with `rel="preload"` fetches what it names through the Session, as Blink's `LinkLoader` and `PreloadHelper` do, in [`native/dom/html_link_element.cc`](../native/dom/html_link_element.cc), once it is in a document that a window shows, and again when its `rel`, `href`, `as`, `type`, `media` or `sizes` changes:

- **What it fetches.** `as` names the Request Kind: `style`, `script`, `image` and `font` their own, and `fetch` a `fetch`, at Blink's priorities, which are the kinds' own but for a font's, `High` where `@font-face` asks for `VeryHigh`. A `type` that the kind does not take, a `media` that the Machine does not match and an `href` that is no URL fetch nothing, the first and the last with Chrome's console warnings, and so does an `as` that names no kind, with `<link rel=preload> must have a valid `as` value`, or `uses an unsupported `as` value` for one of the HTML Standard that Chrome does not preload, such as `document`. An image's `imagesrcset` and `imagesizes` choose its URL, as an `img`'s do. Without `crossorigin`, a preload goes in mode `no-cors` with credentials, a font's too, so that `@font-face`, which asks in mode `cors`, does not take it, as in Chrome; `referrerpolicy`, `fetchpriority` and the `integrity` of a script, a style sheet or a font go with it.
- **What a page sees.** `load` fires at the link once the fetch ends, from a task for one that the document has already, and `error` for a network error, an error status of a kind that does not ignore it, which every kind but an image's does, or a body that does not match `integrity`, with Chrome's console errors.
- **What takes it.** A request of the same URL, Request Kind, mode and credentials takes a preload that nothing has used, even one that a newer preload of the URL and another kind follows, as Blink keys its preloads by URL and type, and holds the window's `load` back from then on if it is an image's, a style sheet's or a font's, as Blink's `ResourceFetcher` promotes a preload; until then a preload holds nothing back. A `fetch()` takes one of `as="fetch"`, so it makes no request of its own, if it is a GET of the preload's mode and credentials mode, with no `keepalive` and no header of a conditional request such as `If-None-Match`, as Blink's `Resource::CanReuse()` has it: its response is the preload's ([`native/dom/preloaded_fetch.cc`](../native/dom/preloaded_fetch.cc)). Such a preload keeps a response of an error status, and its link fires `load`, as Blink's `RawResource` ignores the status. A link that goes, or that loads something else, lets its fetch go, which is cancelled from a task if nothing else uses it, and drops a `load` or `error` that a task was about to fire.
- **Known Gaps**: `as="track"`, which has no Request Kind; the warning that Chrome writes when a preload goes unused; an `XMLHttpRequest`, which takes no preload of `as="fetch"` where Chrome's async one does; and the response's `Link` header, which Chrome reads for preloads and hints as it reads the links.

A `link` with `rel="modulepreload"` fetches the single module script that it names into the window's module map, as Blink's `PreloadHelper::ModulePreloadIfNeeded()` does, without running it, and fires `load` at the link once the module map has it, or `error` where the fetch fails or the body does not match the integrity metadata: the `integrity` attribute's, or, with no such attribute, what the import map's `integrity` says of the URL. `as` names the module type: none or `script` a JavaScript module, sent as a `module`, and `json` a JSON module, as a `module_json`. Any other `as` fires `error`, with Chrome's warning `<link rel=modulepreload> has an invalid `as` value`, and no `href`, or a `media` that the Machine does not match, fetches nothing and fires nothing, the first with `<link rel=modulepreload> has no `href` value`. `crossorigin` gives the credentials mode as a module script's does, `referrerpolicy` and `fetchpriority` go with the request, and its referrer is the document. A module script that imports the URL later takes the module script from the module map, with no request. **Known Gaps**: `as="style"`, a CSS module, which fails without M9's CSSOM where Chrome fires `load`; and Chrome's preload scanner, which fetches a `modulepreload` link in the markup before the element does, as a JavaScript module whatever its `as` and without the import map's integrity, where a Page fetches it from the element alone: from the markup, Chrome sends a JSON module's preload with a module's lines, fetches one whose `as` names no module type, and fetches one whose import map integrity fails twice.

A `link` with `rel="prefetch"` fetches what it names as a request of the Request Kind `prefetch`, as Blink's `PreloadHelper::PrefetchIfNeeded()` does, once and again as a preload's link does: at the lowest priority, which the load scheduler holds back until the body like any request below `High`, in mode `no-cors` with credentials, or `cors` as `crossorigin` says, with its `referrerpolicy` and `fetchpriority`. It holds nothing back, nothing else of the document takes it, and `load` or `error` fires at the link once it ends.

A `link` with `rel="preconnect"` or `rel="dns-prefetch"` hints at its origin, as Blink's `PreloadHelper` does, once and again as a preload's link does, and the preload scanner hints at a preconnect's origin before the parser gets there, as long as its URL is absolute or the document has a `base` element. The Session follows each hint of an HTTP or HTTPS origin as Chrome's browser does ([`native/preconnect_manager.cc`](../native/preconnect_manager.cc)), through the `PreconnectManager` of Chrome 157 on its default path: the newest hint first, three at a time, each looks up the Session's proxy for the origin, resolves the host's name at the lowest priority unless a proxy serves it, and, for a preconnect, then opens one connection to the origin, over HTTPS where HSTS upgrades it, which a later request of the same credentials and partition takes, as `NetworkContext::PreconnectSockets()` does. A preconnect's connection carries credentials, so that a request with them takes it, unless `crossorigin` says `anonymous`. A connection that is open or opening already for the same origin, credentials and partition is enough, so the scanner's hint and the link's open one.

**The favicon.** Once the top-level document's `load` event has been dispatched, the Page asks for its icon as Chrome's favicon driver does ([`native/page/favicon_loader.cc`](../native/page/favicon_loader.cc)), as a request of the Request Kind `favicon`: the last `link` among the head's children whose `rel` has `icon`, with an `href` and a `media` that matches, as Blink's `Document::IconURLs()` orders them, or, with none, `/favicon.ico` of an HTTP or HTTPS document's origin. If no image comes of it, as the Page reads its header, it asks for the next, from the last. A `data:` icon asks for nothing, and an icon's request adds an entry to the window's Performance Timeline whose initiator type is `other`, as Chrome's image downloader names its loader `internal`. The Page keeps which icons it fetched, and which gave no image, as Chrome's favicon service does, and asks for neither again, so a document that loads again asks for no icon.

A `link` with `rel="stylesheet"` and a `style` element load their **style sheets** as Blink's `LinkStyle` and `StyleElement` do, in [`native/dom/html_link_element.cc`](../native/dom/html_link_element.cc) and [`native/dom/html_style_element.cc`](../native/dom/html_style_element.cc), though the Runtime applies no style until M9: what it keeps of a sheet is its `@import` rules ([`native/dom/style_sheet_contents.cc`](../native/dom/style_sheet_contents.cc)), which it fetches in turn.

- **What a sheet fetches.** A link's sheet is a `style` request, unless the link is `disabled` or its `type` is neither empty nor `text/css`, with its `crossorigin`, `referrerpolicy`, `fetchpriority`, `integrity` and `charset`. Its `@import` rules are those Blink's parser takes at the top of the sheet, after `@charset` and `@layer` statements and before any rule that parses, an at-rule with a block counting only if Blink's parser makes a rule of its prelude, as `CSSParserImpl` checks each: a `@supports` or `@container` condition, the names of `@keyframes`, `@counter-style`, `@property`, `@font-palette-values`, `@position-try` and `@layer`, the selectors of `@page` and `@scope`, the families of `@font-feature-values`, the parameters of `@function`, nothing for `@font-face`, `@starting-style` and `@view-transition`, and the `syntax`, `inherits` and initial value of `@property`, and each goes as a `style` request with the sheet's URL as `Referer`, a sheet's of another origin too, as the Session takes the URL of a response that the document had whole, under the policy that the sheet's `Referrer-Policy` header names, else the document's, at the priority of the sheet above it; an import of the sheet itself, or of one above it, fetches nothing, the document's own URL for a `style` element's sheet, whose base URL is the document's. A `style` element's imports go from the document. An import whose URL does not resolve fails, as Blink's fetcher blocks the text that its `KURL` keeps, but for empty text, as of a relative URL against a base of about:blank, which matches the null URL of a `style` element's sheet above it, and so imports nothing. A sheet whose `Content-Type` is not `text/css` is empty, and so imports nothing, unless the document is in quirks mode and the response is not opaque.
- **What it holds back.** A sheet that the parser inserts, and whose `media` matches, holds back the scripts after it until it and its imports have loaded, and, in the body, the parser too, as Blink's `HTMLDocumentParser` waits for one; one for other media, or an alternate one, holds nothing back and goes at Blink's lowest priority. A sheet that a script inserts holds back no script. Every sheet holds the window's `load` back until it has loaded.
- **What a page sees.** `load` fires at the element once the sheet and its imports have loaded, a `style` element's too, or `error` if the sheet or an import failed, a sheet whose body does not match `integrity` included, with Chrome's console errors; from a task, as in Chrome. A `style` element of a document that no window shows, as `DOMImplementation` and `DOMParser` make, fires them too, from the tasks of the window whose script made it, as Blink's `Document::GetTaskRunner()` takes its execution context's, and holds back neither its parser nor a script; its imports go to a fetcher that blocks each, as Blink's null fetch context does, so its sheet fails, but for one that imports nothing. A sheet of the wrong type loads, empty, unless its response says `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, which fails it with Chrome's console error, as Blink's `ResourceLoader` checks a style request's response.
- **Known Gaps**: the `crossorigin()`, `integrity()` and `referrerpolicy()` modifiers of `@import`, which Chrome has not shipped; `@import` with a false `supports()`, which loads nothing in Chrome; an SVG `style` element's imports; whether the initial value of an `@property` rule fits its syntax and is computationally independent, and whether the default of an `@function` parameter fits its type, which need the values of style (roadmap, M9), so that a rule Blink's parser drops for such a value still ends the imports after it; `@font-face` and the other URLs a sheet names, which need style; and CSSOM, M9's.

The document's resources, as Blink's `ResourceFetcher` keeps them for a document ([`native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc`](../native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc)), serve classic scripts and module scripts too: while the document loads, a script of a URL that it fetched already, or preloaded, takes that response, and runs it decoded as the request that fetched it said, as Blink's `ScriptResource` keeps its decoder. A module script, of any module type, takes the resource of its URL, the fragment aside, while the document loads or while its fetch runs, as Blink's `DocumentModuleScriptFetcher` fetches a `ScriptResource`: another module's, or a classic script's or a link preload's of `as="script"` with `crossorigin`, of its mode `cors` and credentials mode, but not a classic script's of mode `no-cors`. Its module script has that response's URL, so modules whose URLs differ only in their fragments make one request, and each `import.meta.url` is the first one's, as in Chrome. A request with `integrity` takes only a resource fetched with the same metadata. A resource whose load failed, by a network error or by an error status of any type but an image's, is fetched again by the next request, even while the document loads, as Blink's memory cache drops it, unless that request takes it as an unused link preload. An element that lets its fetch go before the fetch ends, as a script that never runs or an `img` whose `src` changes does, has it cancelled from a task once nothing else uses it, as Blink cancels a resource with no clients left.

The document's **referrer policy** is the one that the response's `Referrer-Policy` names, its last token that names one, or the one that a `meta` element named `referrer` sets once it is in the document, with the legacy keywords that Blink takes there, `never`, `always`, `default` and `origin-when-crossorigin`, and Chrome's console error for a value that names none, such as `Failed to set referrer policy: The value 'nonsense' is not one of ... The referrer policy has been left unchanged.`. Each request whose own policy is the empty string, an element's, a `fetch()` and a navigation that the document starts, goes with it, and the Session works out its `Referer` from it, as Chrome's network service does.

### The order of a document's requests

A document's requests go in Chrome's order and at Chrome's priorities, which three pieces of Blink give, ported in [`native/dom/html_preload_scanner.cc`](../native/dom/html_preload_scanner.cc), [`native/dom/resource_load_scheduler.cc`](../native/dom/resource_load_scheduler.cc) and [`native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc`](../native/dom/resource_fetcher.cc):

- **The preload scanner** reads the markup ahead of the parser with a tokenizer of its own, as Blink's main-thread `HTMLPreloadScanner` does with `ThreadedPreloadScanner` off, as Chrome 157 ships it: the first piece of the body as it comes, any piece that comes while the parser waits for a script or a style sheet, and the rest each time the parser stops to wait. It fetches what the elements to come ask for, a classic script's `src`, an `img`'s source as its `srcset`, `sizes` and `picture` choose it, a `link`'s style sheet or preload, an image `input`, a `video`'s `poster`, SVG's `image` and `script`, and the `@import` rules at the start of a `style` element, with their attributes and the document's referrer policy, which a `meta` element named `referrer` changes as the scanner meets it, and resolved against the first `base` element. The elements take what it fetched. It fetches no `data:` image, no lazy image, which waits for its rendering, nothing after a `meta` element of `Content-Security-Policy`, as `AllowPreloadingWithCSPMetaTag` is off, and nothing in a `template`. What a write gives while the parser waits has a scanner of its own, as Blink's insertion scanner.
- **Priorities**, as `ResourceFetcher::ComputeLoadPriority()` gives them: style sheets and fonts `VeryHigh`, scripts `High`, images `Low`; a script or a style sheet that the scanner finds after the first image `Medium`; the first five images that the scanner finds and that are not small, 10,000 square pixels or less by their `width` and `height`, `Medium`; an async or deferred script `Low`; a style sheet for other media, or an alternate one, `VeryLow`; with `fetchpriority` and render-blocking over them.
- **The load scheduler**, as Blink's `ResourceLoadScheduler` of a frame: until the parser inserts the body, a request below `High` starts only while fewer than two requests run, or a `Medium` one while fewer than two `Medium` ones do, and the others wait, best first, then in the order they came; with the body, they all start.
- **Known Gaps**: module scripts and `rel="modulepreload"`, which the module loader does not take from the scanner yet, and whose fetches the load scheduler does not count, as they go around the document's resources, so a `modulepreload` of the markup gives its entry the initiator type `script` where Chrome's scanner gives `other`; the scanner's guess of the viewport from a `meta` element named `viewport`, which a desktop's Chrome ignores anyway; and the priority that an image gets once it is laid out in the viewport, which needs layout (roadmap, M9).

Why a Page reads the markup ahead of its parser at all is in [#259](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/259)'s Decisions. The Parity Checks of the `document_*` cases replay the recorded document through a Page ([parity.md](parity.md)), and [`tests/test_page_preloads.py`](../tests/test_page_preloads.py) checks what the scanner fetches while the parser waits and what waits before the body.

### The Cache API

`caches`, `CacheStorage` and `Cache` follow Blink's, with Chrome's checks, errors and messages, and keep their entries in the Session, which runs the Cache API as Chrome's browser does, as [#262](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/262) chose: every Page of the Session shares the caches of a storage key, as two tabs of a profile do, and another Session has caches of its own. A document's storage key is the library's, as for `localStorage` ([Keys](#localstorage-and-sessionstorage)): a frame of another site than the top-level document, or under one, has caches apart from the same origin's at the top level.

- **Who may.** `caches` is there only in a secure context, so not on a Page's first about:blank, whose origin is opaque, as in Chrome. A document that is a secure context but whose origin is opaque would get Chrome's `SecurityError` from it, as would a worker of a data: URL. A dedicated worker reaches the caches of its creator's storage key, through a host of its own on its thread: the `SessionSender` numbers every context's questions in one series, as the Session needs them in order, and hands each answer to the thread of the context that asked. A context that goes, such as a worker that ends, takes with it the caches it opened and the bodies it could still read, as its pipes go with it in Chrome, so that a cache deleted after it goes lets its files and quota go.
- **The Page's side.** [`native/dom/cache_storage.cc`](../native/dom/cache_storage.cc) and [`native/dom/cache.cc`](../native/dom/cache.cc) port Blink's `CacheStorage` and `Cache`: `put()` takes a GET of an HTTP or HTTPS URL, refuses a response with `Vary: *`, a partial one and one whose body is used, takes the response's body and sends the entry; `add()` and `addAll()` fetch each request through the window's `fetch()`, refuse a response that is not ok with `TypeError: Request failed` after a console error that says why, abort the other fetches when one fails, and send all of the entries in one batch; a match, `matchAll()`, `keys()` or `delete()` with a method other than GET settles without asking, unless it ignores the method. Each promise settles from a task of its own, on Blink's queue of miscellaneous platform APIs, once the Session has answered ([The messages](#the-messages), 40 and 41).
- **The Session's side.** [`native/cache_storage`](../native/cache_storage) ports Chrome's `CacheStorage` and `CacheStorageCache`: each cache is a `//net` disk cache of its own, its operations run as Chrome's scheduler runs them, matches and keys alongside each other and each put and delete alone, a put replaces the entry of its URL whatever its method and `Vary`, a batch that would write the same URL twice fails with `InvalidStateError: ... duplicate requests (...)`, a match follows `ignoreSearch`, `ignoreMethod`, `ignoreVary` and the stored response's `Vary`, results come in the order the entries were put, caches are listed in the order they were made, a query is capped at 10 MB without its bodies, and a deleted cache still answers through the `Cache` that a page holds until the page lets it go. What a Page sends is hostile input: the library checks each call before it acts on it.
- **Bodies.** A put's body stays in the Page until the cache writes it, and goes a megabyte at a time as the Session asks, and a matched response's body comes only once a script reads it, 64 KiB at a time as the Page gives credit, as a fetch's body does. So neither side holds more of a body for the other than a part, and a body that no script reads never leaves the Session, but for a matched response's `clone()`, which reads all of the body into the Page for both copies at once, as Blink's tee reads a body that it cannot hand on whole: Chrome's matched body is a blob, which each copy reads by itself, where the Session gives a stored body to one reader. A response with an empty body comes back with a null body, as Chrome's does.
- **Quota.** A put needs the room its entries take, before it writes anything, within the quota that Chrome enforces for a storage key: in a storage directory, 75% of a pool of 80% of the disk that holds it, and Incognito's in memory, a third of a random 15% to 20% of the Host's memory; past it, it fails with `QuotaExceededError: Quota exceeded.`. `navigator.storage.estimate()` gives what the caches hold, with `usageDetails.caches`, and 10 GiB more ([`native/storage_quota.h`](../native/storage_quota.h)).
- **Where it is kept.** In the Session's storage directory ([From Python](#from-python)), under `CacheStorage`, in a directory for each storage key named after a hash of it, as Chrome names one after its origin: each cache is a `//net` simple cache in a directory of its own, and a file lists the caches in the order they were made. Without a directory, in memory until the Session goes. A frame under a top-level document of an opaque origin, such as a data: URL's, which no later document has the key of, keeps its caches in memory too.

An opaque response, which a page gets from a `no-cors` fetch, goes in with the status, URL, header lines and body that no script reads, and comes back opaque, with a null body, as in Chrome. It counts in the usage with Chrome's padding, which hides its size: the library draws one at random for a response from the network, and the same for each hit of the HTTP cache, and each copy of the response keeps it, a clone or a match's ([`native/response_padding.h`](../native/response_padding.h)). The V8 code cache that a service worker's put makes is not there yet, and neither is Chrome's opaque response blocking: a response that it blocks, such as HTML from another origin, goes in with its header lines and body, where Chrome's goes in without either. A response's `body` stream is a Known Gap, as for `fetch()`.

The tests run [`tests/cache_storage_cases.js`](../tests/cache_storage_cases.js) in a Page and compare what it gives with what Chrome 157 gave for it, in `tests/data/cache_storage_cases.json`, and check that a Session in another process opens the caches of a storage directory again.

### IndexedDB

`indexedDB` and every IndexedDB interface follow Blink's, with Chrome's checks, errors, messages and order of events, and keep their databases in the Session, which runs IndexedDB as Chrome's browser does: every Page of the Session shares the databases of a storage key, as two tabs of a profile do, an open or a delete in one Page waits for the connections of the others, and another Session has databases of its own. A document's storage key is the library's, as for `localStorage` ([Keys](#localstorage-and-sessionstorage)): a frame of another site than the top-level document, or under one, has databases apart from the same origin's at the top level. A dedicated worker has its creator's, and its IndexedDB runs on its thread through a host of its own: the `SessionSender` numbers every context's calls, connections, transactions and cursors in one series, as the Session needs, and hands what answers each to the thread of the context that called. A worker that ends closes its connections, as a document that goes does.

- **Who may.** A document of an HTTP or HTTPS origin. One whose origin is opaque, such as about:blank, still has `indexedDB`, and `open()`, `deleteDatabase()` and `databases()` give Chrome's `SecurityError`.
- **The Page's side.** [`native/dom/idb_*`](../native/dom) port Blink's `IDBFactory`, `IDBOpenDBRequest`, `IDBRequest`, `IDBDatabase`, `IDBTransaction`, `IDBObjectStore`, `IDBIndex`, `IDBCursor`, `IDBCursorWithValue`, `IDBKeyRange`, `IDBRecord`, `IDBVersionChangeEvent` and `DOMStringList`: keys and key paths as Blink converts and evaluates them, the key that a key generator gave put into the value it reads back, index keys worked out before a put, results in the order of their requests, events from a request to its transaction and its database, a transaction that commits by itself once the task that made it, or the last result's events, end with nothing pending, the abort that an uncaught exception in a handler causes, and the cursor's prefetch of 5 entries, then twice as many each time up to 100. Each answer comes from a task of its own, on Blink's database access queue ([The messages](#the-messages), 42 and 43).
- **The Session's side.** [`native/indexed_db`](../native/indexed_db) ports Chrome's browser: each database's open and delete requests, one at a time, with versionchange at the other connections, blocked when one stays open, and the upgrade's transaction; transactions with their task queues, the quota checked at commit and the 60-second timeout of one that blocks another Page; cursors; and the locks of Chrome's on-disk store, by which a transaction shares its database's lock and holds each object store of its scope, shared to read and alone to write, so that two readwrite transactions on other object stores run at once, and an upgrade or a delete has the database alone. A storage key's databases are one ordered store, keyed as Chrome's on-disk store keys them, and a transaction's writes stay with it until it commits them, all at once. What a Page sends is hostile input: a call that Chrome's browser takes for a bad message from a renderer refuses the Page, and closes the storage key's databases, as Chrome does.
- **Values.** A value travels and is kept as `SerializedScriptValue` writes it, whole: Chrome wraps one over 64 KiB in a blob and compresses what it keeps, which no page sees. Known Gap: a call or an answer travels in one frame, so a request whose keys and value come to more than 16 MiB fails with the `UnknownError` that Blink gives one past Mojo's larger limit, and a name, key path or index keys that large abort their transaction, where Chrome's would succeed.
- **Where it is kept.** In the Session's storage directory ([From Python](#from-python)), in the SQLite database `IndexedDB`, which keeps each storage key's ordered store, a row for each entry. Chrome keeps each in a LevelDB of its own, a file no page reads. A storage key's databases load into memory before its first open, delete or `databases()`, and each commit writes through to the file from there, in order, so an open Session holds the databases its Pages have reached in memory. `navigator.storage.estimate()` reads what the file holds of the others. Without a directory, in memory until the Session goes, and so for a frame under a top-level document of an opaque origin, which no later document has the key of.

The tests run [`tests/indexed_db_cases.js`](../tests/indexed_db_cases.js) in a Page and compare what it gives with what Chrome 157 gave for it, in `tests/data/indexed_db_cases.json`, and check that the Pages of a Session share databases, that an upgrade in one Page asks another's connection to close, that another Session has databases of its own, that a call too large for a frame fails without ending the Page, and that a Session in another process opens the databases of a storage directory again. [`tests/test_page_storage_keys.py`](../tests/test_page_storage_keys.py) checks a frame's storage key.

### Storage buckets

`navigator.storageBuckets`, `StorageBucketManager` and `StorageBucket` follow Blink's, with Chrome's checks, errors and messages, and keep their buckets in the Session, which runs them as Chrome's quota manager does: each storage key has its default bucket, which `indexedDB`, `caches` and `navigator.storage` reach, and the buckets that a page opens by name beside it, each with an IndexedDB, a Cache API, a quota and an expiration of its own. Every Page of the Session shares the buckets of a storage key, as two tabs of a profile do, and another Session has buckets of its own. A document's storage key is the library's, as for `localStorage` ([Keys](#localstorage-and-sessionstorage)). A dedicated worker has its creator's, and asks through a host of its own on its thread, whose questions the `SessionSender` numbers with every other context's, as for the Cache API.

- **Who may.** A secure context of an origin that is not opaque. A page that is not a secure context has no `navigator.storageBuckets`, and in a secure context of an opaque origin `open()`, `keys()` and `delete()` give Chrome's `SecurityError`.
- **Names and options.** A name is 1 to 63 lowercase Latin letters, digits, `-` and `_`, not `-` or `_` first, so no page names the default bucket, whose name is `_default`, and `default` is a bucket like any other; another name rejects with Chrome's `TypeError`, which quotes it for `open()` and not for `delete()`. `open()` reads `expires`, `persisted` and `quota`, in that order: `durability` and `StorageBucket`'s `durability()` and `locks` are behind runtime-enabled features that are off in stable, and a Page has them only with those features on, `locks` as a Known Gap. A quota of 0 and an expiration that has passed reject before or as the Session answers. Opening a bucket that is there gives a new `StorageBucket` of it and changes only a later expiration: its quota and durability stay those it was made with, and `persisted` asks for the persistent storage permission, which a Page never has, so `persisted()` and `persist()` give `false`. A storage key may have as many buckets as its quota holds of 20 MiB, and at least one, the default bucket among them once IndexedDB or the Cache API has reached it; one more rejects with `QuotaExceededError: Too many buckets created.`.
- **Policies.** `estimate()` gives what the bucket's IndexedDB and caches hold, and as its quota the bucket's own, or else the usage and 10 GiB more, with an empty `usageDetails`; `navigator.storage.estimate()` gives what every bucket of the storage key holds. A write in a bucket needs room within both its storage key's quota and its own: past the bucket's, a transaction aborts with a `QuotaExceededError` with no message, and a cache's put rejects with `QuotaExceededError: Quota exceeded.`. `setExpires()` takes any time, even one that has passed, and `expires()` gives it in milliseconds, to the microsecond that Chrome keeps, or an infinity for one past the largest; a bucket that has expired stays until `keys()` lists the storage key's buckets, an `open()` of its name, or the Session's round of deleting them, 5 minutes after it opens and every 30 minutes after, deletes it.
- **Deleted buckets.** `delete()` deletes a bucket with its data, and resolves for a name that has none. The bucket's IndexedDB connections close, as Chrome's do: their transactions abort with `UnknownError: Connection is closing because of: Force close delete origin`, and each `IDBDatabase` hears `close`. A `StorageBucket` of a bucket that has gone keeps its own, which another open of the name never gets: its `indexedDB` gives `UnknownError: Internal error.`, its `caches` an `UnknownError` for `open()` and `has()`, no names, no match and `false` from `delete()`, a `Cache` that a page holds of it no match and a `NotFoundError` for `keys()` and `put()`, and its calls an `UnknownError`, but for `persist()`, which answers from what the Session last read of the bucket for every `StorageBucket` of it in the Page, as Chrome's `BucketHost` does, until a call finds it gone.
- **The Page's side.** [`native/dom/storage_bucket_manager.cc`](../native/dom/storage_bucket_manager.cc) and [`native/dom/storage_bucket.cc`](../native/dom/storage_bucket.cc) port Blink's: a bucket's `indexedDB` and `caches` are an `IDBFactory` and a `CacheStorage` that name it in their calls, and each promise settles from a task of its own once the Session has answered ([The messages](#the-messages), 180 and 181). A call of `StorageBucketManager` goes to the Session from a task of its own, after what its task sent beside it, such as the commit that a transaction sends once the task's microtasks have run, since Chrome's browser acts on such a call only after hops through its quota manager's threads: so a bucket that `delete()` deletes closes its connections after that commit, as in Chrome.
- **The Session's side.** [`native/page_storage_buckets.cc`](../native/page_storage_buckets.cc) ports Chrome's `BucketManagerHost` and `BucketHost`, and [`native/storage_quota.cc`](../native/storage_quota.cc) the quota manager's buckets. A deletion looks the bucket up on the database's sequence first, as Chrome's does, so that a commit that the Page sent before it ends first.
- **Where it is kept.** In the Session's storage directory ([From Python](#from-python)), in the SQLite database `QuotaManager`, whose table `buckets` has Chrome's columns of what a page can reach, and each bucket's IndexedDB and caches beside the default bucket's, under its storage key and its ID, which no other bucket ever has. Without a directory, in memory until the Session goes.

`getDirectory()` answers as `navigator.storage.getDirectory()` does, a Known Gap. Two things Chrome does that a Page does not: a bucket whose `indexedDB` or `caches` a page reached before its deletion, but never called, keeps working in Chrome, on the deleted bucket's ID, when that store held nothing; and Chrome's renderer stops when a deleted bucket's `indexedDB` opens after a call found the bucket gone, where a Page gives `UnknownError: Internal error.`.

The tests run [`tests/storage_buckets_cases.js`](../tests/storage_buckets_cases.js) in a Page, in memory and with a storage directory, and compare what it gives with what Chrome 157 gave for it, in `tests/data/storage_buckets_cases.json`, and check that the Pages of a Session share buckets, that a deletion in one Page closes another's connection, that another Session has buckets of its own, that a page that is not a secure context has none, that a Session in another process opens the buckets of a storage directory again, and that a deleted bucket leaves nothing there. [`tests/test_page_storage_keys.py`](../tests/test_page_storage_keys.py) checks a frame's buckets and what the library refuses.

### The Performance Timeline

A window's `performance` keeps the entries that Blink keeps for a top-level page, from one record of how the document loaded, its `DocumentLoadTiming` and `DocumentTiming`, which `performance.timing`, the navigation's entry and `chrome.loadTimes()` all read ([`native/dom/document_load_timing.h`](../native/dom/document_load_timing.h)). The Session sends the times of each response that //net measured, which every process of the machine shares, and the Page coarsens each with `performance.now()`'s clamper, so that all of them agree with `timeOrigin` and with each other, as in Chrome ([`native/dom/performance.cc`](../native/dom/performance.cc), [`native/page/resource_timing.cc`](../native/page/resource_timing.cc)):

- **The navigation's entry**, a `PerformanceNavigationTiming` at 0, is in the timeline from the document's start, first among those at 0, and reads the document's times as they come: its fetch, connection and response from //net, redirects, `domInteractive`, `DOMContentLoaded`, `domComplete` and `load`, and its duration, which is `loadEventEnd`. Its `type` is `navigate`, its unload times 0, as a tab's first document follows none it could read, and `activationStart`, `criticalCHRestart` and `notRestoredReasons` are 0, 0 and `null` unless the navigation restarted for Critical-CH, which Chrome makes a redirect. Redirects to another origin show as none, as for a navigation that sent no referrer, and so do they in `performance.timing` and `performance.navigation`. `confidence` is `null` until the document is interactive, and then a new object for each read, as Chrome's browser process answers once Blink tells it: `high` for a navigation once the browser has started, with Chrome's noise, at the rate 0.4994798 an answer drawn at random. Its body's sizes come once all of it has.
- **`visibility-state`**: one entry at 0, `visible`, which Blink adds when it makes a window's `Performance`.
- **An entry for each of the Page's requests**, a `PerformanceResourceTiming` named by the URL it started at, with the initiator type that Blink's loaders give it: `script` for a script or a module, `fetch` for `fetch()`, the element's local name for an image, `img`, SVG's `image`, an image `input` or a `video`'s poster, `link` for a link's style sheet, preload or prefetch, `css` for an import, and `other` for the icon, added when it ends, failed or cancelled too, before the script runs or the body's last read settles, as Blink's `ResourceFetcher` adds it. One that the library refused before it started gets none, as Blink fails such a fetch before it loads. The Fetch Standard's TAO check, which the library runs on each hop as Chrome's `CorsURLLoader` does, decides whether the entry shows the times of its steps, its protocol, its sizes, its `deliveryType` and its `Server-Timing` metrics. CORS decides whether it shows the status, the minimized MIME type and the content coding, so an opaque response, a script's from another origin, shows neither. `renderBlockingStatus` is `blocking` for a script that the parser waits for, or that says `blocking="render"`, as Blink decides. A response from the cache is delivered from it, with no bytes on the wire, and one that the server validated counts 300 for its headers, as Blink counts them. At most 250 wait in the buffer, or as many as `setResourceTimingBufferSize()` says; past that, `resourcetimingbufferfull` fires at `performance` from a task of its own, and what its listeners make room for joins, as the Resource Timing standard says.
- **User Timing's marks and measures**, with Blink's checks and messages, in the order of their start times. A mark's or a measure's `detail` is a structured clone of what the page gave, read back once, so the same object on each read, as [Structured clone and messaging](#structured-clone-and-messaging) says, with Chrome's `DataCloneError` for what it cannot copy, such as `HTMLDocument object could not be cloned.`. A measure may start or end at an attribute of `performance.timing`, as in Chrome.
- **`performance.timing` and `performance.navigation`**, the same objects each time. `performance.timing.toJSON()` gives its attributes in the order of Blink's hash table of them, as Chrome does, and `performance.toJSON()` gives `timeOrigin`, `timing` and `navigation`.

Each entry has the window's `navigationId`, which Blink draws from 100 to 10000 for each window. Entries of paint, input, layout shifts, long tasks and soft navigations never come, since a Page paints nothing and takes no input, and `getEntriesByType()` of one of those types gives none and warns in the console, as Chrome does. `PerformanceObserver`'s constructor, `observe()` and the rest stay Known Gaps.

### What the DOM has

| Interfaces | What they have, beyond the members that throw as Known Gaps |
| --- | --- |
| `Node` | The tree, `nodeType`, `nodeName`, `nodeValue`, `textContent`, `appendChild()`, `insertBefore()`, `replaceChild()`, `removeChild()`, `moveBefore()` of `ParentNode`, which moves an element or character data within its tree as `insertBefore()` does, with its records and range updates, but runs no removing or post-insertion steps that would lose its state, as Blink's state-preserving atomic move: a script does not run again and a modal dialog or an open popover stays so, `cloneNode()`, `normalize()`, `isEqualNode()`, `compareDocumentPosition()`, `contains()`, `getRootNode()`, `isConnected`, `ownerDocument`, `childNodes`, `baseURI`, `lookupPrefix()`, `lookupNamespaceURI()` and `isDefaultNamespace()` |
| `Element`, `HTMLElement` | Names, attributes with their namespaces, `getAttributeNS()`, `setAttributeNS()`, `removeAttributeNS()` and `hasAttributeNS()`, `attributes`, `getAttributeNode()`, `getAttributeNodeNS()`, `setAttributeNode()`, `setAttributeNodeNS()` and `removeAttributeNode()`, `classList` and `part`, reflected attributes, `innerHTML` and `outerHTML` both ways, written as HTML in an HTML document and as XML in any other, as `XMLSerializer` writes them, and parsed as XML there, `getHTML()`, which gives what `innerHTML` gives, as no element has a shadow root yet, `querySelector()`, `querySelectorAll()`, `matches()`, `closest()`, `getElementsByTagName()`, `getElementsByTagNameNS()`, `getElementsByClassName()`, `insertAdjacentElement()`, `insertAdjacentText()` and `insertAdjacentHTML()`, `tabIndex` with Blink's defaults, `nonce`, whose content attribute becomes `""` as an HTML, SVG or MathML element goes into a document whose response has a `Content-Security-Policy` or `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` header, as Blink's `HideNonce()` does, though a Page enforces no policy yet, `HTMLElement`'s `hidden`, `dir`, `translate`, `draggable`, `spellcheck`, `writingSuggestions`, `contentEditable` and `isContentEditable` as Blink reads them without style, and the members of `ParentNode`, `ChildNode` and `NonDocumentTypeChildNode`, such as `append()`, `before()`, `remove()` and `children` |
| `Document`, `HTMLDocument` | `new Document()`, `documentElement`, `doctype`, `head`, `body`'s getter, `title`, `URL`, `compatMode`, `characterSet`, `contentType`, `defaultView`, `createElement()`, `createElementNS()`, `createDocumentFragment()`, `createProcessingInstruction()` with Blink's checks of its target and data, `createCDATASection()`, which throws Blink's `NotSupportedError` for an HTML document, `createAttribute()`, `createAttributeNS()`, `importNode()`, `adoptNode()`, `implementation`, `createTextNode()`, `createComment()`, `createRange()`, `createTreeWalker()`, `createNodeIterator()`, `getElementById()`, `getElementsByName()`, `getElementsByTagName()`, `getElementsByTagNameNS()`, `getElementsByClassName()`, `querySelector()`, `querySelectorAll()`, `ParentNode`'s members, `cookie`, `readyState`, `currentScript`, `open()`, `close()`, `write()` and `writeln()`, as [Loading a document](#loading-a-document) says, `xmlVersion`, which is null for an HTML document and `"1.0"` for any other and takes only `"1.0"`, `xmlEncoding`, which is null, and `xmlStandalone`, as Blink has them, which an XML declaration sets ([The XML parsers](#the-xml-parsers)), and what [a top-level page reads](#what-a-top-level-page-reads) of it: `location`, `domain`, `designMode`, `lastModified`, its collections, `scrollingElement`, `activeElement`, visibility, full screen and picture-in-picture, and `customElementRegistry`, `hasStorageAccess()` and `featurePolicy` |
| `DOMImplementation`, `XMLDocument` | Every member: `createDocumentType()`, with Blink's check of the name, `createDocument()`, which makes an `XMLDocument` whose `contentType` is `application/xhtml+xml` for the HTML namespace, `image/svg+xml` for SVG's and `application/xml` for any other, as Blink's does, `createHTMLDocument()` and `hasFeature()`. The `createElement()` of an `application/xhtml+xml` document makes elements of the HTML namespace, as the DOM Standard says |
| `XMLSerializer`, `DOMParser` | `serializeToString()`, which writes a node as XML whatever its document, as Blink's `MarkupAccumulator` does: each element whose namespace its context lacks declares it, with the prefixes that are in scope kept and `ns1`, `ns2` and on made where one names another namespace, an element with no children closes itself but an HTML element that has an end tag, text is escaped with no `&nbsp;`, an attribute's tabs and line breaks become references, a doctype keeps its identifiers, and a CDATA section, comment or processing instruction is written as it is ([`markup_serializer.h`](../native/dom/markup_serializer.h)). `parseFromString()` makes, at the URL and with the origin of the window that made the parser and with no window, an `HTMLDocument` for `text/html`, whose scripts never run, whose noscript holds markup and which refuses declarative shadow roots, and an `XMLDocument` of the MIME type given for the others, which [the XML parsers](#the-xml-parsers) read, as Blink's `DOMParser` does |
| `CharacterData`, `Text`, `Comment` | Their data and its methods, `new Text()`, `new Comment()`, `splitText()` and `wholeText` |
| `ProcessingInstruction`, `CDATASection` | `target`, and a processing instruction's attributes, which Chrome reads of its data as an XML start tag's, as Blink's libxml2 path reads them: `getAttribute()`, `hasAttribute()`, `getAttributeNames()` and `hasAttributes()` read the data, and `setAttribute()`, `removeAttribute()` and `toggleAttribute()` write it again as `name="value"` pairs, which observers hear of as a change of the data. A CDATA section is a Text node of a document that is not HTML, whose text `textContent` counts. Serialized, a processing instruction is `<?target data?>`, and a CDATA section its text. `sheet` is a Known Gap, which gives null |
| `DocumentType` | Its name and identifiers, and `ChildNode`'s members |
| `Window` | `window`, `self`, `document`, `performance`, `setTimeout()`, `setInterval()`, `clearTimeout()`, `clearInterval()`, `queueMicrotask()`, `fetch()`, `navigator`, `clientInformation`, `screen`, `innerWidth`, `innerHeight`, `outerWidth`, `outerHeight`, `screenX`, `screenY`, `screenLeft`, `screenTop` and `devicePixelRatio`, as [The window](#the-window) says, `matchMedia()`, `crypto`, `atob()`, `btoa()`, `requestIdleCallback()`, `cancelIdleCallback()`, `event`, `scheduler`, `cookieStore`, `webkitRequestFileSystem()`, `reportError()`, as [Errors at the window](#errors-at-the-window) says, `structuredClone()`, `postMessage()` to the window itself, as [Structured clone and messaging](#structured-clone-and-messaging) says, and the attributes that [a top-level page reads](#what-a-top-level-page-reads) |
| `History`, `Navigation`, `NavigationHistoryEntry`, `NavigationActivation`, `PopStateEvent` | Every member, as [Session history](#session-history) says, but for what it lists as Known Gaps |
| `Location`, `BarProp`, `VisualViewport`, `Viewport`, `StyleMedia`, `CrashReportContext`, `CustomElementRegistry`, `TrustedTypePolicyFactory`, `DocumentPictureInPicture`, `FontFaceSet` | What [a top-level page reads](#what-a-top-level-page-reads) of each, with `TrustedTypePolicyFactory`'s table and checks, and nothing more: navigating, custom elements, policies and fonts are Known Gaps |
| `HTMLCollection`, `HTMLAllCollection`, `NodeList` | Every member, as [Collections](#collections) says: live views of the tree, with `HTMLAllCollection`'s names, `namedItem()` and `item()`, and the static `NodeList` of `querySelectorAll()` |
| `AbstractRange`, `NodeRange`, `Range`, `StaticRange` | Every member but `Range`'s geometry and `expand()`, as [Ranges and traversal](#ranges-and-traversal) says |
| `TreeWalker`, `NodeIterator` | Every member, with `NodeFilter`'s constants and the filter that a page gives, as [Ranges and traversal](#ranges-and-traversal) says |

| `Notification`, `PerformanceObserver`, `PressureObserver`, `PushManager`, `MediaSource` | Their static attributes alone, and `Notification`'s `permission`, `requestPermission()` and constructor, as [The members pages call most](#the-members-pages-call-most) says. A Known Gap makes their objects, whose other constructors and members are Known Gaps |
| `CustomEvent` | Every member: `detail` from its init dictionary, and `initCustomEvent()` |
| `EventTarget`, `Event` | Every member but `when()`, whose `Observable` the Runtime lacks, with a node's dispatch through the tree, as [Events](#events) says |
| `ErrorEvent`, `PromiseRejectionEvent` | Every member, as [Errors at the window](#errors-at-the-window) says |
| `MutationObserver`, `MutationRecord` | Every member, as [Mutation observers](#mutation-observers) says |
| `Navigator` | What [The window](#the-window) lists, `permissions`, `geolocation`, `storage`, `getGamepads()`, `locks`, `getBattery()`, `cpuPerformance`, `keyboard`, `mediaDevices` and `credentials`, as [The members pages call most](#the-members-pages-call-most) says. Its other members, such as `clipboard`, are Known Gaps |
| `Permissions`, `PermissionStatus`, `Geolocation`, `GeolocationPositionError`, `StorageManager`, `PermissionsPolicy`, `PresentationRequest`, `PresentationAvailability`, `DOMFileSystem`, `Entry`, `DirectoryEntry`, `CredentialsContainer` | What [The members pages call most](#the-members-pages-call-most) says, but `request()`, `revoke()` and `requestAll()` of `Permissions`, behind a feature that is off, `getDirectory()` of `StorageManager`, `start()` of `PresentationRequest`, what reads and changes a file system, and `create()`, `store()` and `preventSilentAccess()` of `CredentialsContainer` |
| `BatteryManager`, `Keyboard`, `KeyboardLayoutMap`, `MediaDevices`, `MediaDeviceInfo`, `InputDeviceInfo` | What [The members pages call most](#the-members-pages-call-most) says, but `lock()` and `unlock()` of `Keyboard`, capturing and its constraints of `MediaDevices`, and `getCapabilities()` of `InputDeviceInfo` |
| `PluginArray`, `Plugin`, `MimeTypeArray`, `MimeType` | Every member, with their indexed and named properties, but `refresh()`, as [The window](#the-window) says |
| `NavigatorUAData`, `NetworkInformation`, `Screen`, `ScreenOrientation` | Every member, as [The window](#the-window) says |
| `MediaQueryList`, `MediaQueryListEvent` | Every member, as [Media queries](#media-queries) says |
| `Performance` | `now()` and `timeOrigin`, coarsened as Chrome's are, `memory`, and [the Performance Timeline](#the-performance-timeline): `getEntries()`, `getEntriesByType()`, `getEntriesByName()`, `mark()`, `measure()`, `clearMarks()`, `clearMeasures()`, `clearResourceTimings()`, `setResourceTimingBufferSize()`, `onresourcetimingbufferfull`, `timing`, `navigation` and `toJSON()`. `interactionCount` stays 0, and `eventCounts` gives what [a top-level page reads](#what-a-top-level-page-reads) |
| `PerformanceEntry`, `PerformanceMark`, `PerformanceMeasure`, `PerformanceResourceTiming`, `PerformanceNavigationTiming`, `PerformanceServerTiming`, `PerformanceTiming`, `PerformanceNavigation`, `PerformanceTimingConfidence`, `VisibilityStateEntry` | Every member, as [the Performance Timeline](#the-performance-timeline) says, but `initiatorUrl`, which `ResourceTimingInitiator` holds back from stable. An entry of a type that a Page never makes, such as `PerformancePaintTiming`, is an entry with nothing behind it |
| `AbortController`, `AbortSignal` | Every member, as [Abort signals](#abort-signals) says |
| `TaskController`, `TaskSignal` | Their constructor and signal, its priority and abort. `setPriority()`, `prioritychange` and `TaskSignal.any()` are Known Gaps |
| `Crypto`, `QuotaExceededError` | `getRandomValues()` and `randomUUID()`, as [The members pages call most](#the-members-pages-call-most) says, and `subtle`'s object, whose members stay refused; every member of `QuotaExceededError` |
| `TextEncoder`, `TextDecoder` | Every member, as [Text encodings](#text-encodings) says |
| `IdleDeadline` | Every member, as [Idle callbacks](#idle-callbacks) says |
| `Scheduler` | `postTask()` and `yield()`, as [The scheduler](#the-scheduler) says |
| `LockManager`, `Lock` | Every member, as [Web Locks and the Cookie Store](#web-locks-and-the-cookie-store) says |
| `CookieStore`, `CookieChangeEvent` | Every member, as [Web Locks and the Cookie Store](#web-locks-and-the-cookie-store) says |
| `MessageChannel`, `MessagePort`, `MessageEvent`, `BroadcastChannel`, `UserActivation` | Every member, as [Structured clone and messaging](#structured-clone-and-messaging) says, but `MessagePort`'s `onclose`, behind a feature that only tests turn on |
| `URL`, `URLSearchParams` | Every member of `URL`, as Blink's has it on `KURL` (`native/dom/resolved_url.h`): parsing against a base, `URL.parse()` and `URL.canParse()`, each part of the URL with its setter, as a link's (`native/dom/url_utils.h`), `toJSON()` and `webkitURL`. `searchParams` is one `URLSearchParams` that follows the query and writes itself back into it. `URLSearchParams` takes a query, pairs or a record, decodes and encodes as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` does, and has `size`, `append()`, `delete()` and `has()` with a value, `get()`, `getAll()`, `set()`, `sort()` by code units and `toString()`. It iterates as a pair iterable, over the list as it is at each step. `URL.createObjectURL()` and `revokeObjectURL()` are the File API's, as [The File API](#the-file-api) says |
