It is [`tools/capture/probe.js`](../tools/capture/probe.js), and it runs unchanged in real Chrome, where [the capture kit's pages](capture.md#its-recording-cases) run it for a **Baseline** in a top-level page, in frames and in a dedicated worker, and in a Page, where a **Parity Check** [opens the same page](parity.md#the-script-fingerprint). An indirect eval of it, `(0, eval)(source)`, gives a promise of its record as JSON's text, which the Page program prints and a Page's `evaluate()` returns. [`tools/capture/probe.py`](../tools/capture/probe.py) reads it.

It follows what [#207](https://github.com/darionxt/mitto/issues/207)'s walk of Chrome 155, 156 and 157 found, and uses only what V8 has, taken before it reads anything, so that it runs to its end in a Page that has few interfaces yet and goes on running as the bindings grow. It uses no regular expression, whose matches would change `RegExp`'s legacy statics, which it reads.

### What it records

1. **The walk.** From `globalThis`, breadth first, each object that it reaches, once, under the path where it found it first, such as `navigator.userAgentData`. An object reached only as another's `[[Prototype]]` gets its path last, so that `Function.prototype` is found as that and not as `Object`'s `[[Prototype]]`. For each object, its own properties in order, with their attributes, its `[[Prototype]]`, what `Object.prototype.toString` calls it, and for a function its `name`, `length` and source text where that is not `function NAME() { [native code] }`. A function whose own properties are only its usual `length` and `name`, as nearly every method and accessor is, is written where it is found. For an object that is neither a function nor a prototype, what each getter on it and on its prototype chain up to `Object.prototype` gives on it, and for a function what its own getters give, such as `Notification.permission`. A node of the DOM other than the document is written by its `nodeName` and not entered, so that no layout runs. The walk stays in its own realm: an object whose prototype chain ends at another realm's `Object.prototype`, such as the window of a frame's parent of the same origin, is written as `["foreign", tag]` and not entered, and a node of another document, such as a frame's element in its parent, by its `nodeName`. A window of another origin has no prototype, so the walk reads the few members that it shows, as a frame of another site sees its parent.
2. **The checks.** What each function, getter and setter of a prototype, and of an object whose `[[Prototype]]` is an interface's or a class's prototype, such as the window, throws when it is called on a new plain object, and what each constructor does without `new`. A promise that such a call gives is followed to what it settles with, once the microtasks queued before have run. The members of a namespace, such as `console`, `Math` or `CSS`, and the static members of an interface belong to no kind of object, so no check calls them.
3. **The values that the Persona and the Machine decide**, beyond what the getters give: `navigator.userAgentData.getHighEntropyValues()` with every hint, `matchMedia()` with each media feature that Blink's `media_feature_names.json5` names, which keywords and integers of each match, and whether the window's and the screen's sizes and `devicePixelRatio` agree with it, the resolved options of each of `Intl`'s constructors, how many values `Intl.supportedValuesOf()` gives of each kind, the time zone through `getTimezoneOffset()` on the first of each month of 2026 and `Date`'s strings of a winter and a summer day, the state of each permission that `navigator.permissions.query()` knows, and the lists of `document.featurePolicy`. Each call it waits for has a time limit of 5 seconds. A worker has no `matchMedia()` and no permissions policy, so there the probe leaves those out, and its getters give what the Persona and the Machine decide in a worker, such as `WorkerNavigator`'s values.
4. **What the page reported** to a `ReportingObserver` while the probe ran, which shows a getter or a check that queues a report.

The record holds `format`, `objects` with one entry for each object in the order found, `values` and `reports`. The probe's opening comment says what each holds, and how values and outcomes are written. Most checks throw `TypeError: Illegal invocation`, which the record leaves out, so that only the other outcomes take room.

### What it leaves out

- **Any other call.** It calls nothing that asks a person or opens something, such as `requestPermission()`, a picker, `getUserMedia()` or `window.open()`, and nothing else that follows the machine: fonts, canvas, WebGL and audio, which M9 and M10 bring, codecs and media capabilities, voices, the keyboard's layout, the battery, devices and their availability, such as `enumerateDevices()`, Bluetooth, USB, gamepads and XR, WebAuthn's authenticators, the storage estimate, and what `chrome.loadTimes()` and `chrome.csi()` return.
- **What it does not read, with why**, each in a list in the probe. The getters `window.fence` and `navigator.webkitPersistentStorage`, and `navigator.deprecatedRunAdAuctionEnforcesKAnonymity` of Chrome 155 and 156 without the variations seed, which queue a deprecation report when read (#207). The check of `Intl.v8BreakIterator` without `new`, which V8 still answers with a break iterator, and Chrome with a deprecation report. Each of these is in the record as skipped.
- **Promises that it finds.** It never waits for one, since some never settle, such as `navigator.serviceWorker.ready`. It handles each, so that one that is rejected is not reported as rejected with no handler.
- **Other realms**, such as the window of a frame's parent, which the walk does not enter. Each frame and each worker is a Recording Case of its own, in which the probe runs.

### What changes from run to run

The times, the navigation entry's random `key` and `id`, `navigator.connection`'s `rtt` and `downlink`, which #207 found, and `performance.memory`'s figures change between two runs of one Chrome. [The Normal Form of a Script Fingerprint](capture.md#the-normal-form-of-a-script-fingerprint) hides them, with the values that the machine decides, so that two records compare equal. `script_normal_form()` in [`script_normal_form.py`](../tools/capture/script_normal_form.py) writes it, and capture.md says what it hides and why.

### Its size

In Chrome 157.0.8103.39 on the Windows machine, from a user data directory with the variations seed at an HTTPS page at `capture.test`, it reaches 2,150 objects in about 100 ms, and gives the same record twice in a row but for the values above. Its record takes about 1.3 MB as `dumps_record()` writes it, with each property, getter's value, check and value of the third step on a line of its own, so that two records differ by lines, and so does its Normal Form. That is within the 1.5 MB that a Baseline of the Script Fingerprint may take for each Chrome Release, which the owner chose on 2026-11-12, for the first Recording Case. Later Recording Cases keep only [what differs from the first](capture.md#what-a-later-case-keeps), within 50 KB for a frame and 250 KB for a worker. In a dedicated worker it reaches about 740 objects. In a Page today it reaches about 250 objects.

[`tests/test_page_probe.py`](../tests/test_page_probe.py) runs it in a Page through a Session, and in the Page program, twice each, and checks that it runs to its end, leaves nothing in the console, and gives the same Normal Form.

## From Python

Until the release that ships the Runtime, Python's Page is private: `Session._open_page(url)` returns a Page that a `with` statement closes, and `await AsyncSession._open_page(url)` an AsyncPage that an `async with` statement closes. Neither is in `mitto.__all__` or in [api.md](api.md), and the release that ships the Runtime names them `open_page()`. On a Session whose library has no Page program beside it, as in a wheel that a release made, opening a Page raises a `NativeLibraryError` that says that the Runtime is not in this build. Starting the Page's process blocks for the few milliseconds that it takes, also on the event loop of an AsyncSession.

`_open_page(url, timeout=None)` navigates the Page to `url` through the Session, as [Loading a document](#loading-a-document) says, and returns once the window's `load` event has been dispatched. `timeout` is a number of seconds for that wait. A navigation that fails raises the `RequestError` that `session.get(url)` raises for that failure, and a timeout raises `PageTimeoutError`: either closes the Page first. `about:blank` navigates nowhere, as a new tab's first document needs no request. `url` is then the document's URL, after the navigation's redirects and those that the document started before it loaded, or about:blank for a response that Chrome makes no document of, such as a 204, and it follows the navigations that the Page's documents start, as each gets its response, and those within a document. The Page's scripts run in its document's context, which has V8's built-ins, its console and the interfaces that [the bindings](#the-bindings) have so far, with [timers](#timers) and [`fetch()`](#fetch).

- `evaluate(expression, timeout=None)` runs a script and returns its result as Python's `json` reads what `JSON.stringify()` writes, with `None` for what it writes nothing for. It waits for a promise to settle, as the Page program does. `timeout` is a number of seconds, as a request's total time is.
- `console_messages` holds what the scripts wrote to the console, each with its `level` and `text`, `uncaught_errors` what a console says of each error that no script caught, and `known_gaps` the name of each Known Gap that each document reached, once for each document, for tests and debugging. Each keeps the last 1000, and fewer when their text is longer than 32 Mi characters, so that a Page that writes long messages cannot fill the caller's memory.
- `wait_for_navigation(timeout=None)` waits until the next navigation that the Page's documents started has ended, and returns a `PageNavigation`: its `url`, `status`, and whether it stayed within the document, `same_document`, or ended with no document, `kept_document`, as a 204 or a download does. Each call takes the oldest that no call has taken, so one that a script starts, which the Page has heard of once the script's call has returned, counts even if it ends before the call. One to another document ends once its document has fired its `load` event or another has taken its place. One that failed before a response, or that another took the place of, raises the `RequestError` that a request to its URL raises for that failure.
- `process_id` is the ID of the Page's process.
- `close()` ends the process and waits for it to go, which takes up to 2 seconds before a kill. An AsyncPage waits for that on a thread of its own.

`Session(_storage=path)` and `AsyncSession(_storage=path)` keep what the Session's Pages store in that directory, which is made if it does not exist, for later Sessions too, as Chrome keeps a profile's ([ADR-0010](adr/0010-a-directory-of-its-own-for-page-storage.md)). The native core writes the stores there, `localStorage` in the SQLite database `LocalStorage`, IndexedDB in the SQLite database `IndexedDB`, the Cache API's caches under `CacheStorage` and the storage buckets in the SQLite database `QuotaManager`, holds the directory while the Session is open, so that one Session at a time has it, in any process, with the file `mitto.lock` there on macOS and Linux, as it holds a cache's ([parity.md](parity.md#where-it-lives)), and writes all that is left before `close()` returns. The directory names the Machine whose storage it holds, in `Machine.json`, as a file of cookies names its Machine: a Session that opens on it claims that Machine, and one that `machine=` or `cookie_file` gives another Machine raises a `ValueError`. Another Session that has the directory open raises an `OSError` with `EBUSY`. Without `_storage`, the Session keeps it all in memory until it closes, as Chrome's Incognito mode does. `_storage` stays private with the Page API.

A call fails with an error of `mitto._errors`, sorted by what a caller does next, as a request's are. Each is a `MittoError`, and private too:

| Error | What happened | What a caller does next |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ScriptError` | The script threw, the promise that it gave was rejected, or its result cannot be read as JSON, such as a BigInt. The message is what a console says, such as `Uncaught TypeError: x is not a function` | Fix the script. The Page goes on |
| `PageEndedError` | The Page has ended: its process crashed, ran out of memory or was killed, broke the rules of the messages, or did not know its time zone or locale, or the Session closed. Each later call raises it again | Open another Page |
| `PageTimeoutError` | The timeout passed before the result arrived, or before the document loaded. It is a `TimeoutError` too | Wait longer, or close the Page: a script that never ends keeps it busy |
| A `RequestError` | Opening the Page: its navigation failed, as `session.get(url)` would have, such as a `ConnectError` | As for that request |

A Page that has been closed raises a `ValueError`, as a closed Session does. A Page waits for its events the way the Session's requests do. Page blocks in the native core with the GIL released, and several threads may use one Page: one of them at a time waits in the native core, and hands each the result that it waits for. Closing a Page while another thread waits ends that wait first, with `mitto_page_end_waits()`. An AsyncPage wakes when the Session's event descriptor, or its waiting thread on Windows, names the Page, and takes its events without waiting. A call takes at most 100 events before it looks for its result again, so that a Page that writes without end cannot keep it from its result or its timeout, or an AsyncPage hold up the loop. Closing the Session ends its Pages, whose calls then raise `PageEndedError`, and each still needs closing.

| [`tests/test_page_navigator.py`](../tests/test_page_navigator.py) | Pages of Sessions with several Machines, and with each Persona, through both APIs: what `navigator`, `navigator.userAgentData`, `navigator.connection`, `screen` and the window give is what the same Session sends in its client hints and `User-Agent` to the page's server, `rtt` and `downlink` as a `fetch()` to it sends them, or what persona.md works out from the Machine; `navigator.languages` from the Session's `Accept-Language` | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_navigator.py`](../tests/test_navigator.py) | The values of `navigator.userAgentData` write back each Persona's own user agent client hints, and the languages come from `Accept-Language` | Every job that runs the tests, with no build |
| [`tests/test_page_events.py`](../tests/test_page_events.py) | The event loop through the command line: the order of tasks, microtasks and timers, the HTML Standard's examples of timers and `queueMicrotask()`, nested timers and intervals clamped from the seventh on, IDs and clearing, timeouts that wrap, errors that timers and microtasks throw, the coarsened clocks, Chrome's checks of the timers' arguments, idle callbacks and their periods, the scheduler's priorities and what `yield()` inherits, and `window.event`, against Chrome | The native job on every Host |
| [`tests/test_page_cookie_store.py`](../tests/test_page_cookie_store.py) | The Cookie Store in a Page that a Session opened, against Chrome | The native job on every Host |
| [`tests/test_page_locks.py`](../tests/test_page_locks.py) | Web Locks in a Page that a Session opened, against Chrome | The native job on every Host |
| [`tests/test_page_resource_policy.py`](../tests/test_page_resource_policy.py) | `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` and credentialless requests of Pages that a Session opened, against Chrome 157: each policy for a no-cors `fetch()`, a script and an image of another origin, a redirect, a request of mode cors, `require-corp`, `credentialless` and a worker's own embedder policy | The native job on every Host |
| [`tests/test_page_isolation.py`](../tests/test_page_isolation.py) | [Cross-origin isolation](#cross-origin-isolation) of Pages that a Session opened, on a local server, against Chrome 157: which headers isolate a page, the console error for COOP at an untrustworthy URL, a document that navigates, workers, shared memory and `Atomics.waitAsync()` | The native job on every Host |
| [`tests/test_page_document.py`](../tests/test_page_document.py) | A script gets a document that the program builds from markup, finds and changes its elements, and the tree reads back as expected; about:blank's document; the doctype's quirks mode; the window as the global object; Known Gaps; nodes and wrappers through garbage collection; the XML markup of `XMLSerializer` and of a document that is not HTML, the XML attributes of a document and `DOMParser`'s HTML documents, as Chrome 157 gave them | The native job on every Host, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer |
| [`tests/test_page_probe.py`](../tests/test_page_probe.py) | [The probe](#the-probe) runs to its end in a Page through a Session and in the Page program, leaves nothing in the console, and gives the same record twice in a row but for the values that change from run to run; how a record is read, hidden and written | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel, the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer for the Page program alone, and the Python jobs for what needs no Page |
| [`tests/test_page_renderer.py`](../tests/test_page_renderer.py) | What Chrome's renderer adds beyond the IDL files, against Chrome 157's values: `window.chrome` and its place on the global object, `loadTimes()`, `csi()` and `app`, `console.memory`, `console.createTask()` and `MemoryInfo`, no `SharedArrayBuffer`, `Atomics.wait()` refused, `WebAssembly`'s members, `compileStreaming()` and `instantiateStreaming()` with Blink's checks, the 8 MB limit, `Error.isError()` of a `DOMException`, `Temporal.Now`'s clock and `--js-flags=`. Through a Session, the load times of a document from a local server, and `performance.memory`, precise at a site, with the heap's limit of the Machine's memory | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| `html_tree_builder_test` | html5lib's tree construction tests: each builds its tree, or records a rule it skipped, or is in the list of failures | The same |
| [`tests/test_page_abort.py`](../tests/test_page_abort.py) | `AbortController`, `AbortSignal`, `TaskController` and `TaskSignal`, as Chrome 157 gave them: reasons, `abort` events and their order across the signals that follow each other, `AbortSignal.abort()`, `timeout()` and `any()`, `throwIfAborted()`, `addEventListener()`'s `signal`, a `Request`'s signal, a `fetch()` with an aborted signal, and the errors of each member | The native job on every Host, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer |
| [`tests/test_page_document_encodings.py`](../tests/test_page_document_encodings.py) | Through a channel that the test holds, a document and its scripts in each encoding of the Encoding Standard, declared by the response, by each kind of `<meta>` and by the document's encoding, a byte order mark, an XML declaration, Chrome's detector and its default for the top-level domain and the locale, the prescan's limits and pieces that come apart, and the query of a script's URL in each encoding, as Chrome 157 gave them in [`tests/data/document_encodings.json`](../tests/data/document_encodings.json) | The native job on macOS and Linux, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer |
| [`tests/test_page_streams.py`](../tests/test_page_streams.py) | The Streams Standard, as Chrome 157 gave it: the interfaces and their errors, reads of default and byte streams, BYOB readers and requests, the queuing strategies, `tee()`, async iteration, writers, transform streams, `pipeTo()` and `pipeThrough()`, the streams of encodings and compression, and the order of reads, enqueues and a page's microtasks | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_page_uploads.py`](../tests/test_page_uploads.py) | A `fetch()` whose body is a stream, through both APIs at the capture server, as Chrome 157 gave it there: over HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 a stream of parts, empty ones, chunks that are not a `Uint8Array`, streams that error, an abort, a 302, and a preflight before a request to another site, even of mode `same-origin`, with the Recording Case's stream held to its Baseline, and over HTTP/1.1 a failure with nothing sent and the stream unread | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_page_text_encoding.py`](../tests/test_page_text_encoding.py) | `TextEncoder` and `TextDecoder`, as Chrome 157 gave them: what `encode()` and `encodeInto()` write and give, every label of the Encoding Standard and others that Chrome refuses, the decoding of every byte by each single-byte encoding and of every pair of bytes by each CJK encoding, by checksums, and which of them each lacks, streams, byte order marks, `fatal`, and the errors of each member | The native job on every Host, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer |
| [`tests/test_page_file_api.py`](../tests/test_page_file_api.py) | The File API, as Chrome 157 gave it: blobs made from strings, buffers and blobs, their types and slices, reads as text, bytes and streams, `FileReader` and `FileReaderSync` with their events and errors, files, blobs in clones and as bodies, blob URLs, and fetches of `data:` and `blob:` URLs | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_page_fetch_bodies.py`](../tests/test_page_fetch_bodies.py) | Pair iterators, as Chrome 157 gave them: the results and prototype of `Headers`', `URLSearchParams`' and `FormData`'s iterators, sorting, changes while they iterate and `forEach()`; `FormData`'s entries and what its members take; `FormData` and `URLSearchParams` bodies as Blink writes them; and `formData()` of multipart and urlencoded bodies, those that do not parse and those that stream | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_header_order.py`](../tests/test_header_order.py) | The order on the wire of the lines that a script sets on `fetch()`, in a window and in a worker, over HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2 and HTTP/3, as Chrome 157 sent them for the same script ([`tests/header_order_cases.js`](../tests/header_order_cases.js), [`tests/data/header_order_cases.json`](../tests/data/header_order_cases.json)), and of those that a caller gives a request of the kinds `fetch` and `xhr` | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_range_order.py`](../tests/test_range_order.py) | Where a `Range` line goes on the wire, after what `//net` adds as Chrome's HTTP cache puts it, or where it was, on a page's `fetch()`, as Chrome 157 sent it for the same script, and on a caller's request of the kinds `fetch`, `navigation` and `image`, as `//net`'s own cache sends it, in a Session with a cache and in one without | The native job on every Host, with the Page program from the installed wheel |
| [`tests/test_page_media.py`](../tests/test_page_media.py) | `matchMedia()`: queries written back as Chrome 157 writes them, a `MediaQueryList` and its listeners, `MediaQueryListEvent`, features that parse as the runtime-enabled features say, and, on a Session, each media feature as the Session's Machine decides it | The native job on every Host, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer |
| [`tests/test_page_performance.py`](../tests/test_page_performance.py) | The Performance Timeline against what Chrome 157 gave: User Timing's marks, measures, `detail` and errors through the command line, and, through a Session, a page whose navigation, scripts and fetches of two origins fill the timeline, with what the TAO check and CORS hide, `Server-Timing`, sizes of a body that came compressed, agreement with `performance.timing`, `timeOrigin` and `chrome.loadTimes()`; redirects of the navigation; the buffer of resource entries and its event; and a response from the cache | The native job on every Host, and the Runtime's builds with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer for the command line's case |
| `media_query_test` | The cases of Blink's media query tests that need no layout, against the parser and the evaluator in `native/css` | The native job on every Host, and the sanitizer workflow's `asan` and `ubsan` jobs |
| `page_messages_fuzzer` | How the library reads what a Page sends | The sanitizer workflow's fuzz job |
| `media_query_fuzzer` | The CSS tokenizer, and the media query parser and evaluator, on any input: the tokens cover the input in order, and what the parser writes back of what it wrote back of a list whose blocks all end is the same again | The same |

To run the tests of Pages against a local build, name the C test program and the Page program, which the tests copy beside the library:

```bash
export MITTO_CORE_TEST="$MITTO_WORKSPACE/chromium-<tag>/src/out/mitto/mitto_core_test"
export MITTO_PAGE_PROGRAM="$MITTO_WORKSPACE/chromium-<tag>/src/out/runtime/mitto_page"
uv run pytest tests/test_page_process.py tests/test_page_program.py
```

The tests in `tests/test_native.py` and `tests/test_page.py` that open a Page need the Page program beside the library that `MITTO_NATIVE_LIBRARY` names, as in a wheel, and skip without it.
